Read and change one symbol of the schematic the `sch` frame holds, by uuid, with no KiCad dialog (island `v0.2.3-cc15`, on an engine with `kicadSymbolGet` and `kicadSymbolSet`; a host gates both on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `symbol`, below, or on `island.json` `ops` before any engine boots; an island without them answers `unknown_op`, or `unsupported` on an engine without the exports). They read the document the editor holds: unsaved edits included.

- `uuid` is the symbol's uuid in KiCad's lower case spelling (`8-4-4-4-12` hexadecimal digits), as `ev.selected` carries it. `sheetPath` is a sheet path in `project.open`'s form (`/` for the root, then one lower case uuid and a slash per level, at most 64 levels), as `sheet.tree` reports it. A symbol on a sheet shown more than once (a reused sheet: Glasgow's `io_buffer.kicad_sch` is `IO_Buffer_A` and `IO_Buffer_B`) is one object with an instance per path: its designator, unit and flags are the instance's, its other fields the symbol's.
- `symbol.get`: the symbol on `sheetPath` when that path holds it, else on the sheet the editor shows, else on the first path of the hierarchy holding it (the answer's `sheetPath` says which). `not_found` when no sheet holds it.
- The answer, a symbol sheet, has exactly these keys: `uuid`, `sheetPath`, `sheetName` (the sheet's name as the editor shows it; the root's is the project's name), `ref`, `value`, `footprint` (`lib:name`, `""` for none), `libId` (`lib:name`), `fields` (every field in KiCad's order, the five mandatory first: `{ name, value, shown, kind }`, `name` KiCad's own (`Reference`, `Value`, `Footprint`, `Datasheet`, `Description`, then each user field's), `shown` its visibility on the sheet, `kind` one of `reference`, `value`, `footprint`, `datasheet`, `description`, `user`), `inBom`, `onBoard`, `inSim`, `dnp` (the instance's, for the default variant; `inBom` true means not excluded from the bill of materials, and so on), `rotation` (0, 90, 180 or 270) and `mirror` (`none`, `x` or `y`) as KiCad decomposes the symbol's transform, `x` and `y` (the anchor, millimeters, 4 decimals), `unit` and `units` (1-based; `units` 1 for a single unit part), `bodyStyle` and `bodyStyles` (2 when the symbol has an alternate drawing), and `filters` (the footprint filters of the symbol's own definition, as KiCad's footprint chooser ranks by them; `[]` for none).
- `symbol.set` takes exactly `uuid`, `sheetPath` (the instance the change is for) and `set`, which holds at least one of: `ref` (1 to 64 characters, no control character), `value` (at most 1,024 characters), `footprint` (`lib:name` with both halves, or `""`), `fields` (1 to 64 `{ name, value?, shown? }`, each naming an existing field once, with `value`, `shown` or both; `Reference` and `Value` by their names too, the designator's rule and the footprint's applying to theirs), `addField` (`{ name, value, shown }`, a new user field whose name, 1 to 128 characters with no control character, no field of the symbol has in any casing), `removeField` (a user field's name), `inBom`, `onBoard`, `inSim`, `dnp` (booleans), `rotation` (0, 90, 180, 270), `mirror` (`none`, `x`, `y`), `x` and `y` (millimeters, finite, at most 50,000 in magnitude), `unit` (1 to the symbol's `units`) and `bodyStyle` (1, or 2 when the symbol has two drawings). Lengths count characters (code points); a text holding a NUL or a lone surrogate is refused. One thing is changed once: a field set by its own key and in `fields`, a field both removed and changed, or removed and added again (in any casing) is refused. Anything else, or any other key, answers `bad_args`; so does a set the symbol itself bounds (a field it lacks, a mandatory field removed, a taken name added, a unit past its units, a second drawing it has not): `message` says which.
- The change is ONE edit, as KiCad's own symbol properties make one: one undo step (the reader's Undo takes all of it back), connectivity worked out again, the sheet modified, and an `ev.edited` after the answer. The answer is the symbol read back once the engine's apply queue is empty and the read shows the change. A mirror across Y reads back in KiCad's own spelling, the mirror across X turned 180 degrees more (Glasgow's U1 turned to 90 and mirrored across Y reads `rotation` 270, `mirror` `x`); a rotation or a mirror sent alone keeps the other half's reading. The host's next `project.save`, or the reader's Ctrl+S, writes the change.
- The checks, in order, every one before any effect: the args (`bad_args`); the frame's kind (`unsupported` in a `pcb` frame, "a schematic edits in a sch frame"); the engine (`not_ready` before `ev.ready`; `unsupported` without `kicadSymbolGet`, `kicadSymbolSet` or `kicadCollabBusy`, which `message` names); a schematic opened by `project.open` or `project.import` (`not_ready`); a load parked, a popup menu or a dialog up, or an item placed by `place` hanging off the pointer (`busy`, nothing changed); the symbol (`not_found`, `message` the uuid, when no sheet holds it; for `symbol.set` also when `sheetPath` does not, `message` "<uuid> on <sheetPath>"); the symbol's own bounds (`bad_args`). Then `not_applied` when the engine refuses the set or the read back does not show it within about 5 s.
- It waits in the request queue like any request; while a `project.import` or a `sheet.check` runs, `symbol.set` answers `busy` (a `symbol.get` is still answered).
- Why engine exports (2026-11-06): the island reached a symbol's properties only through KiCad's own properties dialog, which the island never shows. So the engine gained `kicadSymbolGet(uuid, sheetPath)`, a synchronous read answering the sheet as JSON (`""` for no such symbol), and `kicadSymbolSet(argsJson)`, which reads the set whole against the symbol and queues ONE `SCH_COMMIT` pushed as "Edit symbol" on the editor's apply coroutine with the properties dialog's own setters (the other units of a multi-unit part kept in step, the fields placed again after a turn inside the same commit) and answers at once (pcbjam `cc/main` e8b2abe, engine run 10). An island on an engine without them lists no `symbol` and answers `unsupported` naming the export.
- Measured on Glasgow over the local pair with engine run 10 built locally (2026-11-07): in the GPU window (Chromium 157), `symbol.get` of U1 answers 0.62 ms after the request (median of 5, 0.46 to 1.96 ms) and a `symbol.set` of its value 45.6 ms (median of 5, 16.8 to 113.3 ms), each set one undo step (five sets raised the depth by 5), no dialog. `tests/e2e/symbol.spec.ts` (Chromium, then Firefox): `symbol.get` 0.55 and 1.26 ms, `symbol.set` 125 and 84 ms (medians of 5); every key of a set read back, a mirror across Y read as across X at 180 degrees more, Ctrl+Z (`key.press`) taking a set back, and on the reused sheet a designator set on `IO_Buffer_B`'s path leaving `IO_Buffer_A`'s as it was.

### sheet.annotate
Numbers the parts with no KiCad dialog, as KiCad's Annotate Schematic does (island `v0.2.3-cc15`, on an engine with `kicadAnnotate`; a host gates it on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `annotate`, below).

- `scope` is `"all"` (every sheet of the design) or `"sheet"` (the sheet the editor shows); `order` is `"x"` (across, then down) or `"y"` (down, then across); `reset` true numbers every part afresh, false numbers only the parts not numbered yet (an `R?`), each from the first free number of its letter; `keep` lists 0 to 4,096 symbol uuids (KiCad's lower case spelling) whose designators stay as they are and stay taken (a uuid kept twice counts once). A kept uuid keeps its designator on every unit and every instance sharing it. Anything else, or any other key, answers `bad_args`.
- The numbering is KiCad's own (`SCH_EDIT_FRAME::AnnotateSymbols`, or, with `keep`, its reference lists with the kept designators as additional references, which also reach the kept parts of other sheets), power symbols first as KiCad numbers them, into ONE commit pushed as "Number the parts": one undo step when anything moved, none when nothing did, and an `ev.edited` after the answer when anything moved.
- `changes` lists every designator that moved, one per instance: `uuid` the symbol, `sheetPath` the instance's path in `project.open`'s form, `from` (an `R?` for a part not numbered) and `to`. A part of several units changes one row per unit (each unit is a symbol of its own uuid), passed through as the engine writes them, never merged. Power symbols are numbered too but are not parts: they are never listed. `notes` are KiCad's own `warning` and `error` lines of the run, in order, with the frame's paths taken out, at most 200 of at most 2,000 characters (for example "Duplicate items U30E", a unit of a multi-unit part numbered twice, which the engine's own probe of Glasgow on the local pair reported).
- The checks, in order, every one before any effect: as for `symbol.get` (the args, the frame's kind, the engine with `kicadAnnotate` and `kicadCollabBusy`, a schematic, busy). Then the engine's `kicadAnnotate` runs on the editor's apply queue with a fresh folder `/tmp/cc-annotate/<n>/` outside the project folder (whatever an earlier run left under `/tmp/cc-annotate/` is removed first) and writes its record, `.cc-annotate.json`, there last; the frame answers once the apply queue is empty and the record is there, and removes the folder. `not_applied` when the engine refuses the run, or its record has not come within 60 s.
- It waits in the request queue like any request; while a `project.import` or a `sheet.check` runs it answers `busy`.
- Measured on Glasgow and the island's `unnumbered` fixture over the local pair with engine run 10 built locally (2026-11-07): in the GPU window (Chromium 157), Glasgow, every part numbered, answers no change in 532 ms (median of 5, 475 to 568 ms) with the undo depth unchanged, and a `reset` run renumbering it whole answers 234 changes in 597 ms with one undo step; no dialog. `tests/e2e/annotate.spec.ts` (Chromium, then Firefox): the fixture's two `R?` numbered R2 and R3 in 54 and 71 ms with one undo step and one `ev.edited`, a second run moving nothing and making none, a `reset` run keeping R1 and numbering the others R2 and R3, the same run without the keep numbering the row R1, R2, R3 left to right, Ctrl+Z taking it back; Glasgow 610 and 682 ms. Under the headless browsers' software WebGL, three Glasgow runs in a row lose the canvas's WebGL context and the engine stops (`ev.state` `fatal`); in the GPU window six runs in a row (the five above and the reset) did not, so the e2e runs Glasgow once.

### sheet.check
Runs KiCad's electrical rules check over the whole design with no KiCad dialog, as KiCad's Electrical Rules Checker does with its default severities, and answers its report (island `v0.2.3-cc15`, on an engine with `kicadCheckSheet`; a host gates it on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `check`, below). It takes no args.

- `sheets` lists every sheet instance in KiCad's report order: `path` in `project.open`'s form (as `sheet.tree` reports it), `name` KiCad's human path of sheet names (`/`, `/IO_Banks/IO_Buffer_A/`), never one sheet's name alone. KiCad's report spells a sheet's path another way, `uuid_path`: the root sheet's own uuid first and no slash at the end (`/8f38e89c-17d2-45bd-b2a9-83bd9bb08903/00000000-0000-0000-0000-00005c7b59b0`); the frame drops that first uuid and ends the path with a slash (`/00000000-0000-0000-0000-00005c7b59b0/`), so a finding's `path` is the one `sheet.enter` takes. Each sheet's `violations` are KiCad's findings not excluded, of severity `error` or `warning`: `type` is KiCad's rule key (`erc_item.cpp`: `pin_not_connected`, `pin_to_pin`, `label_dangling`, `duplicate_reference`, ...), `items` the items it names (one or two; at most 64 listed), each `{ uuid, description }` with the item's uuid (a pin's own uuid for a pin, the symbol's for a symbol) and KiCad's own English description of it, at most 1,024 characters (KiCad 10 writes `Symbol U1 Pin 5 [XTALIN, Input, Line]`, `Symbol #PWR015 [GND]`, `Label 'CLK'`, `Global Label 'CLK'`, `Hierarchical Label 'CLK'`, `Hierarchical Sheet Pin 'CLK'`, `Horizontal Wire, length 0.2540 mm`, `Junction`, `No Connect`). The positions KiCad writes are left out (its report divides them by the board's scale, so they read 100 times too small). `errors` and `warnings` count every finding of their severity; at most 4,096 findings are listed, and past that the counts still total every finding and `truncated: true` is added.
- KiCad's own marks stay on the drawing where the findings point (the engine draws them on the sheet shown, and each other sheet's when it is shown). Nothing in the document changes: no undo step, no `ev.edited`, and the sheets' modified state as it was.
- The checks, in order, every one before any effect: as for `symbol.get` (args other than none or `{}`, the frame's kind, the engine with `kicadCheckSheet` and `kicadCollabBusy`, a schematic, busy). Then the engine's `kicadCheckSheet` runs on the editor's apply queue with a fresh folder `/tmp/cc-erc/<n>/` (whatever an earlier run left under `/tmp/cc-erc/` is removed first): KiCad's report as `erc.json`, then its record, `.cc-erc.json`, last; the frame answers once the apply queue is empty and the record is there, reads the report and removes the folder. `not_applied` when the engine refuses the run or its record has not come within 60 s; `island_error` when the record says the report was not written (`message` its first error line) or the report is of another shape.
- The check holds the editor for its run (seconds on a design of Glasgow's size, below; on a frame with the library mirror the first check of a boot also loads about 175 library bundles, the ones the design's symbols name): the editor takes no pointer or key meanwhile, and the frame reads no request. It runs off the request queue: until it is answered every request that would change the document, its view or the editor's state answers `busy` at once (everything but `symbol.get`, `sheet.tree`, `layers.get`, `shutdown` and the library reads, which are answered as usual). A request the host sends while the editor is held is read once the run ends: refused `busy` when the check has not been answered yet, else handled after it.
- Why an engine export (2026-11-06): KiCad's checker is a dialog the island never shows. So the engine gained `kicadCheckSheet(argsJson)`, which queues the dialog's preparation (the exclusions recorded, every check mark cleared, the parts not numbered or numbered twice marked) and the ERC job's body (`ERC_TESTER::RunTests`, then `ERC_REPORT::WriteJsonReport` in millimeters) on the apply coroutine without the job's library load wait, which in this engine would wait on every library of KiCad's whole set the island serves (pcbjam `cc/main` e8b2abe, engine run 10).
- Measured on Glasgow and the island's `unnumbered` fixture over the local pair with engine run 10 built locally (2026-11-07): in the GPU window (Chromium 157), Glasgow answers in 13.7 s (median of 5, 13.5 to 14.5 s; KiCad's own checker in this debug build of the engine, the JS side well under 1 s): its four sheets, 9 errors and 662 warnings on the island's example library (the engine's probe on the local pair with KiCad's whole library counted 122 and 410: a design's library findings depend on the libraries the frame serves), the undo depth unchanged, no dialog. `tests/e2e/check.spec.ts` (Chromium, then Firefox): Glasgow in 17.1 and 22.0 s, its sheets in `sheet.tree`'s paths and order, no `ev.edited`, and a `symbol.set` sent 300 ms into the check answered after the check's own answer; the fixture in 73 and 38 ms with 8 errors and 6 warnings, its six resistor pins `pin_not_connected` (`Symbol R? Pin 1 [Passive, Line]`), its two `R?` `unannotated` and `duplicate_reference`.

### board.preview and board.update
KiCad's Update PCB from Schematic with no KiCad dialog (island `v0.2.3-cc19`, on an engine with `kicadUpdateBoardFromSchematic`; a host gates both on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `boardUpdate`, below, or on `island.json` `ops`). The board engine reads the schematic beside the board it holds and brings the board up to it: `board.preview` answers what an update would do and changes nothing; `board.update` does it.

- The args are exactly the three options, each a boolean, KiCad's own (`replaceFootprints`: a footprint whose symbol names another is replaced; `deleteExtra`: a footprint no symbol has is removed; `relinkByReference`: footprints pair with symbols by designator, not by the link KiCad keeps), and for `board.update` `token`, the preview's, 1 to 128 characters. Anything else, or any other key, answers `bad_args`.
- The schematic is `<the opened board's folder>/<the board's stem>.kicad_sch` in the project folder (the board engine holds the sheets the host staged or wrote with `project.write`); `no_schematic` names that path when it is not there.
- `sheets` are the top-level sheet files the engine read, project paths in `project.open`'s form, in KiCad's order. `changes` are KiCad's changes as records, each with exactly its kind's keys: `add` (`ref`, `footprint`, `pins` (each `{ pin, net, pad }`, `pad` false when the footprint has no pad of that number), `extraPads`, `sameName`: a footprint of that designator is on the board already), `remove` (`ref`, `footprint`), `footprint` (`ref`, `from`, `to`), `reference` (`ref`, `to`), `value` (`ref`, `from`, `to`), `detail` (`ref`, `topic` one of `fields`, `link`, `sheet`, `filters`, `attribute`, `jumpers`, `group`, `class`, `units`, `variant`, `name`, `from`, `to`), `connect` (`ref`, `pin`, `to`), `reconnect` (`ref`, `pin`, `from`, `to`), `disconnect` (`ref`, `pin`, `from`), `net` and `unusedNet` (`net`), `copper` (`item` `via` or `zone`, `name`, `from`, `to`) and `problem` (`code` one of `no_footprint`, `footprint_not_found`, `footprint_without_library`, `locked`, `pad_without_pin`, `pin_without_pad`, `pins_unnumbered`, `via_unknown_net`, `zone_without_pads`, `variant_footprint`, `unannotated`, `duplicate_designator`, `duplicate_sheet_names`; `severity` `warning` or `error`; `ref`, `pin`, `footprint`, `net`, `name`), each with `message`, KiCad's own sentence for logs, never for display. A preview fills `pins`, `extraPads` and `sameName`; an update answers its new parts' connections as `connect` records and their designators in `added`. At most 4,096 records, past them `truncated: true` and the counts still total; `unmapped` counts KiCad's lines that no record carries (0 when every line is mapped). Strings are at most 1,024 characters.
- A preview of parts not numbered yet answers its list with `blocked: "not_annotated"` and the `unannotated` or `duplicate_designator` problems; `board.update` answers `not_annotated` then, and `stale` when the board or the schematic changed since the preview that gave the token. Both change nothing. `schematic_unreadable` when the engine could not read the schematic (`message` says why), or when a dialog came up while the run ran: it is closed without accepting anything it asks (its Cancel, No or Close, else a lone OK, else its close box) and `message` is its title.
- After an applied update the new parts sit beside the board's outline, selected, the whole update one undo step: an `ev.edited` and an `ev.selected` follow the answer. The engine never saves: the host's next `project.save`, or the reader's Ctrl+S, writes the board.
- The checks, in order, every one before any effect: the args (`bad_args`); the frame's kind (`unsupported` in a `sch` frame, "a board op runs in a pcb frame"); the engine (`not_ready` before `ev.ready`; `unsupported` without `kicadUpdateBoardFromSchematic` or `kicadCollabBusy`, which `message` names); a board opened by `project.open` or `project.import` (`not_ready`); a load parked, a popup menu or a dialog up, or an item placed by `place` hanging off the pointer (`busy`, nothing changed); the schematic (`no_schematic`). Then the engine's export runs the job on the editor's apply queue in a fresh folder `/tmp/cc-update/<n>/` (whatever an earlier run left under `/tmp/cc-update/` is removed first), writing its report and then its record, `.cc-update.json`, last; the frame answers once the apply queue is empty and the record is there, and removes the folder. `not_applied` when the engine refuses the run or its record has not come within 30 s.
- It waits in the request queue like any request; while a `project.import` or a `board.check` runs it answers `busy`.
- The run opens KiCad's schematic editor hidden, which takes wx's keyboard focus: after the answer the frame gives the drawing its focus back with a middle press near the canvas's corner (a middle press selects nothing), so the reader's keys and `key.press` reach KiCad again. After an applied update the frame reads the selection, since the engine selects the new parts with no selection emit: the `ev.selected` naming them follows the answer.
- Why an engine export (2026-11-10): KiCad's Update PCB from Schematic is a dialog the island never shows. So the engine gained `kicadUpdateBoardFromSchematic(argsJson)` (pcbjam `cc/main` 70fe8e2, bbdf4c6, e34d7a4 and 3dd83b2, with kicad `cc/main` 88eece91's change log of `BOARD_NETLIST_UPDATER`; engine run 12). An island on an engine without it lists no `boardUpdate` and answers `unsupported` naming the export.
- Measured over the local pair with engine run 12 built locally (2026-11-10, `tests/e2e/board-update.spec.ts`, with the library mirror served): first-board's preview 400 and 415 ms the first of a boot, then 11 to 36 ms (Chromium, then Firefox), the update 57 to 103 ms, its two parts beside the outline and selected, one undo step that Ctrl+Z takes back whole and Ctrl+Y brings again, the saved board holding both with their pads' nets; Glasgow's preview 871 and 799 ms first, then 189 to 249 ms, with 1,817 records and every line mapped. Without the library mirror each new part answers a `footprint_not_found` problem. An applied update reports KiCad's own lines for a new part beside its `add`: its designator from `REF**`, its value, its link to the symbol, its sheet and its footprint filters.
- **Keep groups and Apply block layouts** (island `v0.2.3-cc25`, unit i-block-layout, on an engine with the marker `kicadBoardUpdateGroups`, engine run 15; a host gates both on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `blockLayouts`): both ops also take KiCad's two boxes of Update PCB from Schematic, `transferGroups` (Keep groups: the schematic's groups become groups of the board, each keeping its design block link) and `applyBlockLayouts` (Apply block layouts: each such group takes its block's layout), booleans, `applyBlockLayouts` only beside `transferGroups` (alone, or `true` beside `transferGroups` false, answers `bad_args`). With `transferGroups` true the answer adds `layouts: { applied: number, failures: [{ uuid: string, reason: string }] }`: a preview always `{ applied: 0, failures: [] }`, an update how many groups took their layout and each that did not (the group's uuid on the board, KiCad's reason, at most 1,024 characters, at most 4,096 failures listed). An update with layouts is KiCad's Update step plus one step per laid out group. An update whose two boxes differ from its preview's answers `stale`. With `transferGroups` false, or neither box sent, the run and its answer are today's, on any engine. A group's block row must be loaded (`project.libs`) for its layout to apply. On an engine without the marker (engine run 14) a request with `transferGroups` true answers `unsupported` "kicadBoardUpdateGroups" after its args are checked. <!-- ext:i-block-layout -->

### board.check
KiCad's design rules check of the board with no KiCad dialog, as KiCad's Design Rules Checker does with the board's own severities, its findings answered as records (island `v0.2.3-cc19`, on an engine with `kicadCheckBoard`; a host gates it on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `drc`, below). The board engine checks the board it holds, unsaved edits included.

- The args are exactly `fill` (pour every copper area again first, as KiCad's dialog does by default), `parity` (compare the board with the schematic beside it) and `allTrackErrors` (list every problem along a track, not only its first), each a boolean. Anything else, or any other key, answers `bad_args`.
- The answer has exactly these keys: `violations`, `unconnected` (findings of type `unconnected_items`) and `parity` (the comparison's findings, KiCad's parity keys: `missing_footprint`, `extra_footprint`, `duplicate_footprints`, `net_conflict`, `footprint_symbol_mismatch`, `footprint_filters_mismatch`, `footprint_symbol_field_mismatch`), each a list of findings; `parityRun`, `yes` when the board was compared, `off` when not asked, `no_schematic` when no schematic sits beside it, `not_annotated` when its parts are not all numbered, `unreadable` when the schematic could not be read (the run goes on without the comparison); `filled`, whether the fill ran (engine run 12 pushes its undo step every time, even with nothing to pour: one undo step and an `ev.edited` after the answer, at the answer's `depth`); `skipped`, rule keys the run left out (at most 8); `ignored`, the rule keys the board's settings turn off (at most 128); `errors` and `warnings`, the findings NOT excluded of each severity, and `excludedCount`, the excluded ones, over all three lists, counted by the frame; `depth`, the undo depth after the run; and `truncated: true` when the lists were cut.
- A finding has exactly `marker` (the uuid of the mark KiCad left on the board; `board.explain` takes it), `type` (KiCad's rule key, `drc_item.cpp`: `clearance`, `unconnected_items`, `extra_footprint`, ...), `severity` (`error` or `warning`), `excluded` and `comment` (an exclusion the board's project file keeps, and its comment; excluded findings are listed), `layer` (a canonical layer name or `""`), `x` and `y` (the mark's position, millimeters), `items` (at most 64 listed), `rule` (`{ source, name }` when the test recorded the rule that raised it: `source` one of `board`, `netclass`, `custom`, `keepout`, `local`, `netTie`, `tuning`, `other`; else null), `actual` (millimeters, read from KiCad's message where it carries one, else null) and `message` (KiCad's sentence, for logs). An item has exactly `uuid`, `kind` (`pad`, `track`, `arc`, `via`, `zone`, `footprint`, `field`, `text`, `shape`, `outline`, `hole` or `other`), `ref` and `pad` (a pad's designator and number; `ref` also a footprint's, a field's and a footprint shape's), `net` (KiCad's net name), `layer`, `parent` (the footprint holding a pad, field or text, else `""`) and `description` (KiCad's English, for logs). At most 4,096 findings are listed, over the three lists in that order; the counts total all of them. KiCad's own words are cut at 1,024 characters.
- KiCad's marks stay on the board until the next check, and every check makes them anew: a mark's uuid holds until the next check, so `board.explain` takes the last check's marks. No undo step but the fill's; the marks change nothing the host saves. A mark cannot be selected by `select` (the engine's select by uuid takes no mark); the reader's double-click on one, or `E` with one selected, sends `ev.boardProperties` of kind `marker`. A pad's item has no `layer` (`""`): it lies on several.
- A check that compares the board with the schematic opens KiCad's schematic editor hidden, which takes wx's keyboard focus: after the answer the frame gives the drawing its focus back with a middle press near the canvas's corner, which selects nothing.
- Why an engine export (2026-11-10): KiCad's Design Rules Checker is a dialog the island never shows. So the engine gained `kicadCheckBoard(argsJson)`, KiCad's DRC job's body on the apply coroutine with the board's own rules compiled first (a rules file that does not compile stops the run), and `kicadExplainViolation(marker)`, `BOARD_INSPECTION_TOOL::InspectDRCError`'s evaluation answered as data (pcbjam `cc/main` 70fe8e2; engine run 12).
- Measured over the local pair with engine run 12 built locally (2026-11-10, `tests/e2e/board-check.spec.ts`): the drc-small fixture checked in 556 to 708 ms the first of a boot and 285 to 406 ms after, the fill, the comparison and every track error each changing it by less than 50 ms; its four findings exactly (the clearance against its custom rule `Tight`, 0.15 mm against 0.25 mm, the connection not made, R2 with no symbol, the excluded stub with its comment); ten checks in a row left the engine's memory at 369 MB; Glasgow checked once in 3.0 s (Chromium) and 5.0 s (Firefox) with the comparison, 429 violations and 421 differing (326 on run 12 rebuilt, island `v0.2.3-cc19`, 2026-11-10: 2.95 s in Chromium, 5.0 s in Firefox; the first build joined schematic connection points at (x, y) and (y, x) and listed 95 false net conflicts); a change sent during a check answered `busy`, a read answered. `board.explain` of the clearance mark answers in 9 to 12 ms (medians of 5, 10 and 11 ms).
- The checks, in order, every one before any effect: as for `board.preview` without its schematic (the args, the frame's kind, the engine with `kicadCheckBoard` and `kicadCollabBusy`, a board, busy). Then the engine's export runs in a fresh folder `/tmp/cc-drc/<n>/` (an earlier run's removed first), writing its report and then its record, `.cc-drc.json`, last; the frame answers once the apply queue is empty and the record is there, and removes the folder. `rules_invalid` when the board's custom rules file does not compile (`message` KiCad's parse message), with no check run; `island_error` when the report is of another shape, or when a dialog came up while the run ran (closed without accepting anything, as for `board.preview`; `message` is its title); `not_applied` when the engine refuses the run or its record has not come within 60 s.
- The check holds the editor for its run: it runs off the request queue, and until it is answered every request that would change the document, its view or the editor's state answers `busy` at once (everything but `board.item.get`, `board.explain`, `layers.get`, `shutdown` and the library reads, which are answered as usual, once the editor is free).

### board.explain
The rule behind one finding of the last `board.check`, as KiCad's own "Show DRC rule" works it out, read with no KiCad window (island `v0.2.3-cc19`, on an engine with `kicadExplainViolation`; a host gates it on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `explain`). The args are exactly `{ marker }`, a mark's uuid in KiCad's lower case spelling.

- The answer has exactly `constraint` (`clearance`, `hole_clearance`, `hole_to_hole`, `edge_clearance`, `track_width`, `track_angle`, `track_segment_length`, `connection_width`, `via_diameter`, `annular_width`, `hole_size`, `text_height`, `text_thickness`, `max_uncoupled` or `assertion`), `unit` (`deg` for `track_angle`, `mm` for every other constraint), `min`, `opt` and `max` (numbers or null), `source` and `name` (as a finding's `rule`; `name` is `""` for the board's own settings), `condition` (a custom rule's condition as its file writes it, else `""`; at most 2,048 characters), `layer` and `items` (0 to 2, each `{ uuid, netclass }`, the net class each item is in).
- KiCad explains sixteen rule keys: `clearance`, `hole_clearance`, `hole_to_hole`, `copper_edge_clearance`, `track_width`, `track_angle`, `track_segment_length`, `connection_width`, `via_diameter`, `annular_width`, `drill_out_of_range`, `microvia_drill_out_of_range`, `text_height`, `text_thickness`, `diff_pair_uncoupled_length_too_long`, `assertion_failure`. A mark of any other key answers `no_rule`; a board whose custom rules do not compile answers `rules_invalid`; no mark of the board with that uuid answers `not_found` (`message` the uuid).
- The checks, in order, as for `board.check` (the engine with `kicadExplainViolation`). It is a read: while a `board.check` runs it is answered once the editor is free.

### board.item.get and board.item.set
Read and change one board item's properties by uuid with no KiCad dialog (island `v0.2.3-cc19`, on an engine with `kicadBoardItemGet` and `kicadBoardItemSet`; a host gates both on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `boardItem`). They read the board the editor holds, unsaved edits included.

- `board.item.get` takes 1 to 512 uuids in KiCad's lower case spelling and answers the items the board holds, in the order asked (a uuid it does not hold is left out, so an empty list says the items are gone; a uuid asked twice is asked once, and an answer never names a uuid twice). A field or a text of a footprint answers its footprint, once. Each item has exactly its kind's keys: a footprint `{ kind: "footprint", uuid, locked, ref, value, libId, side ("front" or "back"), x, y, rotation (degrees, over -180 to 180), inBom, inPos, dnp, boardOnly, variant, symbolPath }`; a track or an arc `{ kind, uuid, locked, net, layer, width, length }`; a via `{ kind: "via", uuid, locked, net, x, y, diameter, drill, viaType ("through", "blind", "buried" or "micro"), top, bottom }`; a copper area `{ kind: "zone", uuid, locked, name, net, layers, priority, clearance, minWidth, padConnection ("thermal", "solid", "none" or "thtThermal"), thermalGap, spokeWidth, netChoices (every net of the board) }`; a board text `{ kind: "text", uuid, locked, text, layer, x, y, rotation, height, width, thickness, bold, italic, knockout, mirrored, hAlign ("left", "center" or "right") }`; anything else `{ kind: "other", uuid, type }`, `type` one of `pad`, `shape`, `dimension`, `textbox`, `table`, `group`, `image`, `barcode`, `rulearea`, `marker`, `other`. Lengths and positions are millimeters; layers are KiCad's canonical names (`F.Cu`, `In1.Cu`, `User.1`: letters, digits, dots and underscores, 1 to 32 characters), never a layer's name as the board renamed it, as `layers.get`'s `canonical`; a `net` of `""` is no net; a text is 1 to 4,096 characters and may hold line breaks.
- `board.item.set` takes exactly `uuid` and `set`, which holds exactly ONE key: `x`, `y` (at most 50,000 in magnitude), `rotation` (-360 to 360), `locked`, `side`, `inBom`, `inPos`, `dnp`, `ref` (1 to 64 characters, no control character), `value` or `name` (at most 1,024, no control character), `width`, `diameter`, `drill`, `clearance`, `minWidth`, `thermalGap`, `spokeWidth`, `height`, `thickness` (0.001 to 1,000), `layer`, `net`, `priority` (a whole number, 0 to 1,000,000), `padConnection`, `text` (1 to 4,096, line breaks and tabs allowed), `bold`, `italic`, `knockout`, `hAlign`. Each kind takes its own keys: a footprint `x`, `y`, `rotation`, `locked`, `side`, `inBom`, `inPos`, `dnp`, and `ref` and `value` only when it is board only (else it takes them from the schematic); a track or an arc `width`, `layer` (a copper layer) and `locked`; a via `x`, `y`, `locked`, `diameter` and `drill` (the hole smaller than the via); a copper area `name`, `net` (one of its `netChoices`, or `""`), `priority`, `clearance`, `minWidth`, `padConnection`, `thermalGap`, `spokeWidth` and `locked`; a text `x`, `y`, `rotation`, `locked`, `layer`, `text`, `height`, `width`, `thickness`, `bold`, `italic`, `knockout`, `hAlign`. A set of another kind's key, or one the item refuses, answers `bad_args` (`message` says why), and so does a set the engine refuses (a layer the board does not use, a bound KiCad keeps).
- The change is ONE edit, as KiCad's own properties panel makes one: one undo step, the board modified, and an `ev.edited` after the answer. The answer is `{ item }`, the item read back once the engine's apply queue is empty and the read shows the change (within KiCad's rounding: lengths to 4 decimals, angles to 3, a rotation by its angle). A copper area's change does not pour it again (KiCad's default).
- The checks, in order, every one before any effect: as for `board.explain` (the engine with `kicadBoardItemGet`, `kicadBoardItemSet` and `kicadCollabBusy`); for a set, then the item (`not_found`, `message` the uuid, when the board does not hold it) and its own refusals (`bad_args`). `not_applied` when the read back does not show the change within about 5 s. While a `board.check` runs, `board.item.set` answers `busy` and `board.item.get` is answered once the editor is free.
- Why engine exports (2026-11-10): the island reached a board item's properties only through KiCad's own dialogs, which it never shows. So the engine gained `kicadBoardItemGet(uuidsJson)` and `kicadBoardItemSet(argsJson)`, each set ONE `BOARD_COMMIT` through the item's own setters (pcbjam `cc/main` 70fe8e2; engine run 12).
- Measured over the local pair with engine run 12 built locally (2026-11-10, `tests/e2e/board-item.spec.ts`): `board.item.get` of one footprint answers in 0.3 to 1.1 ms (medians of 5, Chromium then Firefox, on drc-small and Glasgow); a set reads back in 11 to 22 ms on drc-small (medians per kind) and 40 to 42 ms on Glasgow's U1; every key of every kind read back as sent, each one undo step with one `ev.edited`, Ctrl+Z (`key.press`) taking the last one back.

### project.export
Makes one output of the design, any file desktop KiCad makes from it through its Plot, Fabrication Outputs and Export menus or a `.kicad_jobset`, with no KiCad dialog (island `v0.2.3-cc23`, on an engine with `kicadExportBoardOutput` in a `pcb` frame or `kicadExportSchematicOutput` in a `sch` frame, engine run 14; a host gates it on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `export`, below, and each output on `island.json` `exportKinds` listing its job, or before any engine boots on `ops` listing `project.export`; an island without it answers `unknown_op`, or `unsupported` on an engine without the export). It reads the document the frame holds, as the editor holds it: unsaved edits included, the copper pours as the board last filled them (no refill: a refill is an edit). Nothing in the document changes: no undo step and no `ev.edited`.

- The args are exactly `job` and `settings`, and `pages` for a schematic plot only. `job` is one of the jobs `island.json` lists as `exportKinds` (`bridge/exports.json`): KiCad's own job names, each run in its frame's kind, `pcb_export_gerbers`, `pcb_export_drill`, `pcb_export_pos`, `pcb_export_ipc2581`, `pcb_export_odb`, `pcb_export_ipcd356`, `pcb_export_pdf`, `pcb_export_svg`, `pcb_export_dxf`, `pcb_export_ps`, `pcb_export_gencad`, `pcb_export_stats` and `pcb_export_3d` in a `pcb` frame, `sch_export_plot_pdf`, `sch_export_plot_svg`, `sch_export_plot_dxf`, `sch_export_plot_ps`, `sch_export_netlist` and `sch_export_bom` in a `sch` frame, and ours, the exporters beneath KiCad's own dialogs where KiCad has no job: `cc_footprint_report`, `cc_idf`, `cc_specctra_dsn`, `cc_hyperlynx`, `cc_footprint_associations` and `cc_footprints` in a `pcb` frame, `cc_symbols` in a `sch` frame. `settings` is KiCad's own settings JSON for the job, the `settings` of a job in a `.kicad_jobset` (`kicad-cli jobset run` of the same settings is the same job): a plain object of at most 128 keys, each value a boolean, a finite number, a string of at most 1,024 characters or a list of at most 256 such strings; a key the job leaves out stays at the job's own default, which is `kicad-cli`'s. Ours take only their own keys: `cc_footprint_report` `{ units: "mm" | "in" }`, `cc_idf` `{ units: "mm" | "thou", include_unspecified: boolean, include_dnp: boolean }`, the others none. `pages` (`sch_export_plot_*` only) is 0 to 1,024 page numbers as the sheets print them, each 1 to 32 bytes of UTF-8; none or an empty list plots every sheet. Anything else, or any other key, answers `bad_args`.
- The engine reads the settings as KiCad's jobset runner does, then fixes what our page never varies: a sent key the job does not have (a jobset written by another KiCad: `hpgl_page_size` in a schematic plot) is left out and named in the answer's `dropped`; a known key whose value is of another JSON type, or a key one of ours does not take, makes the engine refuse the whole call (`bad_args`, "the engine refused the settings"), so KiCad's own reader never throws on it. Whatever is sent: the output path and name are the run's (`output_filename`, `output_dir` and `description` are ignored), the color theme is KiCad's default (the editor's own is the viewer's night palette), no zone refill (`check_zones` off), the project's own drawing sheet, no variant, `use_global_settings` off; for `pcb_export_3d` VRML only (any other format fails with "Only VRML is made here.", OpenCASCADE is not in the engine), `overwrite` on, no model folder, absolute paths, no model substitution; for `pcb_export_odb` a zip; for `pcb_export_drill` Excellon in millimeters, decimal, the absolute origin, plated and unplated holes apart and the map on, with the report named by KiCad (pcbjam `cc/main` `wasm/bindings/cc_output_common.h` `MergeSettings`). A layer list names layers by the board file's own names (`F.SilkS`, `B.SilkS`, `F.Cu`, `Edge.Cuts`), as KiCad's jobsets write them: the shown name (`F.Silkscreen`) is not one of them and the job leaves it out; the files are named by the shown names (`glasgow-F_Silkscreen.pdf`).
- `files` are every file the run wrote, sorted by path: `path` the file's name as `kicad-cli jobset run` names it (`glasgow-all-pos.csv`, `glasgow.d356`, `glasgow-odb.zip`, `glasgow_statistics.json`), or one folder and a name where the output is a folder (Allegro's netlist adds `devices/<device>.txt`; `cc_footprints` writes `<board>.pretty/<footprint>.kicad_mod`); never deeper. `bytes` is a fresh `Uint8Array` per file, transferred. `notes` are the run's own `warning` and `error` lines, in order, with the frame's paths taken out (`cc_footprints` names the footprint names it kept once). `dropped` are the setting keys the job does not have.
- The checks, in order, every one before any effect: the args (`bad_args`); the frame's kind against the job's (`unsupported`, "a board output runs in a pcb frame" or "a schematic output runs in a sch frame"; a `symedit` frame answers it `unsupported` as every document op); the engine (`not_ready` before `ev.ready`; `unsupported` without the frame's export or `kicadCollabBusy`, which `message` names); a document opened by `project.open` or `project.import` (`not_ready`); a load parked, a popup menu or a dialog up, or an item placed by `place` hanging off the pointer (`busy`, nothing changed).
- Then the engine's export queues the job on the editor's apply queue, into a fresh folder `/tmp/cc-out/<n>/` outside the project folder, so `project.save`, `project.forget` and `project.write` never see it (whatever an earlier run left under `/tmp/cc-out/` is removed first). The engine writes its record of the run, `.cc-export.json` (`{ code, lines, dropped }`), last; the frame answers once the apply queue is empty and the record is there, reads every other file, and removes the folder. A dialog of KiCad's met meanwhile (a progress dialog aside) is closed without accepting anything it asks and the answer is `island_error`, `message` its title, as for `board.check`. A record that has not come within 45 s answers `not_applied` (the folder removed): five times the slowest Glasgow output measured (ODB++, 8.4 s in Firefox).
- `export_failed` (below) when the run ended with any code but success: `message` is its first `error` line, else for the engine's own refusal (code -1) its last line ("Only VRML is made here.", "The VRML exporter wrote no board (an empty scene)."), else "the export ended with code N", the frame's paths taken out. Nothing is answered of a failed run's files. More than 1,024 files, or more than 512 MiB together, answers `island_error` ("the export wrote more than the protocol carries"); so do a folder inside a folder and a link. None of Glasgow's outputs comes near (the schematic as DXF is the largest: 30.5 MB in four files).
- It waits in the request queue like any request; while a `project.import` runs it answers `busy`, and while a `sheet.check` or a `board.check` runs it answers `busy` at once.
- Why engine exports (2026-11-16): `project.plot` made the Gerbers and drill files only, and every other file desktop KiCad makes was out of reach (its dialogs are never shown). Every KiCad output that is a job runs through KiCad's own job classes and `KIWAY::ProcessJob`, as `kicad-cli` and KiCad's jobsets do: one general export a face, a job name and its settings, never one export per format, so a desktop `.kicad_jobset` runs as written. The outputs that are no job in KiCad (the footprint report, IDF, Specctra DSN, Hyperlynx, the footprint associations, the board's footprints, the design's symbols) are small runners in the engine around the exporter beneath KiCad's dialog (kicad `cc/main` K-O1 to K-O4: IDF and Allegro report their errors in place of message boxes, Hyperlynx's exporter reachable, the VRML exporter compiled). So the engine gained `kicadExportBoardOutput(argsJson)`, `kicadExportSchematicOutput(argsJson)` and `kicadOutputJobs()`, the list of the jobs it runs with each one's own settings and defaults (pcbjam `cc/main` 598e368, 1acc0b1, 94b8da2; engine run 14). The e2e checks that list against `bridge/exports.json`.
- What engine run 14 does not make, said by the answer, never a file offered: VRML (`pcb_export_3d`) writes only the 16 byte VRML header on Glasgow and the first board, so the engine answers it `export_failed` ("The VRML exporter wrote no board (an empty scene)."), its cause not found yet (pcbjam 44e3e9f); the drill report (`pcb_export_drill` with `generate_report`) ends with code 4 and no file: KiCad's job names the report inside the run's folder and then resolves that path against the folder again, and the engine forces `report_filename` to its empty default, so no setting avoids it (the drill files and maps alone are made); Specctra DSN on Glasgow is refused by KiCad's own exporter, "IO_ERROR: Multiple footprints have the reference designator 'REF**'." (`kicad-cli` 10.0.4's own export refuses it the same way).
- Measured on Glasgow over the local pair with engine run 14 built locally (2026-11-17, `tests/e2e/export.spec.ts`, Chromium then Firefox, from the request to the answer): the drill files with their map 16 to 87 ms (PDF, Gerber, SVG, DXF and PostScript maps; the PDF maps 44,317 and 13,000 bytes, the drill files 8,709 and 444), IPC-D-356 11 to 17 ms (119,425 bytes), IPC-2581 2.2 s in Chromium and 3.4 s in Firefox (10,359,226 bytes; compressed 741,926), ODB++ 6.0 to 6.1 s and 8.4 s (617,288 bytes), GenCAD 49 to 54 ms, the placement file 11 to 34 ms (`glasgow-all-pos.csv` 16,944 bytes; `glasgow-top.pos` and `glasgow-bottom.pos`; `glasgow-pnp_top.gbr` and `glasgow-pnp_bottom.gbr`), the footprint report 13 to 22 ms (236,073 bytes), the board as PDF 295 to 963 ms (`glasgow.pdf` with a page per layer, one page, or a file per layer: F.Cu 341,165 bytes, Edge.Cuts 153,754), as SVG 56 to 124 ms, as DXF 460 to 531 ms (F.Cu 7,569,139 bytes in millimeters), as PostScript 28 to 44 ms (F.Cu 707,598), IDF 39 to 41 ms (`glasgow.emn`, `glasgow.emp`), Hyperlynx 311 to 318 ms (2,047,050 bytes), the footprint associations 11 to 21 ms, the statistics 1.0 to 1.7 s (`glasgow_statistics.rpt` 2,385 bytes, `glasgow_statistics.json` 4,115), the board's footprints 389 to 509 ms (41 files), a jobset's Gerbers 44 to 62 ms; in a `sch` frame the BOM 35 to 65 ms (one row per part 14,081 bytes), the schematic as PDF 900 ms every sheet (887,858 bytes) and 284 ms the first sheet (`pages: ["1"]`), as SVG 284 ms, DXF 0.95 to 1.06 s (four files, 30,489,764 bytes), PostScript 251 to 257 ms, the netlists 22 to 98 ms (KiCad 545,245 bytes, KiCad XML, OrcadPCB2, CadStar `.frp`, Allegro with its 70 `devices/` files, PADS `.asc`, SPICE 5,032 bytes, SPICE model), the symbols 114 ms (`glasgow.kicad_sym` 203,247 bytes). After every board output, and after every schematic output, the undo depth was as before, no `ev.edited` came within 2 s, no `ev.menu` was sent and `/tmp/cc-out/` was empty; `busy` while a placed footprint hangs off the pointer, the output made once it is dropped. In the GPU window (Chromium 157, medians of 5): IPC-D-356 13 ms, the placement file 12 ms, IPC-2581 2,157 ms, ODB++ 3,000 ms, the board as PDF with a page per layer 724 ms, the statistics 1,051 ms, the schematic as PDF 822 ms and as DXF 891 ms, the KiCad netlist 58 ms; forty IPC-2581 runs on a fresh boot left the engine's memory at 443 MiB throughout, while ten after the other outputs above grew it from 443 to 531 MiB (an engine's memory never shrinks). The sizes are the bytes our engine made; whether each equals `kicad-cli` 10.0.4's own file but for its stamp lines is the site's reference test (outputs spec section 10), not measured here.
- The 3D outputs (island `v0.2.3-cc25`, engine run 15): the job `pcb_render` (KiCad's raytraced picture of the board, `format` `png` or `jpeg`; `jpg` is refused), listed in `island.json` `exportKinds`; a host gates it on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `render` (the engine's `kicadOutputJobs()` lists `pcb_render`). The picture has KiCad's own size, not the asked one: KiCad's raytracer draws in whole blocks, so an asked 1600 by 1200 gives 1568 by 1176 and 800 by 600 gives 784 by 592, as `kicad-cli` 10.0 writes. The engine names the size in its line "The picture is <w> by <h> pixels.", an info line the answer does not carry; it equals the file's own header (a PNG's IHDR, a JPEG's frame header), which is where a host reads it. <!-- ext:3d-i3 -->
- Before a `pcb_export_3d` or `pcb_render` run the island stages the bodies the board's shown model lines name, read from the LIVE board (the engine's `kicadBoardModels`: a part or a body given since the last save included, nothing saved, the board's modified state unchanged; the saved board's lines only on an engine that cannot read them then), a model file the page added with `project.write` found in the project folder before any save: a `${KICAD*_3DMODEL_DIR}` line through the model store (its body written under `/pcbjam/3dmodels/<Lib>.3dshapes/`), a `${KIPRJMOD}`, `$(KIPRJMOD)` or bare relative line copied from the project folder to `/pcbjam/3dmodels/kiprjmod/<relative path>`; a `kicad-embed://` line needs nothing; an absolute path, another variable or a path leaving the project resolves to nothing. The formats the 3D service makes are handed the same bodies, each by its path relative to `/pcbjam/3dmodels` (`kiprjmod/<relative path>`, `<Lib>.3dshapes/<Name>.<ext>`), the only paths the service stages. At most 2,000 files and 512 MiB; the lines past them, and the bodies not found, go into the file without a body. KiCad's own warning for such a body ("Could not add 3D model for R1. File not found: ...") reaches `notes` from a `pcb_render` (a body the render cannot load is a warning line); from a format the 3D service makes it is not carried on engine run 15 (the service answers its report as one text, which the engine reports as an info line, and `notes` holds the warning and error lines only). <!-- ext:3d-i3 -->
- Without the cap `occ`, every `pcb_export_3d` runs with `cc_occ: false` in its settings (engine run 15 takes it and never names it in `dropped`): only VRML is made then (a full `.wrl` on engine run 15: about 18 MB on Glasgow, as `kicad-cli` 10.0.4 writes), every other format answers `export_failed` with the line "Only VRML is made here." <!-- ext:3d-i3 -->
- Each job's time bound comes from `bridge/exportTiming.json` (`island.json` `timing.exportMs`): `pcb_export_3d` and `pcb_render` five times their slowest measured run, at least 60 s and at most 900 s; every other job 45 s. <!-- ext:3d-i3 -->
- Held in a `pcb` frame, each sending `ev.held` and opening nothing: KiCad's File > Export > STEP (`pcbnew.EditorControl.exportSTEP`) and VRML (`pcbnew.EditorControl.exportVRML`) under the cap `export` (the host's Outputs page opens on 3D model for CAD and on VRML), and KiCad's 3D viewer's Export Image (`3DViewer.Control.exportImage`) and Copy 3D image (`3DViewer.Control.copyToClipboard`) under `heldKeys`, with no door of the host's. <!-- ext:3d-i3 -->

### board.rules
The rules KiCad applies to one board item, or between two, as KiCad's own Inspect > Constraints Resolution and Clearance Resolution work them out, read with no KiCad window (island `v0.2.3-cc23`, on an engine with `kicadResolveRules`, engine run 14; a host gates it on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `rules`). The args are exactly `{ a }` for one item or `{ a, b }` for two, uuids in KiCad's lower case spelling, `b` not `a`. It reads the board the editor holds.

- The answer is `{ rules, notes }`. `rules` are 0 to 128 records in KiCad's report order, each with exactly `constraint`, `unit`, `min`, `opt`, `max`, `verdict`, `source`, `name`, `condition`, `layer` and `items`: `constraint` is one of `board.explain`'s fifteen or `physical_clearance`, `physical_hole_clearance`, `silk_clearance`, `courtyard_clearance`, `diff_pair_gap`, `zone_connection`, `thermal_relief_gap`, `thermal_spoke_width`, `min_resolved_spokes`, `solder_mask_expansion`, `solder_paste_abs_margin`, `solder_paste_rel_margin` or `disallow`; `unit` is `deg` for `track_angle`, `percent` for `solder_paste_rel_margin`, `count` for `min_resolved_spokes`, `none` for `zone_connection`, `disallow` and `assertion` (their `min`, `opt` and `max` null), `mm` for every other; `min`, `opt` and `max` numbers or null; `verdict` is `""`, or for `zone_connection` `solid`, `thermal`, `thermal_tht` or `none`, for `disallow` `allowed` or `disallowed`, for `assertion` `pass` or `fail`; `source` and `name` as `board.explain`'s, plus `sameNet` (two items on one net: `clearance` with `min` 0, KiCad's "Min clearance is 0."); `condition` a custom rule's condition (at most 2,048 characters); `layer` the board file's layer name or `""`; `items` 1 or 2 `{ uuid, netclass }` (KiCad reports a group by its first item and a footprint whose pads are one logical pad by that pad, so an item's uuid may differ from the one asked). `notes` are KiCad's report lines that are no rule (at most 32: "Report may be incomplete: some footprint courtyards are malformed.", "No 'diff_pair_uncoupled' constraints defined.", a tented item).
- One item: the track's width, the via's diameter and annular width, the hole size of a pad or via with a hole, the solder mask expansion of a pad, shape or track, both paste margins of a pad, the text height and thickness of text, and every item's keep out verdict and assertions. Two items: the clearance on every copper layer both share (the active layer first), the zone connection of a pad and a zone of one net with its thermal gap, spoke width and minimum spokes, the differential pair gap, silkscreen and courtyard clearance, hole clearance and hole to hole, edge clearance, and physical clearance and physical hole clearance where the board has such rules.
- The checks, in order, as for `board.explain` (the engine with `kicadResolveRules`; a `sch` frame answers `unsupported`). `not_found` when the board holds neither uuid, or not both (`message` the uuids asked); `rules_invalid` when the board's custom rules file does not compile; `no_rule` when KiCad applies no rule (`message` the uuids asked); an answer of another shape is `island_error`. It is a read: while a `board.check` runs it is answered once the editor is free. No undo step, no `ev.edited`.
- Why an engine export (2026-11-16): KiCad's two reports are pages of its own Inspector window, which the island never shows. So the engine gained `kicadResolveRules(argsJson)`, a synchronous read through `DRC_ENGINE::EvalRules` after the board's custom rules compile, as the reports do (pcbjam `cc/main` 598e368; engine run 14).
- Measured on Glasgow over the local pair with engine run 14 (2026-11-17, `tests/e2e/rules.spec.ts`, Chromium then Firefox): a test point pad answers its mask expansion, both paste margins and the keep out verdict (the board's own settings), a 0.15 mm track its width from the Default net class, a pad and a track of two nets the clearance on `F.Cu` from the board's settings, a pad and a track of one net `sameNet`; each in 19 to 24 ms (the pair's median of 5, 19 ms); drc-small with a rules file that does not compile answers `rules_invalid`.
- With engine run 15 (island `v0.2.3-cc25`) `solder_mask_expansion` of a pad, shape or track carries its numbers from the board's mask constraint (`min`, `opt` and `max` as KiCad gives them, never all null), and the clearance of two pads of one through hole part names only the board's enabled copper layers (four on Glasgow), never the 32 copper layers a through hole pad's layer set holds. <!-- ext:lo-e3-i -->

### symbols.get and symbols.set
Every part's fields and flags in one table, read and changed with no KiCad window: the model of KiCad's Symbol Fields Table, with KiCad 10's design variants (island `v0.2.3-cc23`, on an engine with `kicadSymbolsGet` and `kicadSymbolsSet`, engine run 14; a host gates both on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `symbols`, a `sch` frame only, or before any engine boots on `island.json` `ops`). They read the schematic the editor holds, unsaved edits included.

- An instance is `{ uuid, sheetPath }` in `symbol.get`'s spelling: a symbol on a sheet shown more than once is one row per path, and each unit of a part of several units is a row of its own (`unit` and `units` say which; a host folds them). `variant` is `""` for the default, or one of the design's variant names (1 to 128 characters, no control character).
- `symbols.get` takes exactly `{ variant, scope: "all" }` or `{ variant, items }` (1 to 4,096 instances, none twice). The answer is `{ variant, variants, rows, power, truncated, depth }`: `variants` every variant the design names (its project file's and its sheets'), `rows` every non-power instance in the hierarchy's order (or the ones asked that the design holds, in the order asked; one it does not hold is left out), at most 4,096 (`truncated` past that), `power` the power symbols of the scope, counted and never listed, `depth` the undo depth. Each row is exactly `{ uuid, sheetPath, sheetName, ref, unit, units, libId, value, footprint, fields, inBom, onBoard, inSim, inPos, dnp, sheetHeld, overrides, filters, pins }`: `value`, `footprint` (`lib:name` or `""`) and `fields` (every field but the designator, value and footprint, in KiCad's order, each `{ name, value, shown }`, at most 256, KiCad's private fields left out) as they read under the variant; the five flags resolved as the Fields Table resolves them, the enclosing sheets' own flags and rule areas folded in (`inPos` has no sheet flag in KiCad); `sheetHeld` the flags a sheet or a rule area forces that the symbol's own does not; `overrides` what the variant changes (`value`, `footprint`, a field's name, or a flag's key), empty under the default; `filters` the symbol's footprint filters; `pins` its distinct pin numbers over every unit (what a footprint's pads match).
- `symbols.set` takes exactly `{ variant, changes }`, 1 to 4,096 changes of exactly `{ uuid, sheetPath, set }`, one per instance. A `set` holds at least one of `ref`, `value`, `footprint`, `fields`, `addField`, `removeField` with `symbol.set`'s bounds, and the flags `inBom`, `onBoard`, `inSim`, `inPos` (KiCad 10's "Exclude from position files", inverted) and `dnp`. Under a variant `ref`, `addField`, `removeField` and a field's `shown` refuse (a variant holds values and flags only, and a designator is the same in every variant), and `clear` (1 to 512 of what `overrides` names) drops the variant's own values, the part reading the default's again; `clear` outside a variant, or naming what the same set changes, refuses. Every change is ONE commit, "Edit fields", one undo step for the whole set, the other units of a part kept in step as `symbol.set` keeps them; the answer is `{ applied, depth }` (the changes applied, the undo depth after), and an `ev.edited` follows when the depth moved.
- The checks, in order, every one before any effect: the args (`bad_args`); as for `symbol.get` (the frame's kind: `unsupported` in a `pcb` frame; the engine; a document; `busy`); then for a set a read of the instances asked: `no_variant` (`message` the variant) when the design has no such variant, `not_found` when an instance is not in it (`message` "<uuid> on <sheetPath>", or how many are missing when the scope holds power symbols). `symbols.get` with a variant the design lacks answers `no_variant` as well. Then the engine's set queues the commit in a fresh folder `/tmp/cc-symbols/<n>/` and writes its record, `symbols.json`, then `.cc-symbols.json` (`{ error, message }`) last; the answer waits for the apply queue to empty and that file, and the folder is removed. `bad_args` ("the engine refused the set") when the engine refuses the changes at once (a field it does not hold to remove, a field to add it already holds); `no_variant` when the variant went between the check and the apply; `not_applied` past 30 s. While a `sheet.check` or `board.check` runs, `symbols.set` answers `busy` and `symbols.get` is answered.
- Why engine exports (2026-11-16): KiCad's Symbol Fields Table is a dialog the island never shows, and `symbol.get` reads one part at a time with no variant. So the engine gained `kicadSymbolsGet(argsJson)` and `kicadSymbolsSet(argsJson)` (pcbjam `cc/main` `wasm/bindings/cc_rules_sch.inc`; engine run 14), the table's resolution and `kicadSymbolSet`'s setters with the variant name, KiCad's own `SCH_SYMBOL` variant API.
- Measured over the local pair with engine run 14 (2026-11-17, `tests/e2e/properties-rules.spec.ts`, Chromium, from the request to the answer): on the variants fixture the table answers in 3.6 ms (1,911 bytes: R1 to R4, `power` 1, R4 `dnp` with `sheetHeld` `["dnp"]` from its sheet, R2 `dnp` under `Lite` with `overrides` `["dnp"]`, R3 4k7 under `Pro` with `["value"]`), a set of one change in 44 ms; on Glasgow the whole table in 19 ms (the median of five; 263 rows, 211 power symbols, 182,056 bytes), a set of one change in 135 to 139 ms and of all 263 rows in 322 to 333 ms, one undo step that Ctrl+Z (`key.press`) takes back; in Firefox the table in 27 ms (the median of five), the 263 rows set in 387 ms. In the GPU window (Chromium 157, medians of five): the table 21 ms, one change 33 ms, the 263 rows 281 ms; the schematic engine's memory went from 637 to 751 MiB over twenty `textVariables` sets and twenty 263-row sets (each set an undo step KiCad keeps; an engine's memory never shrinks).

### symbols.exchange and footprints.exchange
KiCad's Change Symbols and Update Symbols from Library, and its Change Footprints and Update Footprints from Library, with their reset options and no dialog (island `v0.2.3-cc23`, on an engine with `kicadSymbolsExchange` in a `sch` frame and `kicadFootprintsExchange` in a `pcb` frame, engine run 14; a host gates them on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `symbolExchange` and `footprintExchange`).

- `symbols.exchange` takes exactly `{ mode, targets, libId, fields, options, preview }`: `mode` `"update"` (each part from its own symbol, `libId` `""`) or `"change"` (every target to `libId`, `lib:name`); `targets` 1 to 4,096 instances, none twice; `fields` `"all"` or 1 to 256 field names the resets apply to; `options` any of the dialog's checkboxes, each a boolean, every one false when absent: `removeExtra`, `resetEmpty`, `resetText`, `resetVisibility`, `resetEffects`, `resetPositions`, `resetAttributes`, `resetPins` (KiCad's "Reset alternate pin assignments"), `resetPinTextVisibility` and `resetCustomPower`; `preview` a boolean. The symbol comes from the island's library mirror or a library the design registered through `project.libs`, as KiCad's own update reads it.
- `footprints.exchange` takes exactly `{ mode, match, libId, options, preview }`: `match` exactly one of `{ all: true }`, `{ uuids }` (1 to 8,192 footprints), `{ ref }` or `{ value }` (a glob, KiCad's `*` and `?`, 1 to 256 characters) or `{ libId }`; `libId` as above; `options` any of `removeExtraTexts`, `resetTextLayers`, `resetTextEffects`, `resetTextPositions`, `resetTextContent`, `resetFabrication`, `resetClearances` and `reset3D`.
- The answer is `{ preview, rows, applied, truncated, depth }`: a row per instance (`symbols.exchange`, `{ uuid, sheetPath, ref, from, to, status }`) or per footprint matched in the board's order (`footprints.exchange`, `{ uuid, ref, from, to, status }`), at most 4,096; `status` `changed`, `same` (an update the library would not change; the resets still apply), `missing` (no such symbol or footprint, or a symbol with too few units: that part is left alone) or `locked` (a locked footprint, exchanged all the same, as KiCad's dialog does); `applied` the parts processed. A preview changes nothing; an apply is ONE commit (one undo step, an `ev.edited` after the answer), none when nothing applied.
- The checks, in order, as for `symbols.set` (the frame's kind against the op's: `unsupported`). Then the engine's run in a fresh folder `/tmp/cc-exchange/<n>/`, its report `exchange.json`, then `.cc-exchange.json` last; `bad_args` ("the engine refused the exchange") when the engine refuses the args at once (an instance it does not hold, a `libId` its reader does not take), `not_applied` past 30 s. Writes, so `busy` during a check.
- Measured (2026-11-17, Chromium): on the variants fixture with the library mirror (`LIBS_DIR`), R2 changed to `Device:R_Small` previewed in 12.6 ms and applied in 28 ms (one undo step, its value kept), R1's update previewed in 25 ms; the four footprints' update previewed in 24 ms and R1 changed to `R_0805_2012Metric` in 11 ms (one undo step); without the mirror every row answers `missing` (the example library has neither). Glasgow's R1 previewed in 12 to 22 ms; in the GPU window Glasgow's U1 updated from the mirror previewed in 12.6 ms (`same`) and applied in 38 ms (one undo step).

### sch.edit and board.edit
KiCad's global edits with no dialog (island `v0.2.3-cc23`, on an engine with `kicadSchEdit` in a `sch` frame and `kicadBoardEdit` in a `pcb` frame, engine run 14; a host gates them on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `schEdit` and `boardEdit`). The args are exactly `{ op, preview, filter, set }`; each op's `filter` and `set` take only their own keys, of the kind named, and every key is optional but what the op needs to run:

| op | frame | filter | set | needs |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `textGraphics` | sch | `references`, `values`, `otherFields`, `labels`, `sheetTitles`, `sheetPins`, `texts`, `wires`, `buses`, `graphics`, `sheetFields`, `sheetBorders`, `labelFields`, `selected`, `power` (booleans), `sheetPath`, `refGlob`, `symbolGlob`, `fieldGlob`, `netGlob` (1 to 256 characters) | `size` (0.0254 to 254 mm), `lineWidth`, `junctionSize` (0 to 25.4 mm), `bold`, `italic`, `visible`, `showFieldNames`, `hAlign` (`left` `center` `right`), `vAlign` (`top` `center` `bottom`), `lineStyle` (`default` `solid` `dash` `dot` `dashDot` `dashDotDot`) | a target on, a set |
| `tracksVias` | pcb | `tracks`, `vias`, `throughVias`, `microVias`, `blindVias`, `buriedVias`, `selected`, `nets` (1 to 4,096 names), `netClass`, `layer`, `uuids`, `trackWidth`, `viaSize` (mm) | `width`, `viaDiameter`, `viaDrill` (0.001 to 100 mm, or `"netclass"`), `layer` (copper), `annularRings` (`keepAll` `removeExceptStartAndEnd` `removeAll` `startEndOnly`) | tracks or vias, a set |
| `textGraphics` | pcb | `references`, `values`, `otherFields`, `texts`, `graphics`, `dimensions`, `boardItems`, `footprintItems`, `selected`, `layer`, `refGlob`, `footprintGlob`, `uuids` | `layer`, `visible`, `bold`, `italic`, `keepUpright`, `knockout`, `autoThickness`, `defaults` (KiCad's "Set to layer default values"), `height`, `width` (0.001 to 250 mm), `thickness`, `lineWidth` (0.001 to 50 mm) | a target on, a set |
| `teardrops` | pcb | `roundPads`, `rectPads`, `vias`, `trackToTrack`, `pthPads`, `smdPads`, `existing`, and the net filter (`nets`, `netClass`, `layer`, `uuids`, `selected`) | `on`, `curved`, `allowTwoTracks`, `preferZoneConnection`, `lengthRatio`, `widthRatio` (fractions over 0, at most 10), `maxLength`, `maxWidth` (0 to 100 mm) | a target on, a set |
| `swapLayers` | pcb | none | `map`: layer to layer | the map |
| `deleteGlobal` | pcb | `zones`, `teardrops`, `texts`, `graphics`, `footprints`, `tracks`, `vias`, `outline`, `markers`, `all`, `locked`, `unlocked` (default on: locked items are kept unless `locked`), `layer` | none | a kind on |
| `cleanupTracks` | pcb | the net filter | `mergeCollinear`, `removeDangling`, `removeShorts`, `redundantVias`, `tracksInPads`, `danglingVias`, `refillZones` | a set |
| `unusedPads` | pcb | `uuids` | `vias`, `pads`, `keepOuter`, `remove` (default on; off restores, KiCad's "Restore") | vias or pads |
| `cleanupGraphics` | pcb | none | `mergeRects`, `fixOutline`, `deleteRedundant`, `tolerance` (0 to 10 mm) | one of the three |
| `repair` | pcb | none | none | |
| `reannotate` | pcb | none | `order` (0 to 7, KiCad's eight directions in its dialog's order), `round` (0.001 to 100 mm, default 1.27), `by` (`footprint` or `reference`), `frontStart`, `backStart` (0 to 1,000,000), `frontPrefix`, `backPrefix` (0 to 32 characters), `removePrefix`, `removeFrontPrefix`, `removeBackPrefix`, `skipLocked`, `scope` (`all` `selected` `front` `back`), `uuids`, `exclude` (KiCad's exclusion list, "R*, C") | |
| `zoneOrder` | pcb | none | `order`: 1 to 4,096 copper area uuids, none twice, the first the highest priority | the order |

A layer is the board file's name (`F.Cu`, `In1.Cu`); `uuids` 1 to 4,096 in KiCad's spelling. Colors are never set.

- The answer is `{ op, preview, rows, count, truncated, depth, notes }`: `rows` the items changed or to change, at most 4,096 (`count` the whole, `truncated` past the bound), each `{ uuid, kind, words }` and `from` and `to` together where a value changes (`"0.35 mm"`, `"In1.Cu"`, `"R4"`) and, for `reannotate`, `symbolPath` (the footprint's schematic path, `/<sheet uuid>/.../<symbol uuid>`: the host carries the new designators to the schematic with `symbols.set`); `kind` one of the schematic's `reference`, `value`, `sheetName`, `field`, `sheetPin`, `sheet`, `label`, `text`, `junction`, `wire`, `bus`, `graphic` or the board's `track`, `arc`, `via`, `pad`, `footprint`, `zone`, `field`, `text`, `shape`, `marker`, `group`, `dimension`, `item` (a field answers its symbol's or footprint's uuid; a board row's uuid is `""` when KiCad's cleaner names no item); `words` KiCad's own phrase ("Remove zero-length track", "R4 is now R12"); `notes` the run's lines, `reannotate`'s footprints that are not numbered and keep their designators. A preview changes nothing; an apply is ONE commit named for the op (one undo step, an `ev.edited` after the answer) but these, as in KiCad: `repair` makes no undo step, `cleanupTracks` with `refillZones` fills as a second step, and `deleteGlobal`'s markers go with no step.
- The checks, in order, as for `symbols.set`. Then the engine's run in a fresh folder `/tmp/cc-edit/<n>/`, its report `edit.json`, then `.cc-edit.json` last; `bad_args` ("the engine refused the edit") when the engine refuses the args at once (a layer the board does not use, a uuid that is no copper area), `duplicate_designator` when `reannotate` would leave two footprints one designator (nothing changes, a preview included; `message` names them: "two parts would share a designator: Duplicate instances of REF**"), `not_applied` past 30 s. Writes, so `busy` during a check.
- Measured (2026-11-17, Chromium): on the variants fixture the references' size previewed in 22 ms (five rows, "R2 Reference: size" from 1.270000 to 1.500000) and applied in 68 ms; the variants board numbered from 11 in 24 ms, the rows carrying the symbol paths (R4's through its sheet), one undo step. Previews on Glasgow: `cleanupTracks` 614 to 627 ms (221 rows), `tracksVias` 182 to 191 ms (344), `textGraphics` 13 to 19 ms (782), `teardrops` 21 to 25 ms (1,561), `swapLayers` 11 ms, `deleteGlobal` 11 to 21 ms (143), `unusedPads` 12 to 22 ms (565), `cleanupGraphics` 11 to 22 ms (none), `repair` 23 to 24 ms (none), `zoneOrder` of its three copper areas 11 ms; each left the undo depth as it was. `reannotate` on Glasgow answers `duplicate_designator` (Duplicate instances of REF**): Glasgow holds board-only footprints all named `REF**`, which the engine keeps and counts as one designator twice (engine run 14 behavior, not the island's). Firefox: `cleanupTracks` 694 ms. In the GPU window (medians of five): `cleanupTracks` 595 ms, `tracksVias` 162 ms, every other preview 13 to 16 ms; no long task on the host page meanwhile (the engine runs in the frame's own process).

### board.footprints
Every footprint of the board with what carrying its changes back to the schematic needs (island `v0.2.3-cc23`, on an engine with `kicadBoardFootprints`, engine run 14; a host gates it on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `boardFootprints`, a `pcb` frame only). No args. The answer is `{ footprints, variant, truncated }`: at most 8,192 footprints in the board's order, each exactly `{ uuid, ref, value, libId, symbolPath, side ("front" or "back"), locked, dnp, inBom, inPos, boardOnly, fields }` under the board's current variant (`variant`, `""` the default), `symbolPath` the schematic path or `""` for none, `fields` the user fields (`{ name, value, shown }`, at most 256). The checks as for `board.item.get`; a read, answered during a check. Measured (2026-11-17, Chromium): the variants board in 1.3 ms (R4 `dnp`, its path through the Unfitted sheet), Glasgow in 4.1 to 4.2 ms (the median of five; 123,486 bytes), 7.6 ms in Firefox, 4.5 ms in the GPU window.

### settings.get and settings.set
The design's setup, one section at a time, read and changed with no Board Setup, Schematic Setup or Page Settings window (island `v0.2.3-cc23`, on an engine with `kicadSettingsGet` and `kicadSettingsSet`, engine run 14; a host gates both on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `settings`). The args are `{ section }` for a read and `{ section, value, dryRun? }` for a set. The sections, and the frame that holds each:

| section | frame | what it is |
|---|---|---|
| `netClasses` | both | the project file's `net_settings`: `classes`, `netclass_patterns`, `netclass_assignments`, `net_colors` |
| `textVariables` | both | the project's text variables, name to text; a set's `null` removes one |
| `variants` | sch (pcb reads) | `{ names: [{ name, description }], current }`; a set is ONE action: `{ add: name }`, `{ copy: [from, to] }`, `{ rename: [from, to] }`, `{ delete: name }`, `{ describe: [name, text] }` or `{ current: name or "" }` |
| `page` | both | `paper` (KiCad's name: `A4`, `USLetter`, `User`), `width`, `height` (mm, for `User`), `portrait`, `title`, `date`, `revision`, `company`, `comments` (nine; a set's `null` keeps one), `drawingSheet`, and on the schematic `pageNumbers` (`{ sheetPath, page }`) and, in a set, `allSheets` |
| `schFormatting` | sch | the project file's `schematic.drawing` (but its field names), `connection_grid_size`, `subpart_id_separator`, `subpart_first_id`, `annotate_start_num` |
| `fieldTemplates` | sch | `field_names` (`{ name, visible, url }`) |
| `busAliases` | sch | `{ aliases: [{ name, members }] }`, a set replacing the list whole |
| `ercSeverities` | sch | `erc.rule_severities` |
| `pinMap` | sch | `erc.pin_map`, 12 by 12 of 0, 1 or 2 |
| `layers` | pcb | `{ copperCount, layers: [{ name, userName, type, enabled }] }`; a dry run answers `wouldRemove` |
| `stackup` | pcb | `{ rows, boardThickness }`, a row per dielectric sublayer |
| `finish` | pcb | `finish`, `edgePlating`, `castellated` (read only), `edgeConnector`, `impedanceControlled`, `maskExpansion`, `maskMinWidth`, `pasteClearance`, `pasteRatio` |
| `textDefaults` | pcb | `board.design_settings.defaults` |
| `constraints` | pcb | `board.design_settings.rules` |
| `predefinedSizes` | pcb | `track_widths`, `via_dimensions`, `diff_pair_dimensions` |
| `teardrops` | pcb | `teardrop_options`, `teardrop_parameters` |
| `checkSeverities` | pcb | `board.design_settings.rule_severities` |
| `customRules` | pcb | `{ text }`, the board's `.kicad_dru` (at most 256 KiB); a dry run answers its parse errors |

- Project-file sections carry KiCad's own JSON keys as the `.kicad_pro` stores them (its units included), so a host reads a section tolerantly: KiCad adds keys every version. `settings.get` answers the section as one plain object from the editor's memory, unsaved changes included. A set's `value` is a PATCH: a plain object of at least one key and at most 1 MiB of JSON, objects merged, arrays and numbers replaced, a key never removed (so no `null` in a project-file section's patch); the other sections take their own shapes above. The answer is `{ value, errors?, wouldRemove? }`: the section read back after the set, `errors` the custom rules' parse errors (`{ line, column, message }`, at most 1,024), `wouldRemove` the items a `layers` dry run's removed layers hold. A `dryRun` changes nothing; it reports for `customRules` and `layers` only.
- A set is applied with the frame's own sync, as KiCad's Setup windows' OK applies it (the board's net classes, rules and clearance cache; the schematic's connections), and KiCad never undoes a setting: no undo step, no `ev.edited`; removing copper layers that hold items deletes them and clears the undo list, as KiCad's Board Setup does; deleting, renaming or copying a variant a part held is one undo step. Nothing reaches the files until a save, but `customRules`: an apply writes the board's `.kicad_dru` and the frame sends it as `ev.saved` before the answer. The two frames hold one project file each: a host sets `netClasses` and `textVariables` in both.
- The checks, in order: the args (`bad_args`); the frame's kind against the section (`unsupported`, "pinMap is a schematic setting", "customRules is a board setting"; a `pcb` frame's `variants` set, "the board takes variants through Update the board"); then as for `symbols.set`. Then the engine's set in a fresh folder `/tmp/cc-settings/<n>/`, its record `settings.json`, then `.cc-settings.json` last; `bad_args` when the section does not take the value (`message` the engine's), `rules_invalid` when the custom rules do not compile (an apply writes nothing; `message` the first error), `not_applied` past 30 s. A set is a write (`busy` during a check); a read is answered.
- Why engine exports (2026-11-16): Board Setup, Schematic Setup and Page Settings are KiCad windows the island never shows, and KiCad's IPC handlers cover none of the severities, the constraints, the pin map, the field names, the bus aliases, the page or the variants. So the engine gained `kicadSettingsGet(section)` and `kicadSettingsSet(argsJson)`, dispatched to the frame's own editor (pcbjam `cc/main` `wasm/bindings/cc_rules_pcb.inc`, `cc_rules_sch.inc` and `kicad_editor_embind.cpp`; engine run 14).
- Measured (2026-11-17, Chromium): every section reads in 0.2 to 1.4 ms (Glasgow's board `layers` the largest, 9,347 bytes, 0.85 to 0.97 ms; the medians of five); a set of `textVariables` or `netClasses` on the variants schematic in 218 to 233 ms, of Glasgow's board `constraints` in 0.93 to 1.05 s, the custom rules applied on drc-small in 392 ms; drc-badrules' rules dry-run in 15 ms answer `{ line: 4, column: 14, message: "Unrecognized item 'clearence'. Expected assertion, clearance, ..." }`, and an apply of them `rules_invalid` with the file unchanged; no setting moved the undo depth. In the GPU window (medians of five): every section read in 0.2 to 0.9 ms, a `textVariables` set on Glasgow's schematic 296 ms, a `constraints` set on its board 844 ms; `netClasses` with Default's clearance at 0.3 mm, set in 969 ms, then `board.rules` between a pad of /SCL and a track of /SDA answers 0.3 mm from the net class Default where it answered 0.1499 mm from the board's settings before, and a `board.check` after it ran in 2.3 s; twenty `constraints` sets and twenty cleanup previews left the board engine's memory at 442.5 MiB.
