- A dielectric sublayer added or removed (island `v0.2.3-cc25`, engine run 15): a `stackup` set's `value` may be exactly `{ addDielectric: { after } }` or `{ removeDielectric: { index, sub } }` in place of `{ rows }`, one per set, as Board Setup's Add Dielectric Layer and Remove Dielectric Layer work (KiCad's own `BOARD_STACKUP_ITEM` calls, the board thickness rebuilt from the stackup). `after` and `index` name a stackup item as the section's rows name it (an enabled dielectric), `sub` one of its sublayers, each an integer from 0 to 255; add puts a new sublayer after the item's last, its material KiCad's "Not specified"; remove takes a sublayer of an item that has two or more. `dryRun` checks the form and changes nothing. The answer is the `stackup` section read back, one row more or one row less. A host gates both on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `settings:stackupLayers`, listed in a `pcb` frame exactly when the engine's `kicadSettingsSections()` names `stackupLayers` in its board half. <!-- ext:lo-e3-i -->
- The checks of a dielectric form, in order: the form (`bad_args`: both keys, another key beside one, any other key or type inside); in a `pcb` frame with the engine up, `unsupported` naming `kicadSettingsSections` on an engine without that marker (run 14) or `stackupLayers` when the marker does not list it; then as for every set (the frame's kind, a board open, `busy`). The engine answers `bad_args` when the item is no enabled dielectric, the stackup holds 256 rows already, or the item to take a sublayer from holds one only. No undo step (KiCad never undoes a setting). <!-- ext:lo-e3-i -->
- Eight sections more (island `v0.2.3-cc25`, engine run 15), the Setup panels KiCad's two Setup windows still held: in a `pcb` frame `zoneDefaults` (KiCad's 17 `defaults.zones` keys in mm, degrees and the file's enumeration numbers), `zoneHatchOffsets` (`{ layers: [{ layer, offset: [x, y] \| null }] }`, every enabled copper layer in KiCad's order, null the board default), `boardFormatting` (`applyToFields`, `applyToText`, `applyToShapes`, `applyToDimensions`, `applyToBarcodes`, `dashRatio`, `gapRatio`), `tuningPatterns` (`single_track_defaults`, `diff_pair_defaults`, `diff_pair_skew_defaults`, each `min_amplitude`, `max_amplitude`, `spacing`, `corner_style`, `corner_radius_percentage`, `single_sided`), `componentClasses` (`"sheet_component_classes.enabled"`, `assignments`) and `tuningProfiles` (`tuning_profiles_impedance_geometric`, KiCad's own JSON, its widths, gaps and delays in KiCad's internal units); in a `sch` frame `annotation` (`"annotation.sort_order"`, `"annotation.method"`, `annotate_start_num`, `reuse_designators`), `bomPresets` (`bom_presets`, `bom_fmt_presets`, `bom_settings`, `bom_fmt_settings`) and `componentClasses` (read only there). A host gates each on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `settings:<section>`, listed exactly when the engine's `kicadSettingsSections()` names the section in the frame's half; on any other engine a read answers `unsupported` naming `kicadSettingsSections` (run 14) or the section. <!-- ext:m7-i -->
- The eight sections of engine run 15 (island `v0.2.3-cc25`; `settings.get` above names their keys) are set as patches, each value read closed before the engine is asked (`bad_args`: a key the section does not name, a value of another kind or out of KiCad's bounds, a null but a hatch offset's): `zoneDefaults` numbers, none negative but `hatch_orientation`; `zoneHatchOffsets` replaces the list, an enabled copper layer not named going back to the board default, each offset within 1,000 mm; `boardFormatting` ratios over 0 and at most 1,000; `tuningPatterns` merges each object, `corner_style` 0 or 1, `corner_radius_percentage` 0 to 100; `componentClasses` replaces `assignments`, each exactly `{ component_class, conditions_operator: "ALL" \| "ANY", conditions: { TYPE[-n]: { primary?, secondary? } } }`, TYPE one of `REFERENCE`, `FOOTPRINT`, `SIDE`, `ROTATION`, `FOOTPRINT_FIELD`, `CUSTOM`, `SHEET_NAME`; `tuningProfiles` replaces the list whole (names unique and not empty, type 0 or 1, a signal layer once per profile); `annotation` sort order 0 or 1, method 0 to 2, start 0 to 999,999 (the one key `schFormatting` also sets); `bomPresets` each value read by KiCad's own loader first and replacing its key whole. A `sch` frame's `componentClasses` set answers `unsupported` ("the schematic reads componentClasses and never sets it"): the board editor sets the classes. Then the engine as for a read (`unsupported` naming `kicadSettingsSections` or the section), then as for every set. Each set runs Board Setup's or Schematic Setup's own steps after it (the zone defaults loaded, the component classes and tuning profiles synchronized, the plot options and the painter's dash ratios set); no undo step, no `ev.edited`. <!-- ext:m7-i -->

### The symbol editor: a symedit frame and the symedit ops
KiCad's own symbol editor (`SYMBOL_EDIT_FRAME`, wx name `LibeditFrame`) booted with `?frame=symedit`, canvas only, driven with no KiCad window (island `v0.2.3-cc23`, on an engine with the symedit exports, engine run 14: pcbjam `cc/main` `wasm/bindings/symedit_embind.cpp`, kicad `cc/main` K1 to K3; a host gates the ops on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `symedit`, or on `island.json` `ops` before any engine boots). A symbol is opened from its text with no library row (the engine's host mode), every change is ONE commit on the editor's apply coroutine (one undo step, as KiCad's own tools make one), and the host takes the symbol back as text (`symedit.take`) and writes the design's file itself: KiCad's own library save, its library tree and its library tables are never used. The frame boots with no library walk (K1: the tree syncs only the project's own rows, so KiCad's "Loading Symbol Libraries" box never shows and no `ev.menu` comes at boot).

- **The frame.** A `symedit` frame holds no document. It answers `project.open`, `project.import`, `project.save`, `sheet.tree`, `sheet.enter`, `layers.*`, `place`, `footprint.models`, `project.plot`, `symbol.get`, `symbol.set`, `symbol.forEditor`, `symbol.push`, `lib.update`, `sheet.annotate`, `sheet.check` and every board op `unsupported` ("a symbol editor opens symbols, not documents"), whatever their args. `project.write` (every file taken, no `open_document`), `project.libs`, `project.forget`, the library reads, `key.press`, `keys.claim`, `chrome.show`, `readonly`, `view.fit`, `select` (above) and `shutdown` work as in any frame. The place keys stay KiCad's (no `picker`, no `ev.pick`), and the quiet library warm-up does not run. `ev.edited` reads the symbol editor's own undo depth (`SymEditState.depth`) while a symbol is open, the first read after each open or new symbol the baseline; `ev.selected` reads its own selection (`fpPaths` always `[]`) while a symbol is open. In a `sch` or a `pcb` frame every `symedit.*` op answers `unsupported` ("a symbol edits in a symedit frame").
- **The checks**, in order, every one before any effect: the args (`bad_args`); the frame's kind (`unsupported`); the engine (`not_ready` before `ev.ready`; `unsupported` naming the first missing export of `kicadSymEditOpen`, `kicadSymEditNew`, `kicadSymEditState`, `kicadSymEditTake`, `kicadSymEditMarkSaved`, `kicadSymEditItemsGet`, `kicadSymEditItemsSet`, `kicadSymEditItemsAdd`, `kicadSymEditItemsRemove`, `kicadSymEditSymbolGet`, `kicadSymEditSymbolSet`, `kicadSymEditShow`, `kicadSymEditSelection`, `kicadSymEditSelect`, `kicadSymEditCheck`, `kicadSymEditPlotSvg`, `kicadCollabBusy`); a load parked, a popup menu or a dialog up (`busy`, nothing changed); then `no_symbol` for every op but `symedit.open`, `symedit.new` and `symedit.state` while no symbol is open, and `held_edits` for an open or a new symbol over a modified one without `discard`. A change answers once the engine's apply queue is empty and the read back shows every key it set (lengths within KiCad's rounding: 4 decimals of a millimeter), else `not_applied` after about 5 s; an `ev.edited` follows.
- **Records.** Strings are at most 1,024 characters unless named, numbers finite, uuids KiCad's lower case `8-4-4-4-12`; lengths and positions in millimeters, angles in degrees, positions in the symbol's own frame with Y up (as KiCad's symbol editor shows them). An answer of any other shape is `island_error`.
  - `SymEditState`: exactly `{ token, open, name, lib, mode ("library", "placed" or "new"), modified, depth (the undo depth), unit, unitCount, bodyStyle, bodyStyles: { mode ("one", "demorgan" or "named"), names }, parent ("" unless derived), syncedPins }`. `token` is the island's own for the last open (1 to 64 printable characters, `""` before any), which `symedit.markSaved` names.
  - `SymItem`, each with exactly its kind's keys, `unit` and `bodyStyle` 0 meaning every unit or body style: a pin `{ kind: "pin", uuid, unit, bodyStyle, number, name, type, shape, x, y, orientation ("right", "left", "up", "down"), length, nameSize, numberSize, visible, alternates: [{ name, type, shape }] }` (at most 64 alternates; `type` KiCad's file token: `input`, `output`, `bidirectional`, `tri_state`, `passive`, `free`, `unspecified`, `power_in`, `power_out`, `open_collector`, `open_emitter`, `no_connect`; `shape` `line`, `inverted`, `clock`, `inverted_clock`, `input_low`, `clock_low`, `output_low`, `edge_clock_high`, `non_logic`); a field or a text `{ kind: "field" | "text", uuid, unit, bodyStyle, name (a field's KiCad name, "" for a text), text, x, y, angle (0 or 90), size, bold, italic, hJustify ("left", "center", "right"), vJustify ("top", "center", "bottom"), visible, showName }`; a text box `{ kind: "textbox", uuid, unit, bodyStyle, text, x, y, angle, size, bold, italic, hJustify, vJustify, visible, x2, y2, stroke, fill, margins: [left, top, right, bottom] }`; a shape `{ kind: "shape", uuid, unit, bodyStyle, shape ("rectangle", "circle", "arc", "polyline", "bezier"), points, stroke, fill }`, `points` per shape: two corners, a center and a point on the circle, an arc's start, middle and end, a line's 2 to 4,096 points, a curve's four controls. A stroke is `{ width, style ("default", "solid", "dash", "dot", "dash_dot", "dash_dot_dot"), color ("" or #rrggbbaa) }`, a fill `{ type, color }` with `type` one of `none`, `outline`, `background`, `color`, and the hatches KiCad 10 also stores, `hatch`, `reverse_hatch`, `cross_hatch`.
  - `SymbolProps`: exactly `{ name, refPrefix, value, footprint, datasheet, description, keywords, fpFilters, fields: [{ name, value, visible, kind }], unitCount, unitsInterchangeable, unitNames ({ "2": "B" }), bodyStyles, power ("none", "global", "local"), pinNameOffset, showPinNames, showPinNumbers, pinNamesInside, inBom, onBoard, inSim, inPosFiles, jumpers: { duplicateNumbersAreJumpers, groups }, parent }`.
  - `SymFinding`: exactly `{ code, severity ("warning" or "info"), pins, unit, bodyStyle, x, y (both null when KiCad names no place), uuids }`, `code` one of `empty_reference`, `reference_prefix_digit`, `duplicate_pin`, `power_units`, `power_pins`, `power_pin_type`, `power_pin_hidden`, `hidden_power_pin`, `off_grid_pin`, `circle_zero_radius`, `rectangle_zero_size`, and `other` for a code a later engine adds.
- **`symedit.open`** opens a symbol: `text` a `kicad_symbol_lib` document of at most 4 MiB of UTF-8 holding `name` and its `extends` chain (the engine reads it with KiCad's own reader), `name` and `lib` 1 to 1,024 characters with no control character (`lib` the nickname the symbol opens under: never a row of the engine's table), `mode` `library` or `placed` (`placed` keeps the symbol as KiCad's Edit with Symbol Editor makes it, title and undo as KiCad's from-schematic mode), `unit` 1 to 702 and `bodyStyle` 1 to 64 (1 when absent; the engine clamps them to the symbol's), `discard` a boolean. The undo list starts empty and the symbol unmodified. `bad_args` "the text holds no symbol <name> KiCad reads" when the engine refuses the text. The answer is the state once the engine shows the open, with a fresh token.
- **`symedit.new`** makes a symbol as KiCad's New Symbol dialog does, with no dialog: exactly `{ name, lib, parentText, parent, refPrefix, unitCount (1 to 702), unitsInterchangeable, bodyStyles ("one" or "demorgan"), power ("none", "global", "local"), pinNameOffset (0 to 50), showPinNames, showPinNumbers, pinNamesInside, inBom, onBoard, inSim, keepParentFields, discard }`; `parent` `""` with `parentText` `""`, or a name inside `parentText` (a symbol document, then required): a derived symbol takes its parent's units and body styles. Its state reads `mode` `new`.
- **`symedit.take`** answers the open symbol as a `kicad_symbol_lib` document by KiCad's own formatter, its parent included when it is derived: `{ name, text, parent }`. A read: nothing changes. **`symedit.markSaved { token }`** clears the modified flag once the host has written what it took (`bad_args` when `token` is not the state's; no undo step).
- **`symedit.items.get`**: `unit` and `bodyStyle` whole numbers (0 for all), `uuids` 1 to 4,096 none twice, `kinds` 1 to 5 none twice; the items in KiCad's draw order. **`symedit.items.set`**: `uuids` as above and `set`, a non-empty partial of the keys the items' kinds take (never `kind` or `uuid`; a pin `unit`, `bodyStyle`, `number`, `name`, `type`, `shape`, `x`, `y`, `orientation`, `length`, `nameSize`, `numberSize`, `visible`, `alternates` (the list replaced); a field `name`, `text`, `x`, `y`, `angle`, `size`, `bold`, `italic`, `hJustify`, `vJustify`, `visible`, `showName`; a text those less `name` and `showName` plus `unit`, `bodyStyle`; a text box a text's plus `x2`, `y2`, `stroke`, `fill`, `margins`; a shape `unit`, `bodyStyle`, `points`, `stroke`, `fill`); one commit "Edit". A pin key follows KiCad's synced pins mode (the pin dialog's own rule: the same pin of the other units and body styles takes it while the mode is on). `not_found` names the first uuid the symbol does not hold; `bad_args` names a key another kind takes, a mandatory field's name, a field renamed over several items, a shape's point count; the engine's own refusals (a unit past the symbol's units, a field name another field holds) answer `bad_args`. A text holds line breaks; no other control character anywhere.
- **`symedit.items.add`**: 1 to 512 new items, each a full item of its kind (every key the kind takes and no other, and its `kind` and its uuid; a shape its `shape` with the points it takes; a field as a user field, `unit` and `bodyStyle` 0 and a name no field holds; a text with `name` `""` and `showName` false), each uuid the host's own, lower case, none the symbol holds (`bad_args` "<uuid> is taken"); one commit "Add". **`symedit.items.remove`**: 1 to 4,096 uuids, every one held (`not_found`), none of the five mandatory fields (`bad_args`); one commit "Delete"; the answer once none reads back.
- **`symedit.symbol.get`** and **`symedit.symbol.set { set }`**: `set` holds at least one of `name` (1 to 1,024), `refPrefix`, `value`, `footprint`, `datasheet`, `description`, `keywords`, `fpFilters` (at most 256, none empty), `fields` (at most 256 `{ name, value?, visible? }`, each with a value or a visibility, a field named once), `addField` (`{ name, value, visible }`), `removeField` (a user field), `unitCount`, `unitsInterchangeable`, `unitNames` (keys "1" to the unit count), `bodyStyles` (`one` or `demorgan` with no names, or `named` with 2 to 64), `power`, `pinNameOffset`, `showPinNames`, `showPinNumbers`, `pinNamesInside`, `inBom`, `onBoard`, `inSim`, `inPosFiles`, `jumpers` (`duplicateNumbersAreJumpers`, `groups` of the symbol's own pin numbers), `fromParent` (a derived symbol's Take fields from the parent: `{ fields: "all" | names, resetEmpty, resetVisibility, resetEffects, resetPositions, removeExtra }`) and `flatten: true` (a derived symbol made independent); a field is changed once (by its own key or in `fields`, never both, never removed and changed or added again in any casing). One commit "Symbol properties" with KiCad's properties dialog's own setters in its order; a power symbol's value is its name (the dialog's rule). The engine's refusals (a field it lacks, a taken name, `fromParent` or `flatten` on a root symbol) answer `bad_args`.
- **`symedit.show { unit, bodyStyle }`** shows a unit and a body style: no undo step, no modification. `bad_args` for a unit past the state's `unitCount`, or a body style the engine refuses.
- **`symedit.check`** runs KiCad's Symbol Checker (`CheckLibSymbol`, with K2's findings beside its messages) on the grid KiCad's own checker uses (the view's, at least 25 mil), `grid` in millimeters. A read.
- **`symedit.svg`** plots the shown unit and body style with KiCad's own SVG plot (`SVGPlotSymbol`) into a fresh folder `/tmp/cc-svg/<n>/` (an earlier run's removed first), its record `.cc-svg.json` written last; the answer is the file's bytes (a fresh `Uint8Array`, transferred, at most 4 MiB), the folder removed. `plot_failed` when KiCad wrote no SVG; `not_applied` when no record came within about 30 s.
- **Gate 0 and measurements** (2026-11-17, the local pair, `tests/e2e/symedit.spec.ts` on the island's `symedit-small` fixture, engine run 14 built locally, Playwright's Chromium, then Firefox): `ev.ready` 1.5 s after navigation (2.5 s), no `ev.menu` within 5 s after it, the engine's heap 256 MiB at ready and 5 s after (the spec's own measure of 2026-11-16 on engine run 13: 531 MiB for an empty `sch` frame, and the library walk K1 removes cost about 650 MB). `symedit.open` of OPAMP2 answers in 35 ms (28 ms), `symedit.take` in 0.6 ms (9.2 ms), a pin key's set in 44 ms (20 ms) (medians of 5 and 12). Take, open, take is byte for byte equal on every fixture symbol. Ten opens and takes left the heap at 369 MiB (Chromium; Firefox's frame is out of the test's reach). KiCad's checker on CHECKME answers exactly its four findings (`reference_prefix_digit`, `duplicate_pin` pin 3, `hidden_power_pin` pin 1 `info`, `off_grid_pin` pin 2); the SVG of CHECKME is 12,410 bytes.
- Two behaviors measured and open: a `symedit.new` with `bodyStyles` `demorgan` makes a symbol with one body style (its state and symbol read `one`, `symedit.show` refuses body style 2; engine run 14); and in Firefox KiCad's pin tool (`P`, then a press) asks the host (`ev.symedit` `pin`, `isNew`) and never places the pin, where Chromium places it, and its twins in the other units, with one undo step.
- On engine run 15 (island `v0.2.3-cc25`) a `symedit.new` with `bodyStyles` `demorgan` makes a symbol with two body styles: its state and `symedit.symbol.get` read `bodyStyles.mode` `demorgan` and `symedit.show` steps to body style 2 (engine run 14 made one). `tests/e2e/symedit-faults.spec.ts`. <!-- ext:sy4-i -->

### symbol.convert
Reads a symbol file of another tool, or of an older KiCad, with KiCad's own readers, and answers its symbols as text, each as `symedit.take` formats one (island `v0.2.3-cc23`, on an engine with `kicadSymbolLibConvert`; a host gates it on `caps` listing `symbolConvert`, listed by `sch` and `symedit` frames). The args are exactly `{ name, bytes }`: the file's name (1 to 255 bytes of UTF-8, no slash, backslash or NUL, never `.` or `..`; its extension picks the reader as KiCad's own `SCH_IO_MGR` guesses it) and its bytes (a `Uint8Array` of 1 byte to 25 MiB). The frame writes the file under a fresh `/tmp/cc-import/<n>/`, outside the project folder, and removes the folder after the read. The answer is `{ format, symbols: [{ name, text }] }`, `format` KiCad's own name of the reader (`KiCad`, `Legacy`, `EAGLE`, `EasyEDA (JLCEDA) Std`, ...), each name escaped as a library id holds it. `unreadable` when no reader takes the file or the reader refuses it (`message` KiCad's sentence), `too_large` past 2,000 symbols or 4 MiB of text. A `pcb` frame answers `unsupported`. The checks: the args, the frame, the engine (`not_ready`, `unsupported`), busy as for `key.press`. Measured (the e2e, 2026-11-17): the fixture's legacy `.lib` reads as `Legacy` with `LEGACY_R` in 25 ms (40 ms in Firefox), its Eagle `.lbr` as `EAGLE` with `EAGLE_R` in 27 ms (22 ms), and each converted symbol opens in a `symedit` frame as it is. The engine run's own probe read KiCad, Legacy, EAGLE and EasyEDA Std; CADSTAR is refused by KiCad itself without its `.csa`; Altium's `.SchLib` is untested (no sample).

### symbol.forEditor and symbol.push
The schematic side of the symbol editor, in a `sch` frame with a schematic open (island `v0.2.3-cc23`, on an engine with `kicadSymbolForEditor` and `kicadSymbolPushToSchematic`; a host gates both on `caps` listing `symbolEdit`). The checks are `symbol.get`'s (the args, the frame's kind, the engine with `kicadSymbolForEditor`, `kicadSymbolPushToSchematic`, `kicadSymbolGet` and `kicadCollabBusy`, a schematic, busy).

- `symbol.forEditor { uuid, sheetPath }` answers the placed symbol exactly as KiCad's Edit with Symbol Editor builds it (`LoadSymbolFromSchematic`: its library copy flattened, the instance's fields carried back through its rotation and mirror) as a document: `{ name, libId, text }`, ready for `symedit.open` with `mode` `placed`. `not_found` (the uuid) when no sheet holds it.
- `symbol.push { uuid, sheetPath, text, name, libId }` is KiCad's save of an edited symbol to the schematic (`SaveSymbolToSchematic`: every unit of the part, by its designator) as ONE commit "Edit symbol": one undo step and an `ev.edited`. `libId` `""` keeps each unit's library id; `lib:name` (both halves) sets it in the same commit, so the part names the design's own copy. The answer is the symbol sheet read back once the apply queue is empty (and, with a `libId`, naming it). `not_found` "<uuid> on <sheetPath>" when that path does not hold it; `bad_args` "the text holds no symbol <name> KiCad reads" when the engine refuses the text; `not_applied` past about 5 s. The host's next `project.save` writes the change.
- Measured (`tests/e2e/symbol-edit.spec.ts`, 2026-11-17): `symbol.forEditor` of U1 23 ms (2 ms in Firefox), `symbol.push` 22 ms (18 ms), unit B carrying the pushed drawing too, a relinked push reading its new library id, Ctrl+Z taking it back.
- On engine run 15 (island `v0.2.3-cc25`) a placed part's text opened with `symedit.open` `mode` `placed` reads the designator prefix alone: `symedit.symbol.get` answers `refPrefix` `"U"` for U1, never `"U1"`, so `symedit.check` finds no `reference_prefix_digit` for it, and a `refPrefix` set there keeps the number (`IC` makes the Reference field `IC1`). Engine run 14 read the whole designator. `tests/e2e/symedit-faults.spec.ts`. <!-- ext:sy4-i -->

### lib.update
KiCad's Update Symbols from Library with the dialog's defaults (keep the fields' text and places, take the new body, pins and attributes) for named symbols of one library, in a `sch` frame with a schematic open (island `v0.2.3-cc23`, on an engine with `kicadUpdateFromLibrary`; a host gates it on `caps` listing `libUpdate`). The args are exactly `{ lib, names }`: a nickname as `project.libs` takes one and 1 to 64 symbol names, none twice. The placed symbols whose library id is `lib:<name>` take the library's current drawing in one commit; the answer is the engine's `pcbjam:lib-update-done` event: `{ updated, missing }` (the parts updated; the library ids it could not find). A host writes the library and registers it (`project.write`, `project.libs`) first. The checks are `symbol.get`'s with `kicadUpdateFromLibrary` and `kicadCollabBusy`; `not_applied` when the engine does not queue the run or its event has not come within about 30 s. Measured: OPAMP2's two placed units updated in 23 ms (29 ms in Firefox).

### The simulator: sim.*
KiCad's own simulator with no KiCad window (island `v0.2.3-cc23`, on engine run 14). KiCad's `NGSPICE` class in the schematic engine, driven through our engine's exports (pcbjam `cc/main`: `kicadSimModelCatalog`, `kicadSimModelGet`, `kicadSimModelSet`, `kicadSimFileRead` for the models, `kicadSimNetlist`, `kicadSimRun`, `kicadSimStop`, `kicadSimOpOnSheet` for the runs), runs ngspice 46 (XSPICE and CIDER in) in the simulator service: `ngspice_service.js` and `ngspice_service.wasm`, built by PCBJam's recipe beside the engine and served from the release's engine folder (`PIN.json` records both). The engine links no ngspice: its sharedspice client (`wasm/stubs/sharedspice_client.cpp`) asks `globalThis.ngspiceService`, PCBJam's provider, which the island installs at boot in a `sch` frame when the release serves the service (`loader/src/wasm/ngspice-service.ts`, `src/sim-service.ts`). The provider makes its worker on the engine's first request, which the first `sim.run` of a frame makes (KiCad's `NGSPICE::Init`), so a frame that never runs a simulation never fetches the service. The worker fetches the wasm itself and posts its progress, and the island hears every event of the service (`ev.sim` below). A host gates the model ops on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `simModel` and the runs on `sim`, or before any engine boots on `island.json` `ops`.

- **The frame.** Every sim op answers `unsupported` ("the simulator runs in a sch frame") in a `pcb` or `symedit` frame, whatever its args: the simulator lives in the schematic engine. `simModel` and `sim` are listed by `sch` frames only.
- **The checks**, in order, every one before any effect: the args (`bad_args`); the frame's kind (`unsupported`); the engine (`not_ready` before `ev.ready`; `unsupported` naming the first missing export, `kicadCollabBusy` among them: the four model exports for the model ops, the four run exports for the others); for `sim.run`, `sim.result`, `sim.vectors`, `sim.stop` and `sim.release` the service (`unsupported` "ngspice_service.wasm" when the release serves none); a schematic opened by `project.open` or `project.import` (`not_ready`); for `sim.model.set`, `sim.netlist`, `sim.run` and `sim.opOnSheet` a load parked, a popup menu or a dialog up, or an item placed by `place` hanging off the pointer (`busy`). They wait in the request queue like any request (`sim.stop` and `sim.release` excepted, above); while a `project.import`, a `sheet.check` or a `board.check` runs they answer `busy`.
- **Records.** `Problem` is exactly `{ code, severity, text }` with any of `ref`, `uuid`, `sheetPath`, `path`: `code` one of `no_model`, `model_error`, `file_missing`, `file_error`, `pin_map`, `other`; `severity` `error` or `warning`; `text` KiCad's own English (at most 4,096 characters), for a log only: a host words each row by its `code`. At most 4,096 problems are listed. An answer of the engine of any other shape is `island_error`.
- **`sim.catalog`** reads KiCad's compiled model tables. With no args (or `{}`): `{ devices: [{ device, description, types: [{ type, name, description }] }] }`, every device the model dialog's Built-in tab lists (measured on engine run 14: 19 devices and 97 types, `R C L K TLINE SW D NPN PNP NJFET PJFET NMES PMES NMOS PMOS V I XSPICE SPICE`). A type is named by its key: the `Sim.Device` word, then `.` and the `Sim.Type` word when the type has one (`R`, `V.DC`, `V.PULSE`, `NMOS.BSIM4`, and `R.=`, `V.=` for KiCad's behavioral ones). `{ type }` answers that type: `{ type, device, name, description, pins: [{ name, symbolPinHint }], params: [{ name, unit, default, category, description, kind, tuner }] }`, `category` KiCad's grouping (`principal`, `geometry`, `ac`, `dc`, `capacitance`, `temperature`, `noise`, `distributed_quantities`, `limiting_values`, `s_param`, `advanced`, `flags`, `waveform`, `initial_conditions`, `superfluous`), `kind` `instance`, `model` or `other` (an output of the simulator, never set), `tuner` true for the one parameter KiCad's tuner moves. A type key the tables do not hold answers `bad_args`.
- **`sim.model.get { uuid, sheetPath }`** answers one symbol's model as KiCad resolves it for the netlist (`SIM_LIB_MGR::CreateModel`, the schematic's and the symbol's embedded files included), `SimModelSheet`: exactly `{ uuid, sheetPath, ref, enabled (the symbol's inSim), source ("inferred" from its value, "builtin", "file" or "none"), device, type (a key as above), typeName, file ({ path, model } for a file model, else null), pins: [{ symbolPin, modelPin }], params: [{ name, value, default, unit, given }], problems }`, and for an IBIS part also `ibis: { pin, model, diff, type }`. `sheetPath` follows `symbol.get`'s rule (the asked path, else the shown sheet, else the first holding the symbol). `not_found` (the uuid) when no sheet holds it.
- **`sim.model.set { uuid, sheetPath, model }`** sets or removes a symbol's model as ONE commit "Edit simulation model": one undo step and an `ev.edited`. `model` is `null` (the `Sim.*` fields removed: the model is inferred from the value again), `{ source: "builtin", type, params, pins }` or `{ source: "file", path, model, params, pins }`: `type` a key the catalog lists (a device its Built-in tab offers: never a subcircuit or an IBIS part), `params` an object of at most 1,024 parameter names (1 to 64 characters) to values (strings of at most 1,024 characters, `""` for the model's default), `pins` at most 1,024 `{ symbolPin, modelPin }` (1 to 64 characters each, neither named twice; `[]` keeps the model's default map, model pin n on symbol pin n), `path` a model file's path relative to the project folder (no `.` or `..` segment, no leading slash, no backslash, no `$`, no `:`, at most 512 characters) or `kicad-embed://<name>` (a file KiCad desktop embedded in the schematic), `model` the model's name in it (1 to 256 characters). An IBIS file's part takes four reserved params beside its own: `ibis.pin`, `ibis.model`, `ibis.diff` (`""` or `1`) and `ibis.type` (`DEVICE`, `DCDRIVER`, `RECTDRIVER`, `PRBSDRIVER`). The symbol must be on `sheetPath` (`not_found` "<uuid> on <sheetPath>"). The engine reads the set whole on a scratch model before anything is queued (the type known, every param the type's and its value taken by KiCad's `SetParamValue`, the pin map naming the symbol's pins and the model's, the file read and holding the model): a refusal answers `model_invalid` and nothing changes. The answer is the model read back once the apply queue is empty and it shows the set (its source and type, and for a file model its path and model); `not_applied` when it does not within about 5 s. KiCad's `SetParamValue` keeps a value it cannot read as a number as text (measured: `r` `abc` was taken), so a host checks its numbers itself.
- **`sim.file { path }`** reads a SPICE or IBIS model file of the project folder (or an embedded one) with KiCad's own readers: `SimFile`, exactly `{ kind ("spice" or "ibis"), models: [{ name, kind ("model", "subckt" or "ibis"), type, pins }], problems }`. A missing file answers no models and a `file_missing` problem. A host writes a brought file into the folder first with `project.write` (`models/<name>` is the site's place). Measured on the island's `sim-models` fixture: `Q2N3904` read as `NPN.GUMMELPOON` with pins `C`, `B`, `E`, `<S>`; the subcircuit `AMP10` as `subckt` with no pins listed; the IBIS component `CC_BUF` as `IBIS.DEVICE` with pins `1`, `2`, `3`.
- **The analysis line.** The island writes it, never the host: `SimAnalysis` is one of `{ kind: "op" }`, `{ kind: "dc", source, start, stop, step, second?: { source, start, stop, step } }`, `{ kind: "ac", scale ("dec", "oct", "lin"), points, fStart, fStop }`, `{ kind: "tran", step, stop, start?, maxStep?, useIc? }`, `{ kind: "noise", output, reference?, source, scale, points, fStart, fStop }`, `{ kind: "pz", input: [a, b], output: [a, b], transfer ("vol", "cur"), find ("pz", "pol", "zer") }`, `{ kind: "sp", scale, points, fStart, fStop }`, `{ kind: "fft", of, signals, window? }` and `{ kind: "sheet" }`, each with exactly its keys. Every number is a SPICE number (`^[+-]?(\d+\.?\d*|\.\d+)([eE][+-]?\d+)?(f|p|n|u|m|k|meg|g|t)?$` in any case, at most 64 characters: `1meg` is a million, `1m` a thousandth), `points` a whole number 1 to 1,000,000, every net or source one word (no white space, quote, comma, parenthesis, equals sign, semicolon or backslash, at most 128 characters) and a name of the netlist: a node of its element lines, and for a source an element named V, I or R (a DC sweep takes all three or `TEMP`, noise V or I). A name the netlist the island last read does not hold makes the island read the netlist again once, and still unknown it answers `bad_args` ("the netlist names no <name>"). The lines are written as KiCad's simulation command dialog writes them: `.op`; `.dc <source> <start> <stop> <step> [<source2> ...]` (the two sources different); `.ac <scale> <points> <fStart> <fStop>`; `.sp ...` the same; `.tran <step> <stop> [<start> [<maxStep>]] [uic]` (with `uic` and no largest step, the smaller of a fiftieth of the span and the step, as the dialog); `.noise v(<output>[,<reference>]) <source> <scale> <points> <fStart> <fStop>`; `.pz <in> <inRef> <out> <outRef> <transfer> <find>`. `{ kind: "sheet" }` is the schematic's own directive (the first analysis of its text, `no_directive` when it has none). An FFT reads a transient: `of` is that run (a whole number of at least 1, the latest transient, else `no_simulation`), `signals` 1 to 64 of its vectors, each by ngspice's name or KiCad's signal (`/out` or `V(/out)`, in any case, else `bad_args`), `window` one of `none`, `rectangular`, `bartlet`, `blackman`, `hanning`, `hamming`, `gaussian`, `flattop`; its lines are `set specwindow=<window>` (when asked), `linearize <signals>` and `fft <signals>`, each signal written as KiCad's (ngspice's command reader reads a bare `/out` as a division: measured, "syntax error in line segment" and no transform). The FFT's result holds its spectrum plot (x `frequency`) and the linearized copy of the transient. `SimOptions` is exactly `{ voltages, currents, dissipations, events }`, KiCad's four save switches (its defaults true, true, false, false). Nothing else of SPICE crosses the protocol: no `.control`, no free text.
- **`sim.netlist { analysis, options }`** answers KiCad's SPICE netlist of the whole schematic with the island's line as its analysis (an FFT makes none: `bad_args`): `{ netlist, problems, directive }`, `directive` the schematic's own analysis line (`""` when it has none), `netlist` at most 4 MiB (`truncated: true` past it). Its problems are the run's would be: the parts not numbered or numbered twice, each model KiCad could not make, the netlist's own errors. KiCad's netlist names a local net with its sheet's slash (`/out`) and ground `0` (measured on `sim-rc`: `R1 /in /out 1k`). `busy` while a run is live (KiCad's IBIS parts reset the simulator).
- **`sim.run { analysis, options, overrides }`** runs one analysis. `overrides` are at most 64 tuner values `{ ref, value }` (a designator of one word, at most 64 characters, none twice in any case; `value` a SPICE number), each applied as KiCad's tuner applies one (an `alter` of the part's tuner parameter; a part with none is a `warning` problem); an FFT takes none. `busy` while a run is live: one run at a time. The island numbers the run (1, 2, ... in the frame's life), gives it a fresh folder `/tmp/cc-sim/<run>/` (what ended runs left there removed first) and queues it in the engine (`not_applied` when the engine refuses it). The engine reads the circuit (an error problem ends the run `circuit`), makes the simulator on the first run of its life (the service loads there), drops the plots of the same analysis kind (a transient an FFT reads stays until the next transient), attaches the netlist, applies the overrides and starts ngspice's background run, writing `accepted.json` once ngspice took it. The answer is `{ run, problems }` then, the run going on in the service's own thread: the editor is not held, and edits made meanwhile leave the result marked with the undo depth it read (`SimResult.depth`). A run that ends before it was accepted answers its record's error as the code (`circuit`, `no_directive`, `no_simulation`, `sim_failed`, `stopped`, `service_failed`), `message` its first error problem's text, else the record's message; the problems of a `circuit` run are `sim.netlist`'s. `not_applied` when neither the acceptance nor the record came within about 60 s; the island then stops the run, so its record still ends it. Every run that passed the checks ends with exactly one `ev.sim` end, the refused ones too. On its idle the engine reads every plot the run made, each vector's name, KiCad's signal for it and its trace kind, and an operating point's values, which it hands to the schematic as KiCad's own frame does (`SCHEMATIC::SetOperatingPoint`), then writes `result.json` and its record `.cc-sim.json` last; the island reads both with no time limit (a long transient runs until it ends or Stop), sends the end event and removes the folder.
- **`sim.result { run }`** answers an ended run's `SimResult`: exactly `{ run, kind ("op", "dc", "ac", "tran", "noise", "pz", "sp", "fft"), ended ("done", "stopped", "failed"), depth, plots: [{ name, x (the plot's scale vector, null for an operating point or pole-zero), vectors: [{ name, signal, trace, complex, length }] }], op ([{ signal, trace ("voltage", "current", "power"), value }] for an operating point, else null), console (the run's last 2,000 lines of KiCad's and ngspice's own output), problems }`. `name` is ngspice's (`/out`, `time`, `v1#branch`), `signal` KiCad's (`V(/out)`, `I(v1)`), `trace` one of `voltage`, `current`, `power`, `gain`, `phase`, `noise`, `event`, `other` (a plot's scale is `other`). Only the latest run of each analysis kind is held, by the service and the island: an earlier one answers `no_simulation`, so does a run of the frame's previous document, and `busy` the run still live.
- **`sim.vectors { run, names }`** reads 1 to 32 of an ended run's vectors (named as `sim.result` names them, none twice, each in the first plot of the run holding it; another name answers `bad_args`) straight from the service, never through the editor's heap, as `<plot>.<name>`: each `real` a fresh `Float64Array` (the values, or each complex value's real half) and `imag` the imaginary halves of a complex vector, else null, every buffer transferred. More than 256 MiB of numbers together answers `island_error`. `no_simulation` for a run no longer held, or one whose service was released or died since; `busy` while a run is live.
- **`sim.stop`** asks the engine to halt the live run (ngspice's `bg_halt`); its record then says `stopped` and the end event follows. Answered `{}` at once, outside the queue, also with no run live (nothing to stop).
- **`sim.opOnSheet { voltages, currents }`** turns KiCad's own operating point labels on the drawing on or off (the eeschema appearance settings `show_op_voltages`, `show_op_currents`), drawn from the last operating point's values: no undo step, no modified state. `not_applied` when the engine refuses it or its apply queue does not empty within about 5 s.
- **`sim.release`** ends the service's worker, outside the queue: `{}` (also when no worker is booted), `busy` while a run is live. The next run boots a fresh worker (its files from the browser's cache) and every held result's vectors are gone (`sim.vectors` answers `no_simulation`; `sim.result` still answers). A host with one engine releases the service when its Simulate page closes.
- **A dead service.** A worker that dies (a hard ngspice fault: wasm has no recovery from one) settles the engine's waiting requests with an error, the live run ends `failed` with `service_failed`, and the next run boots a fresh worker. When the engine writes no record within about 5 s of the death, the island stops the run itself.
- **The held Simulator.** KiCad's Inspect > Simulator, its toolbar button and its key reach the frame through our engine (kicad `cc/main` K-SIM, in `SCH_INSPECTION_TOOL`, before the Simulator window is made), and the frame holds `eeschema.EditorControl.showSimulator` under `sim` (`ev.held`): KiCad's Simulator window, and with it its probe and tune tools, never opens where the island takes the action. Its model dialog is reached only through that window or a symbol's properties, which the island holds already.
- **Why engine exports (2026-11-16).** The engine compiled KiCad's whole simulator but reached it only through KiCad's Simulator window, which the island never shows, and PCBJam's provider had been removed from the island with the service's files. The exports read and write models and run the analyses with KiCad's own classes, the island writes the analysis line itself from fixed fields, and the vectors go from the service to the host directly.
- Every analysis from the island's lines, measured on `sim-rc` (V1 given an AC magnitude of 1 by `sim.model.set`): AC, 61 points from 1 Hz to 1 MHz, `|V(/out)|` 1.0000 at 1 Hz and 0.0795 at 1,995 Hz; noise, the plots `noise1` (spectra) and `noise2` (totals), every vector `noise`; pole-zero, one pole at minus 1,000 per second (minus 1/RC); an FFT of the transient with the Hanning window, 4,097 points at 199.96 Hz; S-parameters on a circuit with no RF port end `failed` `sim_failed` (ngspice needs KiCad's port sources: not measured with one).
- Measured on the local pair with engine run 14 built locally (2026-11-17, `tests/e2e/sim.spec.ts`, `sim-model.spec.ts` and `sim-held.spec.ts`, Playwright's headless Chromium, then Firefox; not the GPU window): the `sim-rc` RC step read `v(out)` 3.160602 V at 1 ms and 4.966310 V at 5 ms and crossed 63.2 percent at 0.999999 ms (5,023 points), the references to the digits shown; the `sim-divider` operating point read `V(/mid)` 3.836734693877551 V and `I(v1)` minus 0.8163265306122449 mA. The first run of a frame (the service's files from the local server) answered 155 and 154 ms after the request and ended 257 and 254 ms after it; the service booted, `sim-rc` ran to `done` in 107 and 102 ms (medians of 5) and `sim-divider` in 61 and 52 ms. Stop 200 ms into a 20 s transient ended it `stopped` 59 and 191 ms after the press. A 0.5 s transient of 1 us steps (500,832 rows) sent `running` with a percent about every 250 ms (3, 5, 11, ... 96) and ended in 5.9 s (Chromium); a run stopped within its first 200 ms sent no percent. A worker ended by hand mid run ended it `service_failed`, and the next run was `done` in 101 ms (Chromium only: Playwright's Firefox does not reach the island frame's workers). After `sim.release` the next run loaded the service again and was `done` in 170 and 153 ms. `sim.model.set` answered in 22 to 35 ms with one undo step and one `ev.edited`. Inspect > Simulator sent `ev.held` and opened no window (Chromium only: Playwright's Firefox does not reach the island frame's menu). In the GPU window (Chrome 157 over CDP, the local pair, a release id of its own so no earlier engine was cached): the first run of a frame, the service's files never fetched before, answered 119 ms after the request and was `done` 179 ms after it (one `loading` at 8 percent before `running`); with the service in the browser's cache a new page's first run was `done` in 128 ms; booted, `sim-rc` ran to `done` in 111 ms and `sim-divider` in 57 ms (medians of 5); Stop 200 ms into a 20 s transient ended it `stopped` 67 ms after the press; ten runs in a row left the frame's memory as `performance.measureUserAgentSpecificMemory` counts it (workers and reserved wasm memories included) unchanged, 9,613 MiB before and after; after `sim.release` the next run was `done` in 102 ms. On the wire the service is 1,895,808 bytes of wasm and 31,304 of script, gzip level 9 as the build writes them (raw 5,666,939 and 117,051).
- **The run guard** (island `v0.2.3-cc25`). Every `ev.sim` is keyed by its run number: a `loading` or `running` event goes out only for the run live at that moment, and an end only while no other run is live, once per run, never for a run older than one whose end went out (the numbers only grow). Anything else is dropped, so nothing of run N can move or end run N + 1, whatever reaches the island late. Two runs started back to back each end once with their own number (`tests/e2e/lo/sim-faults.spec.ts`). <!-- ext:lo-e2-i -->
- **The problems of a fault** reach the host through the codes above: with engine run 15 a part with no simulation model is a `no_model` problem naming it (Glasgow: U1 among them), and a model file the project does not hold (`Sim.Library`) is a `file_missing` problem naming the part and the path, with no KiCad box: the next op is answered at once. <!-- ext:lo-e2-i -->
- **Pole-zero and S-parameters, measured with a real result** (engine run 14, 2026-11-18): pole-zero on `sim-rc` (V1 given an AC magnitude of 1) answers one plot with one complex vector `pole(1)`, minus 1,000 + 0j per second, minus 1/RC; without an AC magnitude on the input source ngspice answers "The input signal is shorted on the way to the output" and the run ends `failed`. S-parameters on the island's `sim-sparam` fixture (a 100 nH and 40 pF low-pass between two port sources, `portnum` and `z0` 50 in `Sim.Params`) answer the `S_i_j`, `Y_i_j` and `Z_i_j` vectors of both ports, complex, against `frequency`: `|S21|` 1.0000 at 1 MHz and 0.0127 at 1 GHz. <!-- ext:lo-e2-i -->
- **A Stop the service cannot carry out** (island `v0.2.3-cc25`, docs/si1-stop-diagnosis.md). A Stop reaches ngspice as a request to the service's worker, and the engine then waits for ngspice's thread to end. Measured on engine run 14 with 1 ns steps over 200 ms or 2 s, the run then never ended: ngspice's output storage grew to the service's 4 GiB memory within about 2 s (at 4 to 6 percent), and either the worker answered nothing more, the halt included, or ngspice's allocation failed ("malloc: can't allocate"), its thread ended "awaiting to be reset" and no record came. So after a Stop, while the run is still live, the island reads the worker every 300 ms: while ngspice runs it is left to end the run; a worker that does not answer within 250 ms, or whose ngspice runs nothing while the engine is idle twice in a row, is ended (as `sim.release` ends it, which settles what the engine waits on) and the engine is asked to stop again: it finds no run in the fresh service and finishes the run. Measured with 1 ns over 2 s stopped at 6 percent: the end 555 ms after the press, and the next run `done` 204 ms after its request on a fresh worker (`tests/e2e/lo/sim-faults.spec.ts`). The end's phase is the engine's record: on engine run 14 it read `done` (the finish was queued when ngspice's thread ended on its allocation failure); engine run 15 ends such a run `stopped` or `failed`. The ended worker's vectors go with it, and the next run boots a fresh worker. <!-- ext:lo-e2-i -->
- **The run guard** (island `v0.2.3-cc25`): the answer is the asked run's own result; one of another run answers `no_simulation`, as a run no longer held does. <!-- ext:lo-e2-i -->
- `sim.vectors` also reads the vectors `sim.userSignals` made for that run (`user0` to `user15`, the names its answer gave, from the plot they were made in), mixed in any order with the run's own names; a `user<i>` the latest `sim.userSignals` of that run did not make answers `bad_args` as any other unknown name, and one whose plot is gone `no_simulation` (island `v0.2.3-cc25`). <!-- ext:m11-i -->
- **A compiled model** (island `v0.2.3-cc25`). The simulator service is ngspice built for the browser: it loads no OSDI device, no XSPICE code model and no shared object. So the island reads the netlist and every file it includes that the frame's file system holds (each to at most 4 MiB, at most 64 files, a nested include read from its own file's folder) and lists one problem `{ code: "binary_model", severity: "error", ref?, path, text }` after KiCad's own for each line that names a compiled model: a line starting `pre_osdi` or `codemodel`, a `.model` of type `d_cosim` or an XSPICE instance (an `A` line) whose model, its last word, is `d_cosim`, or a `.model`, `.include`, `.inc` or `.lib` line naming a file ending `.osdi`, `.cm`, `.va`, `.vams`, `.so`, `.dll` or `.dylib`. Only those lines' words are read: a net or node name such as `/bus.va` is never taken for a file, and an instance's parameters load nothing. `path` is the compiled file the line names (relative to the project folder when inside it), else the file the line was read in; `ref` is the part's designator on an instance line; `text` is the line, continuations joined. `binary_model` joins the problem codes above for these answers only; KiCad's own problems never carry it. <!-- ext:si1-i -->
- **A compiled model refused before ngspice runs** (island `v0.2.3-cc25`). Before the run is queued the island reads the netlist with the run's options and scans it as `sim.netlist` does; any `binary_model` problem answers `circuit` with the first one's line as `message`, and nothing runs: no run is numbered and no `ev.sim` is sent. `sim.netlist` lists the problems. An FFT reruns no circuit and is not scanned. Measured on the island's `sim-binary` fixture (`tests/e2e/sim-binary.spec.ts`). <!-- ext:si1-i -->

### keys.claim
Claims Alt chords for the host (island `v0.2.3-cc17`, the `hostKeys` cap): while the reader's keyboard focus is inside the frame the host page sees no key, so the frame hands it the chords it claimed as `ev.key` (below). Each chord means Alt held with the key `code` names, Ctrl and Meta not held; `shift: true` means with Shift, `shift` absent or `false` without it. `code` is a `KeyboardEvent.code`: `KeyA` to `KeyZ`, `Digit0` to `Digit9`, `Slash`, `Period`, `Comma`, `Semicolon`, `Quote`, `BracketLeft`, `BracketRight`, `Backslash`, `Minus`, `Equal` or `Backquote` (the physical key, whatever the layout types). A claim replaces the whole claimed set: `{ chords: [] }` claims nothing, and a host that never claims (an older host) gets every key handled exactly as before. At most 64 chords; an unknown field, a code outside the list, a `shift` that is not a boolean, a `chords` that is not an array or one over 64 answers `bad_args` and the set claimed before stays.

The frame never takes KiCad's own Alt chords, its default hotkeys in either editor (`DefaultHotkey( MD_ALT + ... )` in KiCad 10's `common/tool/actions.cpp`, `eeschema/tools/sch_actions.cpp`, `pcbnew/tools/pcb_actions.cpp` and `pcbnew/router/router_tool.cpp`): Alt with `KeyS`, `KeyW`, `KeyZ`, `Digit1`, `Digit2`, `Digit3`, `Digit4` or `Backquote` without Shift, and Alt with Shift and `Comma` (KiCad's Alt+<) or `KeyV`. Such a chord is left out of the set and answered back in `refused`, each once, in the order asked, as `{ code, shift }` with `shift` always a boolean; `claimed` counts the distinct chords taken. It is answered at once, before the engine is up too, and the set holds until the next claim or the frame's end.

`observe` (the island of engine build T17, the `observeKeys` cap) names keys the host hears WITHOUT taking them from KiCad: a closed list, `Escape` only (each once; any other entry, or an `observe` that is not an array, answers `bad_args`). A plain press of an observed key (a `keydown` with no Alt, Shift, Ctrl or Meta held, no auto-repeat) is sent once as `ev.key { code, shift: false, observed: true }` and is NOT canceled, so KiCad still gets it (Escape still cancels KiCad's own tool; claiming it away would stop that). A claim replaces the observed keys too: a claim without `observe` observes nothing. The answer is unchanged.


### shutdown
Releases the engine before the host removes the frame: the document is dropped (no `ev.saved` from here on, and the leave prompt stays quiet), every parked engine activation is unwound, the engine's threads are stopped, its WebGL contexts are released and its globals and window are cleared. The answer `{}` is the last message on the port: the frame then closes the port, answers nothing else (requests sent behind the shutdown included) and emits nothing, `ev.closing` included. The editor is not usable afterwards; the host removes the frame (or reloads it to start again). If the teardown fails the answer is `island_error`, and the port closes all the same. A shutdown sent before `ev.ready` stops the engine at its first park; the host still removes the frame. The host waits for the answer with a timeout (5 s, say) and removes the frame when it expires: in a background tab the browser may throttle the teardown's timers far past that, and the removal's `pagehide` covers the release.

Without the op, removing or navigating the frame runs a shorter teardown from `pagehide`: every parked engine activation is refused and unwinds before the document goes, and the rest (the threads, WebGL, the globals and the window) is left for the browser to release with the document.

<!-- ext sections: generated by scripts/ext/gen.mjs from bridge/ext/<unit>.md; never edit between these lines -->

### The 3D service

KiCad's 3D exports other than VRML (STEP, STEPZ, BREP, XAO, GLB, PLY, STL, U3D, PDF) and its reading of a part's STEP or IGES body run on OpenCASCADE, which the editor's wasm does not carry. The island serves it as a program of its own, `occ_service.wasm` with its script `occ_service.js`, beside the engine files, and runs it in a worker of the `pcb` frame (island `v0.2.3-cc25`, on engine run 15).

- **Cap `occ`**: listed in `ev.ready` `caps` of a `pcb` frame when this island id serves both service files (PIN.json records them, and the build serves both or neither) and the frame installed the service as `globalThis.occService`. Never listed in a `sch` frame. Without `occ` a host keeps the 3D rows other than VRML held.
- **Lazy**: installing the service fetches nothing. The first export or STEP body the engine asks for fetches the wasm (the browser's compiled code cache is used), compiles it and boots the worker; later requests reuse it.
- **Exports**: the engine's request carries the absolute path it reads the file back from; the service gathers the parts' bodies through the island's staging (each body by its path relative to the model root, `kiprjmod/<relative path>` or `<Lib>.3dshapes/<Name>.<ext>`: the service stages no other path), runs the export in the worker and writes the bytes at that path in the engine's file system. Nothing is handed to the browser. A request that names no path is answered "The 3D service was given no file to write."; one that gives no bytes, "The 3D service made no file." (engine run 15 also fails an answer that leaves no file or an empty one with that line).
- **Report**: the answer's `report` is the one road KiCad's exporter's lines take ("Could not add 3D model for R1. File not found: ..."), joined with the staging's own line. The engine reports it as one error line when the export failed (it then reads in `export_failed`'s message) and as one info line when it succeeded, which `project.export`'s `notes` do not carry on engine run 15.
- **Release**: the worker is ended 60 s after its last answer, and at once when the bridge closes; the next request boots a new one.
- **Death**: a worker that ends with requests waiting (a fault, or a worker that closed itself) answers every one `{ ok: false, report: "The 3D service stopped." }`, which the engine reports as the export's failure (`export_failed` through `project.export`); the next request boots a new worker.

### board.models, board.setModels and board.embedModels

The board's 3D model lines, for the host's Models window (island `v0.2.3-cc25`, on an engine with `kicadBoardModels`, `kicadBoardSetModels` and `kicadBoardEmbedModels`, engine run 15; a host gates them on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `boardModels`, listed by a `pcb` frame only). On an older engine the cap is absent and each op answers `unsupported` naming the export. A `sch` frame answers `unsupported`.

`ModelLine` is `{ path, offset: [x, y, z], rotate: [x, y, z], scale: [x, y, z], opacity, show, found }`: `path` as the board writes it (1 to 1,024 characters, no control character), the vectors three finite numbers, `opacity` 0 to 1, `show` a boolean, and `found` where the engine finds the body at that moment: `embedded` (a `kicad-embed://` name the footprint or the board holds), `project` (a file of the project folder), `staged` (a body the engine fetched under `/pcbjam/3dmodels/`) or `missing`.

- `board.models` answers the engine's read as it is: every footprint with its uuid, its reference, its lines in order and its kinds (`notInPos` and `dnp` for the board's current variant), and the board's own embedded files (`type` KiCad's word: `model`, `font`, `worksheet`, `datasheet` or `other`; `bytes` decompressed). It is answered during a `board.check`.
- `board.setModels` replaces the lines of 1 to 4,096 footprints (no footprint twice, uuids lowercase, at most 64 lines each, every key of a line given and no other, `found` left out) as ONE undo step pushed under `label`. The answer comes once the apply queue is empty and every footprint's lines read back as sent: `changed` counts the footprints whose lines the request changed. An `ev.edited` follows. `not_found` names the first uuid that is no footprint's; nothing is changed then.
- `board.embedModels` keeps the bodies of 1 to 4,096 lines (`index` 0 to 63 in its footprint's lines, no line twice) inside the board, as KiCad's Embed 3D models does. The island reads each body itself before anything is written: a `${KICAD*_3DMODEL_DIR}` line through the model store (a VRML line's STEP twin), a `${KIPRJMOD}` or relative line from the project folder, a VRML line with its STEP sibling (`.step` or `.stp`) kept beside it, or the sibling alone when the VRML file is absent. The bodies go into one fresh `/tmp/cc-embed/<n>/`, each under its own file name (a second body of the same name takes `-2`, `-3` before its extension), and the engine keeps them as ONE commit "Keep models inside the board", each line then naming its `kicad-embed://` file. The answer, once the apply queue is empty: `embedded` the files kept, `names` their names (sorted, distinct), `bytes` their size. The folder is removed after every answer. `bad_args` names a line that is not on the board, already inside it, or whose body the island cannot read (a library line the model store does not hold, a project file the folder lacks; a line's `found` `missing` alone refuses nothing, since the engine reads every library line missing until it has fetched the body) (`message` "<reference> <path>", or "<uuid> line <index>"); `too_large` names a body past 25 MiB, or the op when the bodies pass 512 MiB together; nothing is written then. `not_applied` when the engine refuses the embedding or its apply queue does not empty within 30 s.
- The checks, in order, for each: the args (`bad_args`), the frame, the engine (`not_ready`, `unsupported`), a board (`not_ready`), busy as for a board op (and `busy` while the engine cannot read the board's models).
- Held in a `pcb` frame under `boardModels`, each sending `ev.held` and opening nothing: KiCad's Update 3D model paths (`pcbnew.GlobalEdit.migrate3DModels`) and Embed 3D models (`pcbnew.EditorControl.collect3DModels`); the host's Models window opens in their place.

### The drawing sheet editor: a ds frame and the dsedit ops

Island `v0.2.3-cc25`, on an engine with the dsedit exports (engine run 15); a host gates every op on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `dsedit`.

- **The frame.** `?frame=ds` boots KiCad's own drawing sheet editor (`PL_EDITOR_FRAME`, the tool `pl_editor` in the merged editor image), canvas only. A `ds` frame holds no document: every op that ships answers `unsupported` ("a drawing sheet editor opens drawing sheets, not documents") but `key.press`, `keys.claim`, `chrome.show`, `view.fit`, `select` and `shutdown`. In every other frame each `dsedit` op answers `unsupported` ("a drawing sheet edits in a ds frame"). A `ds` frame's `ev.ready` lists only `dsedit` (every export below present), `heldKeys`, `hostKeys` and the engine's export names; no other frame lists `dsedit`. `ev.edited` in a `ds` frame carries the sheet's undo depth while a sheet is open (an open starts the count again and sends none); `ev.selected` carries the sheet's selected items (`fpPaths` `[]`), while a sheet is open.
- **The checks**, in order, every one before any effect: the frame's kind (`unsupported`); the args (`bad_args`); the engine (`not_ready` before it is up; `unsupported` naming the first missing export of `kicadDsEditOpen`, `kicadDsEditState`, `kicadDsEditTake`, `kicadDsEditMarkSaved`, `kicadDsEditItemsGet`, `kicadDsEditItemsSet`, `kicadDsEditItemsAdd`, `kicadDsEditItemsRemove`, `kicadDsEditDefaultsGet`, `kicadDsEditDefaultsSet`, `kicadDsEditPreviewGet`, `kicadDsEditPreviewSet`, `kicadDsEditShow`, `kicadDsEditSelection`, `kicadDsEditSelect`, `kicadDsEditAppend`, `kicadDsEditArmImage`, `kicadCollabBusy`); a load parked, a popup menu or a dialog up (`busy`, nothing changed); then `no_sheet` for every op but `dsedit.open` and `dsedit.state` while no sheet is open. A change answers once the engine's apply queue is empty and the state shows it, else `not_applied` after about 5 s.
  - `DsEditState`: exactly `{ token, open, name, modified, depth (the undo depth), page (1: page 1, 2: the other pages), values (true: the title block filled in; false: its names as typed), items (the sheet's item count) }`. `token` is the island's own for the last open (1 to 64 printable characters, `""` before any), which `dsedit.markSaved` names; `name` is the sheet's path in the design (`""` before any open).
- **`dsedit.open`** opens a drawing sheet: `source` `text` (then `text`, a `kicad_wks` or a legacy `page_layout` document, which KiCad's own parser reads into a scratch model first: `unreadable` with KiCad's line and nothing changed when it cannot), `default` (KiCad's built in sheet, the one every new design draws) or `empty` (KiCad's own empty sheet, which keeps one zero length line); `name` 1 to 1,024 characters with no control character (the frame's title only: nothing is ever written there); `discard` a boolean (`held_edits` when the open sheet holds changes and it is false). Past 8 MiB of text: `too_large`. The undo list starts empty, the sheet unmodified, on page 1 with its values shown. The answer is the state once the engine shows the open, with a fresh token.
- **`dsedit.state`** answers the state, a sheet open or not. **`dsedit.take`** answers the open sheet as a `kicad_wks` document by KiCad's own formatter, `{ name, text }`, with no `(uuid ...)` token (kicad-cli 10.0 refuses one): item uuids hold for the life of one open, so a take opened again gives the same items, order and properties under fresh uuids. A read: nothing changes. **`dsedit.markSaved { token }`** clears the modified flag once the host has written what it took (`bad_args` when `token` is not the state's; no undo step).
- **`dsedit.show { page, values }`** shows page 1 or the other pages, and the title block's values or its names, then redraws: no undo step, not a modification. The answer is the state once it shows both.
- **Held.** In a `ds` frame, under `dsedit`, while a sheet is open, these actions send `ev.held { action }` and open no window of KiCad's: `common.Control.new`, `common.Control.open`, `common.Control.save`, `common.Control.saveAs`, `common.Control.print`, `common.Control.plot`, `common.Control.quit`, `plEditor.EditorControl.ShowInspector`, `plEditor.EditorControl.PreviewSettings`, `plEditor.InteractiveEdit.appendWorksheet`, `common.SuiteControl.openPreferences` and `common.SuiteControl.listHotKeys` (the last two: nothing of the host's replaces them).
- **`shutdown`** in a `ds` frame never asks KiCad's close question: the host decides Save, Discard or Stay before it.

### The drawing sheet editor: items, defaults, preview, append and pictures

Island `v0.2.3-cc25`, on an engine with the dsedit exports (engine run 15); a host gates every op on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `dsedit`, and `ev.dsedit` on `dseditHeld` (the engine's marker `kicadHostDsEdit` beside every dsedit export). Every op makes the checks of the `dsedit` ops above in their order (`unsupported` outside a `ds` frame, `bad_args`, `not_ready`, `unsupported` naming the first missing export, `busy`, `no_sheet`). A change is ONE undo step of KiCad's, then an `ev.edited`; its answer is the read back once the engine's apply queue is empty and the sheet shows every key it set, else `not_applied` after about 5 s. Lengths and positions are millimeters as the sheet stores them, angles degrees; uuids lower case 8-4-4-4-12; strings at most 1,024 characters unless named.

- **Records.** `DsItem` is one of, each with exactly its keys: common `{ kind, uuid, name, comment, pages ("all" | "page1only" | "notonpage1"), start: DsPoint, repeat, step: [incrx, incry] }`, `DsPoint` `{ x, y, corner ("rbcorner" | "rtcorner" | "lbcorner" | "ltcorner") }` measured from that corner of the page's margins; a `line` or `rect` adds `{ end: DsPoint, lineWidth (0: the sheet's default) }`; a `text` adds `{ text (at most 4,096, names such as ${TITLE} kept as typed), labelStep, font ("" KiCad's own), bold, italic, color ("" or #rrggbbaa), hJustify ("left" | "center" | "right"), vJustify ("top" | "center" | "bottom"), rotation, size: [w, h] (0: the default), maxLength, maxHeight (0: none), lineWidth }`; a `polygon` adds `{ rotation, lineWidth, outlines, points }` (its shape is read only); a `bitmap` adds `{ ppi, width, height }` (pixels). `DsDefaults` is `{ lineWidth, textSize: [w, h], textThickness, margins: { left, right, top, bottom } }`. `DsPreview` is `{ paper (KiCad's name: A5 to A0, A to E, GERBER, User, USLetter, USLegal, USLedger), portrait, width, height, title, date, revision, company, comments (exactly 9) }`: the page and title block the sheet is previewed on, never written into the sheet.
- **`dsedit.items.get`** answers the open sheet's items in KiCad's draw order; with `uuids` (1 to 4,096) only those (an id the sheet does not hold is left out), with `kinds` (1 to 5 of `line`, `rect`, `text`, `polygon`, `bitmap`) only those kinds.
- **`dsedit.items.set`** sets `set`, a non empty partial of one kind's editable keys (never `kind`, `uuid`, a polygon's `outlines` or `points`, a bitmap's `width` or `height`), on every item of `uuids` (1 to 512), in KiCad's properties pane order: `bad_args` when an id is not on the sheet or an item's kind lacks a key. Bounds, as KiCad's pane: positions and steps within 10,000; `repeat` 1 to 500; `lineWidth` 0 to 10; `labelStep` within 100,000; `rotation` within 360; `size` 0 to 100 each; `maxLength`, `maxHeight` 0 to 10,000; `ppi` 1 to 100,000; a `text` may hold line breaks, no other control character anywhere. A `font` named by its face is resolved by KiCad's font lookup, so the read back shows KiCad's face for it.
- **`dsedit.items.add`** adds 1 to 64 whole items: a `line` or `rect` (every key of its record), a `text` (every key), or a `bitmap`'s common keys with `ppi` and `png` (base64 of a PNG of at most 4 MiB, which KiCad's picture reader must take); each `uuid` the host makes, never one the sheet holds (`bad_args`). A polygon is never added (KiCad has no polygon tool; polygons arrive by `dsedit.append`).
- **`dsedit.items.remove`** takes the items of `uuids` (1 to 4,096) off the sheet with KiCad's own delete (`bad_args` when an id is not on the sheet; `not_applied` while KiCad is placing one of them) and answers `{ removed }`.
- **`dsedit.defaults.set`**: `lineWidth` 0 to 10, `textSize` 0.01 to 100 each, `textThickness` 0 to 5, `margins` whole, each 0 to 1,000.
- **`dsedit.preview.set`**: `paper` one of the names above; `width` and `height` together (25.4 to 3,048) and only for a `User` paper; `portrait` only for the other papers (the paper the set names, else the one shown: `bad_args` otherwise); the four texts; `comments` exactly nine. The drawing redraws on that page.
- **`dsedit.append { text }`** adds every item of another `kicad_wks` (or legacy `page_layout`) document to the open sheet, its setup taken too, as KiCad's Append Existing Drawing Sheet: KiCad's own parser reads it first (`unreadable` with KiCad's line, nothing changed); past 8 MiB `too_large`. The answer `{ added }` lists the new items' uuids (an id the sheet already holds is replaced by a fresh one) once the sheet holds every one.
- **`dsedit.image.arm { bytes, name }`** holds a PNG (at most 4 MiB: `too_large`; KiCad's picture reader must take it, else `not_applied`) for KiCad's next Place Bitmaps press, which puts it on the pointer with no file dialog and disarms (the press after that places it, ONE undo step; a tool started by its key takes the start as its first press); empty `bytes` disarm. With nothing armed the press sends `ev.dsedit` and places nothing.

### The footprint editor: an fpedit frame and the fpedit ops

Island `v0.2.3-cc25`, on an engine with the fpedit exports (engine run 15); a host gates every op on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `fpedit`.

- **The frame.** `?frame=fpedit` boots KiCad's own footprint editor (`FOOTPRINT_EDIT_FRAME`, a frame of `pcbnew`), canvas only, with no library tree loaded and no picker warm up, in the viewer's palette (its own settings file `fpedit.json` is seeded with the viewer's appearance as `pcbnew.json` is: KiCad's footprint editor reads its color theme there, and unseeded draws KiCad's default footprint background). An `fpedit` frame holds no document of the project's: every op that ships answers `unsupported` ("a footprint editor opens footprints, not documents") but `project.write`, `project.libs`, `lib.index`, `lib.item`, `lib.prefetch`, `key.press`, `keys.claim`, `chrome.show`, `view.fit`, `select` and `shutdown` (KiCad's footprint layers come from the footprint's own record, not `layers.*`). In every other frame each `fpedit` op answers `unsupported` ("a footprint edits in an fpedit frame"). Of the built in caps an `fpedit` frame's `ev.ready` lists only `heldKeys`, `hostKeys`, `write` and `projectLibs`, with `fpedit` (every export below present), the caps the other units list there (`fpeditHeld`, `footprintConvert`, `importGraphics`, `contextMenu` and others) and the engine's export names; no other frame lists `fpedit`. `ev.edited` in an `fpedit` frame carries the footprint's undo depth (`kicadFpEditState().depth`) while a footprint is open (an open or a new starts the count again and sends none; an edit made right after it, before the next read, is still sent); `ev.selected` carries the footprint's selected items (`kicadFpEditSelection()`, `fpPaths` `[]`) while a footprint is open.
- **The checks**, in order, every one before any effect: the args (`bad_args`); the frame's kind (`unsupported`); the engine (`not_ready` before it is up; `unsupported` naming the first missing export of `kicadFpEditOpen`, `kicadFpEditNew`, `kicadFpEditState`, `kicadFpEditTake`, `kicadFpEditMarkSaved`, `kicadFpEditItemsGet`, `kicadFpEditItemsSet`, `kicadFpEditItemsAdd`, `kicadFpEditItemsRemove`, `kicadFpEditFootprintGet`, `kicadFpEditFootprintSet`, `kicadFpEditPadDefaultsGet`, `kicadFpEditPadDefaultsSet`, `kicadFpEditEnumerate`, `kicadFpEditSelection`, `kicadFpEditSelect`, `kicadFpEditCheck`, `kicadFpEditCleanup`, `kicadFpEditEmbed`, `kicadFpEditPlotSvg`, `kicadCollabBusy`); a load parked, a popup menu or a dialog up (`busy`, nothing changed); then `no_footprint` for every op but `fpedit.open`, `fpedit.new` and `fpedit.state` while no footprint is open. An open or a new answers once the engine's apply queue is empty and the state shows it, else `not_applied` after about 5 s.
  - `FpEditState`: exactly `{ token, open, name, lib, mode, modified, depth (the undo depth), padCount, type, padEditMode }`. `token` is the island's own for the last open (1 to 64 printable characters, `""` before any), which `fpedit.markSaved` names; `name` the footprint's name (`""` with none open); `lib` the nickname it opened under (`""` for none, never a row of KiCad's table); `mode` the door it came through (`library`, `placed` or `new`); `type` KiCad's footprint type (`smd`, `through_hole` or `unspecified`); `padEditMode` KiCad's pad edit mode (a custom pad taken apart into its shapes).
- **`fpedit.open`** opens one footprint from its text (`text`, one `(footprint ...)` document, which KiCad's own parser reads; no library is loaded and no row of KiCad's table is named): `name` 1 to 1,024 characters and `lib` 0 to 1,024, with no control character, the names it opens under; `mode` `library` (a footprint of the design's own set), `placed` (a placed part's copy, from `footprint.forEditor`: its text kept as the board sent it) or `new`; `discard` a boolean (`held_edits` when the open footprint holds changes and it is false, nothing changed). The footprint lands at the origin, its Reference and Value filled with KiCad's placeholders when empty (but `placed`), the undo list empty, unmodified, the view fitted. Past 8 MiB of text: `too_large`. `unreadable` ("fpedit.open: the text holds no footprint KiCad reads") when KiCad's parser reads no footprint from the text, nothing changed. The answer is the state once the engine shows the open, with a fresh token.
- **`fpedit.new`** is KiCad's new footprint with no library (the host keeps names unique): `name` and `lib` as above, `type` `smd`, `through_hole` or `unspecified`, `discard` as above; then as `fpedit.open`, `mode` `new`. `bad_args` "fpedit.new: KiCad refused the new footprint" when the engine refuses it.
- **`fpedit.state`** answers the state, a footprint open or not. **`fpedit.take`** answers the open footprint as library text by KiCad's own formatter, from a copy reset as KiCad's own library save resets it (every child keeping its uuid, embedded files kept), `{ name, text }`, `text` one `(footprint ...)` of at most 8 MiB. A read: nothing changes; open, take, open, take gives the same text. **`fpedit.markSaved { token }`** clears the modified flag once the host has written what it took (`bad_args` "fpedit.markSaved: the token is not the open footprint's" when `token` is not the state's; no undo step).
- **`shutdown`** in an `fpedit` frame never asks KiCad's close question: the host decides Save, Discard or Stay before it.

### The footprint editor's items: fpedit.items.get, set, add, remove

Island `v0.2.3-cc25`, in an `fpedit` frame, on an engine with `kicadFpEditItemsGet`, `kicadFpEditItemsSet`, `kicadFpEditItemsAdd`, `kicadFpEditItemsRemove` and `kicadFpEditState` (engine run 15); a host gates them on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `fpedit`. In every other frame each answers `unsupported` ("a footprint edits in an fpedit frame"); on an engine without the exports, `unsupported` naming the first missing one. The checks run in this order, each before any effect: the args (`bad_args`), the frame, the engine (`not_ready`, `unsupported`), a load parked or a popup menu or a dialog up (`busy`), a footprint open (`no_footprint`).

- **`fpedit.items.get`**: `uuids` 1 to 4,096, `kinds` 1 to 11, `layers` 1 to 256 KiCad layer names, none twice; every filter given must hold (an item is on a layer when its layer or one of its layers is listed). The footprint's own items in KiCad's order (fields, pads, then the drawings; a group or a table cell is never listed), each with its `kind` and its `uuid`. `not_found` names the first uuid the footprint does not hold. Answered during a check (a read).
- **`fpedit.items.set`**: `uuids` as above, every one held (`not_found` names the first), all of one kind (`bad_args`), and `set`, a non empty partial of the keys that kind takes (below; never `kind` or `uuid`), each in its type and bounds, and only keys every item has (a text inside a footprint has `keepUpright`, one outside a footprint `locked`; a picture's `grayscale` is an action, not a key it holds): `bad_args` names the key. A shape keeps its `shape`, a dimension its `dimType`, a zone its `ruleArea` (`bad_args` "a <kind> keeps its <key>"); a dimension's `textPos` needs its `textPosition` `manual` (a leader's always is). A set never holds `kind`, so a barcode's type is its key `symbology`. A pad's `copper`, each `layerShapes` entry's `copper` and its `teardrop` are partial; `primitives` and `layerShapes` replace the list; a `backdrill` or `postMachining` side is whole. KiCad's own dialog rules refuse what the dialog refuses (`bad_args`: a hole on an SMD pad, a trapezoid delta on both axes, a through hole pad with no hole, a padstack mode on an SMD pad, a field's name, a table's rows, columns, widths or heights other than they are, an empty text) and reset what the dialog resets (a circle's size squared and its offset dropped, a rounded rectangle's ratio, a type's default layers and hole). ONE undo step "Edit" over every item. The answer is the items read back once every key shows, or once the step shows when the dialog's rules reset a key given; past about 5 s `not_applied`.
- **`fpedit.items.add`**: 1 to 512 new items, each its `kind`, its uuid (the host's own, lower case, none the footprint holds: `bad_args` "<uuid> is taken"), the keys its kind needs and any other key a set of its kind takes: a pad nothing (it starts from the pad defaults, at the origin), a field `name` (one no field holds) and `layer` (its text may be empty), a text `text` and `layer`, a text box `text`, `layer` and `points`, a table `layer`, `colWidths` and `rowHeights` (1 to 64 each; `cells`, when given, rows by columns), a barcode `layer` (`symbology` optional, KiCad's QR otherwise), a shape `layer`, `shape` and `points` (`cornerRadius` on a rectangle only), a dimension `dimType`, `layer`, `start` and `end` (not the same point; the keys of its type only), a zone `layers` and `points` (always a rule area: `ruleArea`, when given, true), a picture `layer` and `png` (base64 of a PNG, at most 4 MiB decoded: a JPEG is converted first), a point `layer`. ONE undo step "Add"; the answer is the new items read back.
- **`fpedit.items.remove`**: 1 to 4,096 uuids, every one held (`not_found`), never the Reference or the Value field (`bad_args`); ONE undo step "Delete"; the answer once none reads back.

The records (lengths in mm, angles in degrees, positions relative to the footprint's origin, a string at most 1,024 characters unless named, a layer KiCad's canonical name; any other shape is `island_error`):

- `pad`: `number`, `pinName`, `pinType`, `type` (`smd`, `th`, `npth`, `conn`, `aperture`), `x`, `y`, `angle`, `locked`, `padstack` (`normal`, `front_inner_back`, `custom`), `copper` (PadShape, the front or only shape), `layerShapes` (`[{ layer, copper }]`, every unique layer but F.Cu, empty while normal), `drill` (`{ shape: "circle" | "oval", size: [w, h] }` or null), `layers`, `dieLength`, `dieDelay` (ps, 0 to 2,000), `clearance`, `maskMargin`, `pasteMargin` (each a number or null), `pasteRatio` (-1 to 1 or null), `zoneConnection` (`inherit`, `none`, `thermal`, `solid`), `customZoneShape` (`outline`, `convexhull`), `thermalSpokeWidth`, `thermalGap` (each or null), `thermalAngle`, `property` (`none`, `bga`, `fiducial_global`, `fiducial_local`, `testpoint`, `heatsink`, `castellated`, `mechanical`, `pressfit`), `unconnectedLayers` (`keep_all`, `remove_except_start_and_end`, `remove_all`), `teardrop` (`{ enabled, useTwoTracks, preferZoneConnection, maxLength, maxWidth, lengthRatio, widthRatio, curved, sizeFilterRatio }`), `backdrill` (`{ front, back }`, each `{ size, end }` with `end` a copper layer or null, or null), `postMachining` (`{ front, back }`, each `{ mode: "none" | "countersink" | "counterbore", size, depth, angle }`).
- PadShape: `shape` (`circle`, `rect`, `oval`, `trapezoid`, `roundrect`, `chamfered_rect`, `custom`), `size`, `offset`, `delta`, `roundRatio` and `chamferRatio` (0 to 0.5), `chamferCorners` (`tl`, `tr`, `bl`, `br`), `anchor` (`circle`, `rect`), `primitives` (a custom shape's only, at most 512, each `{ shape: "line" | "arc" | "rect" | "circle" | "polygon" | "bezier", points, width, filled }` in the pad's own coordinates: a line or a rectangle its two ends, an arc start, middle and end, a circle its center and a point on it, a curve its four controls, a polygon three points or more).
