- `field`: `name`, `text` (one line), `layer`, `x`, `y`, `angle`, `size` (`[w, h]`, 0.001 to 250), `thickness`, `autoThickness`, `bold`, `italic`, `mirrored`, `hJustify` (`left`, `center`, `right`), `vJustify` (`top`, `center`, `bottom`), `knockout`, `font` (KiCad's name; "KiCad Font" its stroke font), `visible`, `keepUpright`. `text`: a field's keys with `name` "" and no `visible`; inside a footprint one line and `keepUpright`, outside one (the board's) lines and `locked`.
- `textbox`: `text` (lines), `points` (a box square to the axes its two corners, settable; a turned box its four, read only), the text style keys of a field (`layer` to `font`), `locked`, `border`, `borderWidth`, `borderStyle` (`solid`, `dash`, `dot`, `dash_dot`, `dash_dot_dot`); after a set the box grows to its text as KiCad's dialog grows it.
- `table`: `x`, `y`, `layer`, `locked`, `cells` (rows of texts as KiCad's grid shows them, a covered cell null), `borderExternal`, `borderHeader`, `borderWidth` (null while both are off), `borderStyle`, `rowSeparators`, `colSeparators`, `separatorsWidth`, `separatorsStyle`, `rows`, `cols`, `colWidths`, `rowHeights` (1 to 64 each; read only on a set: given back equal, or refused).
- `barcode`: `text` (one line, at most 4,096 characters), `layer`, `locked`, `x`, `y`, `width`, `height`, `textSize`, `angle`, `knockout`, `margin` (`[x, y]`), `showText`, `symbology` (`code39`, `code128`, `datamatrix`, `qr`, `microqr`), `errorCorrection` (`L`, `M`, `Q`, `H`). A text that yields no symbol is refused.
- `shape`: `layer`, `shape` (`segment`, `rect`, `arc`, `circle`, `polygon`, `bezier`), `points` (the counts of a primitive's, a segment's two ends; a polygon its main outline), `width`, `style`, `filled` (`fill` as a yes or no; given beside `fill` it agrees), `fill` (`none`, `solid`, `hatch`, `reverse_hatch`, `cross_hatch`; a segment or an arc never filled), `locked`, `cornerRadius` (a rectangle's only, at most half its short side), `hasSolderMask`, `solderMaskMargin` (a length or null for the board's). A segment or an arc, or an unfilled closed shape, needs a width.
- `dimension`: `dimType` (`aligned`, `orthogonal`, `center`, `radial`, `leader`), `layer`, `start`, `end`, `height` and `extensionHeight` (aligned and orthogonal only), `orientation` (`horizontal`, `vertical`; orthogonal only), `leaderLength` (radial only), `textBorder` (`none`, `rectangle`, `circle`, `roundrect`; leader only), `override`, `value`, `prefix`, `suffix`, `arrowDirection` (`inward`, `outward`), `units` (`inch`, `mils`, `mm`, `auto`), `unitsFormat` (`none`, `bare`, `paren`), `precision` (an index 0 to 9 of KiCad's list, not a digit count), `suppressZeroes`, `textPosition` (`outside`, `inline`, `manual`), `textPos`, `keepAligned`, `angle`, `size`, `thickness`, `bold`, `italic`, `hJustify`, `mirrored`, `font`, `lineThickness`, `arrowLength`, `extensionOffset`, `locked`.
- `zone`: `ruleArea`, `name`, `layers`, `locked`, `points` (its main outline; a set replaces it only on a zone of one outline with no hole, 3 points or more), `borderStyle` (`none`, `edge`, `full`), `hatchPitch` (0.1 to 2 on a set); a rule area also `noTracks`, `noVias`, `noPads`, `noCopperPour`, `noFootprints`, `placement`, `placementSource` (`sheet`, `componentClass`, `group`, `designBlock`), `placementName`. A zone of the footprint on no copper layer has no fill keys.
- `image` (a reference picture): `x`, `y` (its center), `scale`, `layer`, `bytes` (the PNG's own size, as given or as the file holds it), `png` (base64) while that is at most 64 KiB, and `locked` in the board frame only. A set takes `grayscale: true` (KiCad's one shot Convert to grayscale, which changes the picture) and never `png`; a scaled side under 15 pixels is refused.
- `point`: `x`, `y`, `layer`, `size` (over 0), `locked`.

### The footprint editor's asks, pad defaults and numbering

Island `v0.2.3-cc25`, in an `fpedit` frame, on an engine with every fpedit export (engine run 15); a host gates the ops on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `fpedit`, and `ev.fpedit` on `fpeditHeld` (the engine's marker `kicadHostFpEdit` beside every fpedit export: KiCad's footprint editor then asks the island before each of its windows, and the island takes every ask). In every other frame each op answers `unsupported` ("a footprint edits in an fpedit frame"); on an engine without the exports, `unsupported` naming the first missing one. The checks run in this order, each before any effect: the args (`bad_args`), the frame, the engine (`not_ready`, `unsupported`), a load parked or a popup menu or a dialog up (`busy`), then for `fpedit.enumerate` a footprint open (`no_footprint`).

- **`PadDefaults`** is the master pad KiCad gives every new pad (its Default Pad Properties): every key of the `pad` record of `fpedit.items.get` but `kind` and `uuid`, its position 0, 0.
- **`fpedit.padDefaults.get`** answers the master pad, a footprint open or not. Answered during a check (a read).
- **`fpedit.padDefaults.set { set }`**: `set` a non empty partial of a pad's keys, read as `fpedit.items.set` reads a pad's (`bad_args` otherwise). KiCad's pad window rules apply: what it refuses answers `bad_args`, what it resets is reset (a circle squares its size). Applied at once, with no undo step, the footprint not modified; a `number` given also becomes the pad tool's next number. The answer is the master pad read back.
- **`fpedit.enumerate { start, step, prefix }`**: `start` and `step` whole numbers 0 to 999, `prefix` 0 to 64 characters with no control character (KiCad's Renumber Pads window's fields). Starts KiCad's own numbering on the open footprint with no window: the reader clicks or drags across the pads, each taking `prefix` and the next number, and KiCad commits the run as ONE undo step on a double click or a tool change (then `ev.edited`), Esc taking it back. The answer `{}` comes as the numbering starts, before the reader's first press. `bad_args` for a footprint with no pads, `busy` while a pad is taken apart (pad edit mode), `not_applied` when KiCad does not start it.
- **The numbering's end** (the island of engine build T17): `ev.ready` lists `fpeditNotice` beside `fpeditHeld` when the engine also has the marker `kicadHostFpEditEnumerateEnd`; the engine then tells the frame when its numbering loop ends (`window.kicadHostFpEdit.onNotice`, read closed) and the frame sends `ev.fpeditNotice`. KiCad's status popup during the numbering never shows on that engine. On an older engine the cap is absent and nothing is sent: the host learns the end only from `ev.edited` (kept) or nothing (taken back).

### The footprint's properties, its embedded files, the checker, Tidy and the SVG

Island `v0.2.3-cc25`, in an `fpedit` frame with a footprint open, on an engine with the fpedit exports (engine run 15: `kicadFpEditFootprintGet`, `kicadFpEditFootprintSet`, `kicadFpEditEmbed`, `kicadFpEditCheck`, `kicadFpEditCleanup`, `kicadFpEditPlotSvg`); a host gates every op on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `fpedit`. The checks are the fpedit ops' own, in their order (the args, `unsupported` outside an `fpedit` frame, `not_ready`, `unsupported` naming the first missing export, `busy`, `no_footprint`). `fpedit.footprint.get` and `fpedit.check` are reads and answer during a check.

- `FootprintProps` is exactly `{ name, description, keywords, fields, type, boardOnly, excludeFromPos, excludeFromBom, dnp, allowMissingCourtyard, allowSolderMaskBridges, clearance, maskMargin, pasteMargin, pasteRatio, zoneConnection, netTieGroups, jumpers, privateLayers, customStackup, userLayerNames, models, embedded }`, as KiCad's footprint properties dialog shows them. `fields` in the footprint's order, each `{ name, value, visible, layer, kind }`, `name` the canonical one (Reference, Value, Datasheet, Description, then the user's), `kind` `reference`, `value`, `datasheet`, `description` or `user`. `type` `smd`, `through_hole` or `unspecified`. `clearance`, `maskMargin` and `pasteMargin` in mm, null for the board's; `pasteRatio` KiCad's own fraction from -1 to 1 (the dialog shows it as a percent), null for the board's; `zoneConnection` `inherit`, `none`, `thermal` or `solid`. `netTieGroups` the pad lists KiCad stores (no empty one); `jumpers` `{ duplicateNumbersAreJumpers, groups }`, each group pad numbers. `privateLayers` canonical layer names. `customStackup` `{ on, copperLayers }` (`copperLayers` reads 2 unless `on`). `userLayerNames` the user layers the editor enables, canonical name to shown name: the editor's preferences, not the footprint's. `models` at most 32 lines `{ path, offset, rotate, scale, opacity, show }` (offset in mm, rotate in degrees, each three numbers). `embedded` `{ name, type, bytes }` per embedded file, `bytes` its size before KiCad compresses it, `type` `model`, `datasheet`, `font` or `other`. Strings are at most 1,024 characters, but the description, the keywords and a field's value, read up to 16,384.
- **`fpedit.footprint.set { set }`**: `FootprintSet` is any of FootprintProps' keys but `fields` and `embedded`, at least one, plus `fields` (patches by name, `{ name, value?, visible?, layer? }`, none twice), `addField` (`{ name, value, visible, layer }`, a new user field) and `removeField` (a name). The island checks the shape and KiCad's bounds (clearance 0 to 100 mm, margins -100 to 100 mm, `pasteRatio` -1 to 1, a custom stackup of an even 2 to 32 copper layers, at most 32 models with rotation within 360 degrees, scale 0.0001 to 10,000, offset within 1,000 mm and opacity 0 to 1, texts of at most 1,024 characters, a line break only in the description); `userLayerNames` is read only, taken only when it equals the get's (`bad_args` "fpedit.footprint.set: userLayerNames is the editor's and read only" otherwise). The engine refuses (`bad_args` "fpedit.footprint.set", nothing changed) a mandatory field added or removed, a jumper group naming a pad the footprint lacks, a private copper, technical, Edge.Cuts or Margin layer, a custom stackup that orphans a layer in use, or a name KiCad's library names refuse. `models` replaces the list; a `name` renames the footprint in the editor only (the host renames the file on save). The setters run in the dialog's order as ONE commit "Footprint properties": one undo step and an `ev.edited`. The answer is the properties read back once they show every key given, or the undo step (KiCad's rules may change a value given), else `not_applied` after about 5 s.
- **`fpedit.embed`**: `{ name, type, bytes }` (a name of 1 to 255 characters with no slash or backslash, never `.` or `..`; the file's bytes, a `Uint8Array` of 1 byte to 25 MiB) is written under a fresh `/tmp/cc-embed/<n>/` outside the project folder and embedded in the footprint by KiCad's own embedded files (compressed and hashed; a file of that name is replaced), ONE commit "Embed a file"; `{ remove }` takes one out, ONE commit "Remove an embedded file" (`not_found`, the name, when the footprint embeds none of that name). The answer is the properties once `embedded` shows the change (a new file with its type and size), the folder removed; `bad_args` "fpedit.embed: KiCad refused the file" when the engine refuses it, `not_applied` after about 5 s. `fpedit.take` carries the embedded files.
- **`fpedit.check`** runs KiCad's footprint checker (`DIALOG_FOOTPRINT_CHECKER::runChecks`, in its order, collecting in place of markers): `{ findings }`, each exactly `{ code, severity, message, uuids, pads, x, y }`. `code` is KiCad's settings key: on engine run 15 `malformed_courtyard`, `missing_courtyard`, `footprint_type_mismatch`, `padstack`, `padstack_invalid`, `through_hole_pad_without_hole`, `holes_co_located`, `hole_to_hole`, `shorting_items`, `footprint` and `silk_over_copper` (a host words these and shows `message`, KiCad's own sentence, for any other); `severity` `error` or `warning` by the design settings (an ignored code is left out); `uuids` and `pads` the items and pad numbers concerned (at most three); `x`, `y` the point in mm in the footprint's frame, both null for a finding on the footprint itself. Nothing changes.
- **`fpedit.cleanup`** is KiCad's Cleanup Graphics over the footprint's drawings (the dialog's 2 mm tolerance) and, with `repair`, Repair Footprint (KiCad 10 renews duplicate uuids). The answer `{ items, repaired }` is what a dry run finds: `items` `{ code, uuids }`, `code` `null_graphic`, `duplicate_graphic`, `lines_to_rect`, `merge_pad` (or `other`), `repaired` the duplicate uuids. With `dryRun` nothing changes; without it the same runs as ONE commit "Tidy footprint" and the answer comes once that undo step shows (`not_applied` after about 5 s), none when nothing is found.
- **`fpedit.svg`** plots the footprint's layers the view shows with KiCad's own SVG plotter (no frame, the page fitted to the footprint, no mirror) into a fresh `/tmp/cc-svg/<n>/`, its record `.cc-svg.json` written last; the answer is the file's bytes (a fresh `Uint8Array`, transferred, at most 4 MiB), the folder removed. `plot_failed` when KiCad wrote no SVG; `not_applied` when no record came within about 30 s.

### footprint.forEditor and footprint.push
The board side of the footprint editor, in a `pcb` frame with a board open (island `v0.2.3-cc25`, on an engine with `kicadFootprintForEditor`, `kicadFootprintPushToBoard`, `kicadBoardItemGet` and `kicadCollabBusy`, engine run 15; a host gates both on `caps` listing `footprintEdit`, listed by `pcb` frames only). The checks, in order, before any effect: the args (`bad_args`); the frame (an `fpedit` frame answers `unsupported` "a footprint editor opens footprints, not documents", a `sch` or `symedit` frame `unsupported` "footprints are read in a pcb or an fpedit frame"); the engine (`not_ready`, `unsupported` naming the first missing export); a board open (`not_ready`); a load parked, a popup or a dialog up, or an item hanging off the pointer (`busy`).

- `footprint.forEditor { uuid }` answers the placed footprint exactly as KiCad's Load Footprint from Board copies it (a clone, unlocked, its nets cleared, flipped to the front, angle 0, at the origin) with every child keeping the BOARD's uuid, formatted as a library save writes it (no library nickname, no `(at)` on the footprint, no pad nets, embedded files kept): `{ name, libId, ref, text }`, `text` one `(footprint ...)` of at most 8 MiB, ready for `fpedit.open` with `mode` `placed`. `not_found` (the uuid) when the board places no footprint by that uuid.
- `footprint.push { uuid, text, libId }` is KiCad's Save Footprint to Board of an edited footprint (`SaveFootprintToBoard`'s update body through `ExchangeFootprint`): a child whose uuid the placed footprint holds keeps it, any other takes a fresh one; the pads take the placed part's nets by name; the part keeps its position, angle, side and its own uuid; ONE commit "Update footprint": one undo step and an `ev.edited`. `text` is ONE `(footprint ...)` of at most 8 MiB. `libId` `""` keeps the footprint the part names; `lib:name` (both halves) sets it in the same commit, so the part names the design's own copy. Nothing is restyled (a fresh footprint editor's settings apply no board defaults to footprint fields, texts, shapes, dimensions or barcodes). The answer is `{ uuid }` once the apply queue is empty and the part reads back through `board.item.get`'s read (with a `libId`, naming it); the host reads the part's record with `board.item.get`. `not_found` (the uuid) when the board places no footprint by that uuid; `bad_args` "footprint.push: the text holds no footprint KiCad reads" when the engine refuses the text (nothing queued, no undo step); `not_applied` past about 5 s. The host's next `project.save` writes the change.

### footprint.convert
Reads a footprint file of another tool, or of an older KiCad, with KiCad's own readers, and answers its footprints as text (island `v0.2.3-cc25`, on an engine with `kicadFootprintConvert`, engine run 15; a host gates it on `caps` listing `footprintConvert`, listed by `pcb` and `fpedit` frames). The args are exactly `{ name, bytes }`: the file's name (1 to 255 bytes of UTF-8, no slash, backslash or NUL, never `.` or `..`) and its bytes (a `Uint8Array` of 1 byte to 25 MiB). The frame writes the file under a fresh `/tmp/cc-import/<n>/`, outside the project folder, and removes the folder after the read. A file KiCad's `PCB_IO_MGR` takes as a set of footprints is read whole (each footprint KiCad's reader enumerates, then loads); any other file is read as ONE footprint by the first reader whose `CanReadFootprint` takes it (KiCad's Import Footprint). Each footprint is reset as a library save resets it (angle 0, on the front) and formatted as `fpedit.take` formats one. The answer is `{ format, footprints: [{ name, text }] }`, `format` KiCad's own name of the reader. `unreadable` when no reader takes the file, the reader refuses it or reads nothing (`message` KiCad's sentence, or "KiCad read no footprint from this file"), `too_large` past 2,000 footprints or 8 MiB of their text. A `sch` or `symedit` frame answers `unsupported`. The checks: the args, the frame, the engine (`not_ready`, `unsupported`), busy while a load is parked or a popup or a dialog is up; it needs no document open.

The readers of KiCad 10 the engine registers, by `format` and the files they take (the run 15 probe owes the measured list, gate 0 item 3): `KiCad` (`.kicad_mod`), `Legacy` (KiCad 5 `.mod`, `.emp`), `Eagle` (`.lbr`), `gEDA / Lepton EDA` (`.fp`), `Altium Designer` (`.PcbLib`, `.IntLib`), `EasyEDA / JLCEDA Std` (`.json`), `EasyEDA / JLCEDA Pro` (`.elibz`, `.epro`) and `PADS` (`.asc`). The fixtures `tests/fixtures/footprint-convert/legacy.mod` (one KiCad 5 footprint, `LEGACY_R0805`) and `package.lbr` (one Eagle package, `EAGLE_R0603`) read as `Legacy` and `Eagle`, one footprint each, in a `pcb` and an `fpedit` frame (`tests/e2e/footprint-edit.spec.ts`, engine run 15).

### graphics.import
Brings a DXF or SVG drawing into the open document by KiCad's own importer, with no window of KiCad's (island `v0.2.3-cc25`, on an engine with `kicadImportGraphics`, engine run 15; a host gates it on `caps` listing `importGraphics`, listed by a `sch` or `pcb` frame on an engine with `kicadImportGraphics` and `kicadCollabBusy`, by a `symedit` frame with the symbol editor's exports too, by an `fpedit` frame with the footprint editor's). It is the body of KiCad's Import Graphics dialogs (the board and footprint editors' and the schematic and symbol editors'), the half picked by the frame.

The args are `name` (the file's name, 1 to 255 bytes of UTF-8, no slash, backslash or NUL), `bytes` (a `Uint8Array` of 1 byte to 25 MB), `place` (`at` or `pointer`), and, all optional: `layer` (a board layer's canonical name, `Dwgs.User`, `F.SilkS`; absent, the frame's active layer; a `sch` or `symedit` frame ignores it), `scale` (0.0001 to 10,000, default 1), `origin` (`[x, y]` in mm, each within 2,000, default `[0, 0]`), `lineWidth` (mm, above 0 and at most 100, or `null` for the importer's default; a DXF only, an SVG keeps its own widths), `dxfUnits` (absent: the file's own `$INSUNITS`), `group` (the items in one group), `fixDiscontinuities` and `tolerance` (join the ends of lines closer than `tolerance` mm, 0 to 100, default 1). Any other key or shape answers `bad_args`.

The checks, in order, before any effect: the args (`bad_args`); the frame (a `ds` frame answers `unsupported`); the engine (`not_ready`, `unsupported` naming the first missing export); a document open (`not_ready` in a `sch` or `pcb` frame, `no_symbol`, `no_footprint`); a load parked, a popup or a dialog up, or (`sch`, `pcb`) an item hanging off the pointer (`busy`); then the file's kind BY ITS CONTENT, never its name: a DXF when, past any `999` comments, its first group is `0` `SECTION`, an SVG when, past the XML declaration, comments and a doctype, its first element is `svg`; anything else answers `not_a_drawing`. The frame writes the drawing under a fresh `/tmp/cc-gfx/<n>/` with the extension of its kind (KiCad picks its reader by the extension), outside the project folder, and removes the folder once the importer has read it.

- `place: "at"`: the drawing lands at `origin` as ONE commit (one undo step and an `ev.edited`; one Ctrl+Z through `key.press` takes the whole drawing back); the answer comes once the apply queue is empty, else `not_applied` after about 5 s. In an `fpedit` frame the items join the open footprint (they save with it through `fpedit.take`), in a `symedit` frame the open symbol's current unit.
- `place: "pointer"`: the answer comes at once and the drawing hangs on the pointer in KiCad's move tool (R rotates it); a click places it as ONE commit, Esc leaves nothing and no undo step. The uuids name the items the click will place. In a `sch` or `pcb` frame the hanging drawing is joined to `place`'s watch: every gate answers `busy` until it ends, and a later `place` cancels it first.

The answer is `{ items, uuids, warnings }`: `items` the drawing's items (a group made by `group` is not counted and its uuid not listed), `uuids` their uuids (at most 4,096), `warnings` KiCad's importer messages, one per line (at most 64, each cut to 1,024 characters). `unreadable` when KiCad read nothing it can place (`message` its warnings, or "KiCad read nothing it can place from this drawing"); `bad_args` "graphics.import: KiCad's importer refused it (a layer this document does not enable, or the document cannot take a drawing now)" when the engine refuses the call.

KiCad's own Import Graphics (Ctrl+Shift+F, its menu and toolbar items) opens nothing: under `importGraphics` a `pcb` frame holds `pcbnew.InteractiveDrawing.placeImportedGraphics` and a `sch` frame holds `eeschema.EditorControl.importGraphics`, each sending `ev.held { action }`; a `symedit` frame holds the latter already under `symedit`; an `fpedit` frame sends `ev.fpedit { ask: "graphics", uuid: "", isNew: false }` (cap `fpeditHeld`). The host opens its own Import a drawing page for each.

The fixtures `tests/fixtures/drawings/outline.dxf` (a closed polyline and an arc, mm) and `logo.svg` (two paths) import in all four frames (`tests/e2e/graphics-import.spec.ts`, engine run 15).

### The footprint editor's held actions and the board's footprint doors

Island `v0.2.3-cc25`. KiCad's footprint editor and the board open windows of KiCad's own that the host's footprint editor page replaces, so the island holds them and the frame sends `ev.held { action }` in their place (from a key, and on an engine whose menus ask the page, `heldMenus`, from KiCad's menus and toolbars); `action.run` of one answers `held`. Each is held only in its frame, only while `ev.ready` listed the cap beside it and a document is open (in an `fpedit` frame: a footprint is open), one `ev.held` per press:

- `fpedit`, under `fpedit` (the footprint editor's own cap: every fpedit export present): `common.Control.save`, `common.Control.saveAs`, `common.Control.revert`, `common.Control.print`, `common.Control.quit`, `common.Interactive.find`, `common.Control.showDatasheet`, `common.Control.showProperties`, `common.Control.showLibraryTree`, `common.Control.show3DViewer`, `common.Control.showFootprintBrowser`, `common.Control.newLibrary`, `common.Control.addLibrary`, `pcbnew.ModuleEditor.newFootprint`, `.createFootprint`, `.editFootprint`, `.duplicateFootprint`, `.renameFootprint`, `.deleteFootprint`, `.cutFootprint`, `.copyFootprint`, `.pasteFootprint`, `.importFootprint`, `.exportFootprint`, `.footprintProperties`, `.padTable`, `.checkFootprint`, `.loadFootprintFromBoard`, `.saveFootprintToBoard`, `.repairFootprint`, `pcbnew.EditorControl.EditLibFpInFpEditor`, `pcbnew.Control.previousFootprint`, `.nextFootprint`, `pcbnew.PadTool.defaultPadProperties`, `.enumeratePads`, `.PushPadSettings`, `pcbnew.GlobalEdit.cleanupGraphics`, `.editTextAndGraphics`, `pcbnew.InteractiveDrawing.placeImportedGraphics`, `.placeReferenceImage` and `pcbnew.FpWizard.showWizards`. A pencil press of the last two drawing tools arrives as `ev.held` too: the host opens its own picture and drawing import there.
- `pcb`, under `footprintEdit` (`footprint.forEditor` and `footprint.push`): `pcbnew.EditorControl.EditFpInFpEditor` (Ctrl+E on a footprint), `pcbnew.EditorControl.EditLibFpInFpEditor` (Ctrl+Shift+E) and `common.Control.showFootprintEditor`. The host opens its own footprint editor on the selected footprint, or on none.

On an engine without the fpedit exports (before engine run 15) an `fpedit` frame lists no `fpedit` and holds none of these; a `pcb` frame without `footprintEdit` lets KiCad open its footprint editor as before. The doors table (`bridge/ext/contextMenuDoors.json`) names `pcbnew.ModuleEditor.padTable` and `pcbnew.PadTool.PushPadSettings` `held`.

### block.applyLayout

Island `v0.2.3-cc25`, in a `pcb` frame, on an engine with `kicadDesignBlockApplyLayout` and `kicadCollabBusy` (engine run 15, unit e-block-layout); a host gates it on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `blockLayouts`, which a `pcb` frame lists when its engine also takes Keep groups and Apply block layouts on `board.preview` and `board.update` (above). It runs KiCad's Apply Design Block Layout over the groups asked with no KiCad window: each group linked to a design block is laid out again as its block's layout, as ONE undo step.

- `uuids`: 1 to 256 groups of the board, lower case, none twice. Each group's design block row must be loaded in the engine (`project.libs` naming the block's library), as a group placed with its block's nickname is.
- The answer is `{ applied, failures }`: how many groups took their layout, and each group that did not, with KiCad's reason (at most 1,024 characters). It comes once the engine's apply queue is empty and the run's record is there. A group placed from a block whose layout lacks a part the schematic names (a group made by `board.update` with Keep groups) fails with KiCad's reason; one placed from that same block's own layout applies.
- The checks, in order, every one before any effect: the args (`bad_args`, `message` the op); the frame (`unsupported`, "a design block layout is applied in a pcb frame"); the engine (`not_ready` before it is up; `unsupported` naming the first missing export, so on engine run 14 `unsupported` "kicadDesignBlockApplyLayout"); a document open (`not_ready`); a load parked, a popup menu, a dialog up or a placed item on the pointer (`busy`). Then `block_failed` when the engine refuses a uuid naming no group ("a uuid names no group of the board"), when nothing applied and no failure is named (KiCad's line, "No groups with a linked design block are selected." for groups with no link), or when a window of KiCad's showed during the run (closed without accepting it, `message` its title); `not_applied` when no record comes within about 30 s; `island_error` for a record of another shape.
- The run's folder under `/tmp/cc-blocklayout/` is removed after every answer.

### block.place

Island `v0.2.3-cc25`, in a `sch` or a `pcb` frame, on an engine with `kicadDesignBlockPlace`, `kicadCollabGetSelection` and `kicadCollabBusy` (engine run 15, units e-block-place and e-block-place-sch); a host gates it on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `blocks` (listed in a `sch` or `pcb` frame whose engine has `kicadDesignBlockPlace` and `kicadDesignBlockSave`). It places a design block the host keeps into the open design with KiCad's own placement and no KiCad window, no chooser and no pane: the block hangs off the pointer, a press puts it down as ONE undo step, Escape drops it with no step, as `place` does.

- `name`: the block's name, `block.save`'s rule (1 to 120 characters, none of `/`, `\`, `:` or a control character, never `.` or `..`).
- `files`: 1 to 3 of the block's own files, each `path` one of `<name>.kicad_sch`, `<name>.kicad_pcb`, `<name>.json`, none twice, `text` the file as KiCad wrote it; at most 32 MiB of UTF-8 together (`too_large` past it). A `sch` frame needs `<name>.kicad_sch` among them, a `pcb` frame `<name>.kicad_pcb` (`bad_args` "block.place: a block placed in a pcb frame needs <name>.kicad_pcb"). The island writes them under a fresh `/tmp/cc-block/<n>/<name>.kicad_block/`, outside the project folder.
- `asSheet`, `keepAnnotations`, `asGroup`: the three options of KiCad's own chooser, handed to KiCad as they are. In a `sch` frame `asSheet` true places the block's schematic as a new sheet: KiCad's smallest sheet box named after the block hangs off the pointer and the press puts the sheet down, its file `<name>.kicad_sch` (`<name>_2.kicad_sch` and on when taken) copied beside the project, which reaches the host with the next save's `ev.saved` like any sheet; false places the block's items. `keepAnnotations` keeps the block's designators; `asGroup` puts what is placed in one group named after the block.
- `nickname` (optional): the `design-block-lib-table` row the block came from (1 to 120 characters, no `:` or control character). With it a group placed as a group carries the block's link (`lib_id "<nickname>:<name>"`), so `block.applyLayout` and `block.save` `linkedGroup` work on it while that row is loaded (`project.libs`); without it the group carries no link. Send it only to an island listing `blocks`.
- One block at a time: an item of `place` or a block still hanging is dropped first (`busy` "the previous item still hangs off the pointer" when it would not go). The block's items, placed as contents, are watched as `place`'s item is, so every gate answers `busy` while they hang; a sheet's box is in no selection, so it is not watched by the other ops, and the next `block.place` drops it first.
- The checks, in order, every one before any effect: the args (`bad_args`, `message` the op); the size (`too_large`); the frame (`unsupported` "a design block is placed in a sch or pcb frame"); the frame's own file (`bad_args`); the engine (`not_ready` before it is up; `unsupported` naming the first missing export, so on engine run 14 `unsupported` "kicadDesignBlockPlace"); a document open (`not_ready`); a load parked, a popup menu or a dialog up (`busy`). Then `not_applied` "block.place: KiCad did not take the block" when the engine cannot read the block, `busy` "block.place: the editor did not take the block" when its items never reach the pointer within about 10 s (a sheet: when the engine's apply queue does not run within that time, `not_applied`), `island_error` for a throw.
- The run's folder under `/tmp/cc-block/` is removed once the items hang, and after every failure; a sheet's folder stays until the next `block.place`, since the press copies the sheet's file from it.

### block.save

Island `v0.2.3-cc25`, in a `sch` or a `pcb` frame, on an engine with `kicadDesignBlockSave` and `kicadCollabBusy` (engine run 15, unit e-block-save). It writes part of the open design as a design block's file with KiCad's own writers and no KiCad window: no overwrite question, no properties dialog, no library write. The host keeps the file beside the block's description (`<name>.json`, the host's own) in its block folder and adds the library row itself.

- `scope`: in a `sch` frame `sheet` (the sheet at `sheetPath`, in `project.open`'s sheet path form: `/` for the root, then one uuid and a slash per level) or `selection` (the selected items; a single selected sheet saves that sheet, a single selected group its children); in a `pcb` frame `board` (the whole board), `selection` (the selected items with their nets) or `linkedGroup` (the one selected group placed from a design block, as KiCad's Save to Linked Design Block). A scope of the other frame answers `unsupported` ("scope board is saved in a pcb frame"); any other frame answers `unsupported` ("a design block is saved in a sch or pcb frame").
- `name`: 1 to 120 characters, none of `/`, `\`, `:` or a control character, never `.` or `..`.
- The answer is `{ files }`, one file: `<name>.kicad_sch` in a `sch` frame, `<name>.kicad_pcb` in a `pcb` frame, `text` the file as KiCad wrote it (UTF-8). It comes once the engine's apply queue is empty and the run's record is there.
- Nothing in the design changes: no undo step, the modified flag as it was, the selection not grouped, no `ev.edited`.
- The checks, in order, every one before any effect: the args (`bad_args`, `message` the op); the frame and the scope (`unsupported`); the engine (`not_ready` before it is up; `unsupported` naming the first missing export, so on engine run 14 `unsupported` "kicadDesignBlockSave"); a document open (`not_ready`); a load parked, a popup menu, a dialog up or a placed item on the pointer (`busy`). Then `block_failed` with KiCad's first line when KiCad refuses (the root sheet or any sheet holding sheets: "Design blocks with nested sheets are not supported."; a path no sheet has: "No sheet has that path."; nothing selected; `linkedGroup` without exactly one selected group linked to a block), `not_applied` when the engine refuses the call or no record comes within about 60 s, `island_error` for a record or file of another shape.
- The run's folder under `/tmp/cc-blocksave/` is removed after every answer.

### libs.remove and paths.set
The run time rows of KiCad's library tables, the host's side of Setup's Sources and Paths, in a `sch` or a `pcb` frame (island `v0.2.3-cc25`, on an engine with `kicadLibsRemoveEntry`, `kicadLibsAddEntry` and `kicadCollabBusy` for `libs.remove`, `kicadPathVarsSet` and `kicadCollabBusy` for `paths.set`, engine run 15; a host gates them on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `libRows` and `pathVars`, listed by `sch` and `pcb` frames only; KiCad's 3rd party variable on `addonPaths`, below). Any other frame answers `unsupported`. Neither needs a document open. The checks, in order, every one before any effect: the args (`bad_args`, `message` the op); the engine (`not_ready` before `ev.ready`; `unsupported` naming the first missing export, so on engine run 14 `libs.remove` answers `unsupported` "kicadLibsRemoveEntry" and `paths.set` `unsupported` "kicadPathVarsSet"); a load parked, a popup menu or a dialog up (`busy`).

- `libs.remove { kind, nickname }` takes back a row `project.libs` added at run time: `kind` `symbol`, `footprint` or `block` (a design block library), `nickname` as `project.libs` takes one. The engine erases the row from its live table and drops what it loaded from it; KiCad's choosers no longer list it. The answer `{}` comes once the engine's apply queue is empty. `not_found` (`message` the nickname) when the engine holds no run time row of that kind and name: a row the boot made (KiCad's own set, the project's tables as KiCad loaded them) is never removed. Each engine removes only the rows it added since its boot: a `pcb` frame answers `not_found` for a `symbol` row, so a host removes a row in the frame of its kind (symbol rows in a `sch` frame, footprint rows in a `pcb` frame; a block row in either frame that added it). A later `project.libs` naming the nickname adds its row again. Past about 10 s with the queue still busy: `island_error`.
- `paths.set { vars }` sets path variables as KiCad's Configure Paths sets them: `vars` maps 1 to 64 names to folders of the project. A name is KiCad's form (`^[A-Z][A-Z0-9_]{0,63}$`) and never one of KiCad's own (`KIPRJMOD`, `KICAD_USER_TEMPLATE_DIR`, `KICAD_PTEMPLATES`, and for KiCad 6 to 10 the versioned `SYMBOL_DIR`, `FOOTPRINT_DIR`, `3DMODEL_DIR`, `TEMPLATE_DIR`, `3RD_PARTY` and `DESIGN_BLOCK_DIR`), but `KICAD10_3RD_PARTY` (below); a folder is a path in `project.open`'s normal form, relative to the project folder (not `${KIPRJMOD}`: a design whose project file sits in a subfolder names the subfolder). The frame hands the engine each folder made absolute in its file system; every library row naming one of the variables (`${NAME}`) loads again, so a row that found nothing before now loads. A variable is never removed; a later `paths.set` of the same name replaces its folder. From the answer on, the frame's `project.libs` resolves rows `${NAME}/...` of the project's tables into that folder too. The answer is `{ set }`, the names in the order sent, once the engine's apply queue is empty; `not_applied` when the engine refuses the call or its queue stays busy past about 10 s (nothing set on the island's side when refused).
- KiCad's 3rd party variable (`addonPaths`, island `v0.2.3-cc25`): `KICAD10_3RD_PARTY`, the folder KiCad 10 installs add-ons into, is the one name of KiCad's own `paths.set` takes, alone or beside the host's own names, as KiCad's Configure Paths sets its value. A host points it at the folder of the project where it wrote an installed add-on's files in KiCad's layout (`symbols/<package>/`, `footprints/<package>/`, `3dmodels/<package>/`), so the design's rows `${KICAD10_3RD_PARTY}/...` (an add-on's `PCM_` rows) load in the engine and resolve for the frame's `project.libs`. It needs an engine that takes the name, which says so with the marker export `kicadPathVarsThirdParty`: `ev.ready` lists `addonPaths` in `sch` and `pcb` frames exactly when the engine has `kicadPathVarsSet`, `kicadCollabBusy` and that marker. A request naming the variable on an engine without the marker answers `unsupported` "kicadPathVarsThirdParty" (after the args, after `kicadPathVarsSet`), and nothing is set. Every other name of KiCad's stays `bad_args`, the other versions' `3RD_PARTY` too, and `project.libs` `vars` never take it.

### sheet.reload

The open schematic or board reads its drawing sheet again (island `v0.2.3-cc25`, on an engine with `kicadDrawingSheetReload`; a host gates it on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `sheetReload`, which a `sch` and a `pcb` frame list on such an engine). It is KiCad's own reload of the `.kicad_wks` the document names, with no window of KiCad's. A host sends it after `project.write` of that `.kicad_wks` (the drawing sheet editor's Save, a text save of the file), so every plot and print from then on carries the new sheet; a board's plot carries the old sheet until the reload. A sheet that does not read gives KiCad's default sheet, with no message; a sheet file that is missing still answers `{}`.

It is no edit: no undo step, the modified flag unchanged, no `ev.edited`.

The checks, in order, every one before any effect: args other than none or `{}` answer `bad_args`; in any frame but `sch` and `pcb` it answers `unsupported`; then as a schematic op in a `sch` frame and a board op in a `pcb` frame: `not_ready` before `ev.ready`, `unsupported` naming `kicadDrawingSheetReload` or `kicadCollabBusy` on an engine without it, `not_ready` with no document opened, `busy` while a load is parked, a popup menu or a dialog is up, or a placed item hangs off the pointer. Then the engine's reload: `not_applied` when it refuses (it refuses while an open is in flight), or when its apply queue does not empty within 30 s; else `{}` once it has.

### Edit in bulk's doors: Change, Update and the Zone manager held

Island `v0.2.3-cc25`. Nine actions of KiCad's open a window of its own that the host's Edit in bulk pages replace, so the island holds them and the frame sends `ev.held { action }` in their place (from a key, from KiCad's menus under `heldMenus`, and from a choice of our context menu, whose item carries `door: "held"`); `action.run` of one answers `held`. Each is held only in its frame and only while `ev.ready` listed the cap of the op the host's page runs, so a host that cannot run the page never has the window taken from it:

- `sch`, under `symbolExchange` (`symbols.exchange`): `eeschema.InteractiveEdit.changeSymbol`, `changeSymbols`, `updateSymbol` and `updateSymbols` (KiCad's Change and Update Symbols window). The host opens Edit in bulk on Refresh and swap.
- `pcb`, under `footprintExchange` (`footprints.exchange`): `pcbnew.GlobalEdit.changeFootprint`, `changeFootprints`, `updateFootprint` and `updateFootprints` (KiCad's Change and Update Footprints window). The host opens Edit in bulk on Refresh and swap.
- `pcb`, under `boardEdit` (`board.edit`): `pcbnew.Control.zonesManager` (KiCad's Zone manager). The host opens Edit in bulk on Copper areas.

The singular action names the selection, the plural one the whole design: the host reads its own selection when it opens the page. The doors table (`bridge/ext/contextMenuDoors.json`) names all nine `held`.

### Edit in bulk's other doors: the bulk edits of KiCad's menus held

Island `v0.2.3-cc25`. Ten more actions of KiCad's open a window of its own that a tool of the host's Edit in bulk page replaces, so the island holds them and the frame sends `ev.held { action }` in their place (from KiCad's menus under `heldMenus`, and from a key where KiCad gives one); `action.run` of one answers `held`. Each is held only in its frame and only while `ev.ready` listed the cap of the op the host's tool runs, so a host that cannot run the tool never has the window taken from it:

- `pcb`, under `boardEdit` (`board.edit`): `pcbnew.GlobalEdit.editTracksAndVias` (the host opens Tracks and vias), `pcbnew.GlobalEdit.editTextAndGraphics` (Text and lines), `pcbnew.GlobalEdit.editTeardrops` (Teardrops), `pcbnew.GlobalEdit.swapLayers` (Swap layers), `pcbnew.GlobalEdit.globalDeletions` (Delete), and Tidy on the group it names: `pcbnew.GlobalEdit.cleanupTracksAndVias` (Tracks and vias), `pcbnew.GlobalEdit.removeUnusedPads` (Unused pads), `pcbnew.GlobalEdit.cleanupGraphics` (Drawings) and `pcbnew.Control.repairBoard` (Repair).
- `sch`, under `schEdit` (`sch.edit`): `eeschema.InteractiveEdit.editTextAndGraphics` (the host opens Text and lines).

`pcbnew.GlobalEdit.editTextAndGraphics` and `pcbnew.GlobalEdit.cleanupGraphics` are also held in an `fpedit` frame, where the footprint editor answers them with its own doors; a frame takes only the row that names it. None of the ten stands in our context menu, so the doors table (`bridge/ext/contextMenuDoors.json`) has no row for them.

### board.padNets
Reads every footprint pad's net in a `pcb` frame with a board open, the facts KiCad's own Update Schematic from PCB takes from the board (island `v0.2.3-cc25`, on an engine with `kicadBoardPadNets`, engine run 15; a host gates it on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `boardPadNets`, listed by `pcb` frames only). No args (absent or `{}`). The checks, in order: the args (`bad_args`); the frame (`unsupported`); the engine (`not_ready`, `unsupported` naming `kicadBoardPadNets`); a board open (`not_ready`); a load parked, a popup or a dialog up, or an item hanging off the pointer (`busy`). A read: answered during a `board.check` or a `sheet.check`, nothing changed, no undo step.

The answer lists `{ ref, pad, net }` per pad: the footprint's reference, the pad's number and its SHORT net name (as the schematic names it), at most 32,768 rows (`truncated` true when the board holds more). Board only footprints, pads with no net and pads with no number are left out. The rows are what `sch.renameNets` takes.

### sch.renameNets
Renames the schematic's nets from the board's, KiCad's own Update Schematic from PCB net name pass alone (no relink, no other field, no swaps), in a `sch` frame with a schematic open (island `v0.2.3-cc25`, on an engine with `kicadSchRenameNets` and `kicadCollabBusy`, engine run 15; a host gates it on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `renameNets`, listed by `sch` frames only). The args are exactly `{ preview, pads }`: a boolean and 1 to 32,768 rows of exactly `{ ref, pad, net }`, each a string of 1 to 1,024 characters with no control character (`board.padNets`'s rows, read in the board's frame and handed over by the host). The checks, in order, before any effect: the args (`bad_args`); the frame (`unsupported` "a schematic edits in a sch frame"); the engine (`not_ready`, `unsupported` naming the first missing export); a schematic open (`not_ready`); `busy` as for every schematic op. During a `board.check`, a `sheet.check` or an import it answers `busy`.

For each pad whose symbol pin (symbols matched by reference, power symbols left out) sits on a net of another name, a row: `ref`, the pin's number `pin`, the schematic's net name `from`, the board's `to`, the sheet path the symbol sits on, and the `outcome`: `rename` (the label that drives the net takes the board's name; `label` its uuid), `add` (no label drives the net, so a label is added at the pin; `label` its uuid when KiCad names it) or `refused` (a power symbol drives the net: nothing changes for it). Pins already on the board's name give no row.

- `preview: true` lists the rows with nothing changed: `applied` 0, `depth` the undo depth as it stands, no undo step.
- `preview: false` makes every rename and every added label as ONE commit "Update net names": one undo step (the reader's Undo takes all of it back), and an `ev.edited` once the depth moved. `applied` counts the rows not refused, `depth` the undo depth after.

The frame queues the run with a fresh `/tmp/cc-renameNets/<n>/` for its record, waits for the apply queue to empty and the record, and removes the folder whatever the answer. `not_applied` when the engine refused the run or no record came within about 60 s; `island_error` for a record of another shape. The fixture `tests/fixtures/rename-nets/` (a label SIG on R1 pin 2 and R2 pin 1, the board naming that net DATA) is read by `tests/e2e/lo/rename-nets.spec.ts` (its run 15 half on cc25).

### Our context menu (`contextMenu`)

Island `v0.2.3-cc25`. Our engine from run 15 (kicad `cc/main` K-MENU, in `TOOL_MANAGER::DispatchContextMenu`) asks the frame before it pops up any canvas menu of KiCad's, the clarify-selection list included, in every editor frame (`sch`, `pcb`, `symedit`, `fpedit`, `ds`). The frame takes the menu while `ev.ready` listed the pseudo-cap `contextMenu`, a document is open, no `project.import` runs and the host is connected; KiCad then waits, parked in the menu, and the frame sends `ev.contextMenu`. Otherwise KiCad's own popup shows as before and `ev.menu` reports it. `ev.ready` lists `contextMenu` exactly when the engine has `kicadHostContextMenu`, `kicadContextMenuChoose`, `kicadContextMenuHighlight` and `kicadRunAction`; on an older engine (run 14 and before) the cap is absent and every op below answers `unsupported` naming the export.

`ev.contextMenu`: `token` names the parked menu in every answer; `at` is the point KiCad asked for, in the frame document's CSS pixels; `title` is KiCad's (empty when it has none, "Clarify Selection" for the list); `kinds` are the distinct kinds of the selection, sorted (`board.item.get`'s words in a board or footprint frame; `symbol`, `field`, `pin`, `wire`, `bus`, `busentry`, `junction`, `noconnect`, `label`, `globallabel`, `hierlabel`, `directivelabel`, `sheet`, `sheetpin`, `text`, `textbox`, `table`, `tablecell`, `shape`, `image`, `group`, `rulearea`, `marker`, `other` in a schematic or symbol frame; `line`, `rect`, `text`, `image`, `other` in a drawing sheet frame). Each item of `items` is exactly `{ id: number, type: "action" | "choice" | "check" | "radio" | "submenu" | "separator", action: string, label: string, enabled: boolean, checked: boolean, key: string, door: "run" | "held" | "prompt" }`, a submenu adding `items`. `action` names KiCad's tool action (empty for a `choice`: a clarify candidate, a gate unit, a zoom or grid preset); `label` is KiCad's own text, with its accelerators, for the host to word (a clarify candidate's label is KiCad's description of the item, never reworded); `key` is KiCad's own key for the action, empty when none; `door` says where a choice ends: `run` (it runs, or the engine hands its form to the host, as for the properties forms), `held` (the island holds it in this frame: `ev.held` follows) or `prompt` (its window is an `ev.prompt` card). An action whose window has no door of ours in this frame (the doors table `bridge/ext/contextMenuDoors.json`, or a held row whose cap is not listed, or a prompt with no `prompts` cap) is left out, never grayed, and named in `omitted`; a submenu left empty goes too, and separators are collapsed (none first, last or doubled). The engine bounds a menu at 400 items, three levels and 128 characters a label. The list may be empty: the host then answers `contextMenu.choose { id: -1 }`.

`contextMenu.choose { token, id }` resolves the parked menu with the item `id`, which KiCad then runs as its own popup would (a check item toggles, a held action sends `ev.held`); `id` `-1` closes it with nothing chosen. Every close of the host's menu sends one choose: a choice, or `-1` for a close with none. A choice is followed by a read of the selection, as a pointer release in the frame is: an item that changes it inside the engine (Select the rest of its sheet) raises no canvas event, and `ev.selected` follows when it changed. It answers `{}`, or `stale` (no menu parked with that token; the host closes its menu), `no_item` (an id the menu was not sent with, an omitted action, a submenu or a separator), `disabled`, `bad_args`. `contextMenu.highlight { token, id }` tells KiCad the item under the host's pointer or focus: in a clarify list KiCad brightens that candidate on the drawing, as on the desktop. Same answers, `-1` refused. Both are answered as they arrive, outside the one-at-a-time order (KiCad is parked in the menu, so a queued request would wait on it forever); no `ev.contextMenuEnd` follows a choose.

While a menu is parked the frame counts it as a popup over the canvas: `key.press` and `view.fit` answer `busy`, a schematic's `project.save` closes it first (as Escape, `ev.contextMenuEnd` `canceled`) and then saves, and a press or Escape in the frame closes it. The ContextMenu key and Shift+F10 in the frame, with nothing over the canvas and no text field focused, run `common.Control.showContextMenu` (KiCad's keyboard menu at its own cursor).

`action.run { name }` runs one tool action of KiCad's by its name (`pcbnew.InteractiveEdit.moveExact`), as a press of its menu item would, on the request queue like `key.press`. It answers `{}`, or `bad_args` (not an action name), `held` (the island holds it in this frame: nothing ran, the host opens its own door), `unsupported` (no `kicadRunAction`, or the action opens a window of KiCad's that has no door in this frame: nothing ran), `not_ready` (no document open), `busy` (a load is parked, a menu, a prompt or a dialog is up, or a placed item hangs off the pointer), `unknown_action` (no tool action of that name in this frame).

### buses.migration.get
The buses whose labels give them two names, read in a `sch` frame with a schematic open (island `v0.2.3-cc25`, on an engine with `kicadBusMigrationGet`, engine run 15; a host gates it on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `busMigration`, listed by `sch` frames only). On this engine KiCad's own Migrate Buses window never opens when a schematic is opened: the labels stay as the file holds them until the host applies names. No args. The checks, in order: the args (`bad_args`); the frame (`unsupported` "a bus op runs in a sch frame"); the engine (`not_ready`, `unsupported` naming `kicadBusMigrationGet`); a schematic open (`not_ready`); a load parked, a popup or a dialog up, or an item hanging off the pointer (`busy`). A read: answered during a `sheet.check`. No event: a host asks once after each successful `project.open` or `project.import` in a `sch` frame when the cap is listed.

The answer lists at most 1,024 buses, each with KiCad's own id for it, the sheet path it lies on, its labels (1 to 64, each its `uuid`, lowercase, and its `text`) and KiCad's proposals, one per label, by KiCad's own rule. A proposal is not always the name a reader wants (KiCad may propose `DATA[0..0]` for `DATA[0..3]`): a host offers them, it never applies one unasked. An empty list means every bus has one name. An engine answer of another shape is `island_error`.

### buses.migration.apply
Names the buses in a `sch` frame with a schematic open (island `v0.2.3-cc25`, on an engine with `kicadBusMigrationApply` and `kicadCollabBusy`, engine run 15; cap `busMigration`). The args are exactly `{ choices }`: 1 to 1,024 choices of exactly `{ labels, name }`, the labels 1 to 64 uuids as `buses.migration.get` answered them (no uuid twice in one request), the name 1 to 1,024 UTF-8 bytes with no line break and no NUL. The checks, in order: the args (`bad_args`); the frame; the engine (`not_ready`, `unsupported` naming the first missing export); a schematic open (`not_ready`); `busy` as for `buses.migration.get`, and during a check or an import.

The frame queues the run with a fresh `/tmp/cc-buses/<n>/` for its records, waits for the apply queue to empty and the run's record, and removes the folder whatever the answer. Every label is resolved first: one the schematic does not hold as a label answers `bus_label_unknown` with nothing changed. Else each label takes its choice's name as ONE commit "Name buses" (one undo step, where KiCad's own window makes none), the connections are worked out again, and an `ev.edited` follows once the depth moved. The answer counts the choices applied and the labels named; a `buses.migration.get` after it lists none of those buses. `not_applied` when the engine refused the run or no record came within about 30 s; `island_error` for a record of another shape.

### sim.userSignals

The reader's own signals over an ended run: KiCad's user defined signals (its simulator's `applyUserDefinedSignals`), worked out by ngspice with no KiCad window (island `v0.2.3-cc25`, on an engine with `kicadSimUserSignals`, engine run 15; a host gates it on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `simUserSignals`, listed in a `sch` frame exactly when the engine has that export).

- **The args**, read closed: `run` (a run number `sim.run` answered) and `signals`, 0 to 16 expressions, each 1 to 256 printable ASCII characters with no line break or other control character, no `;`, no quote (`"` or `'`), no backslash, no backtick and no brace. A `let` takes one expression, so nothing that passes can become a second command; anything else answers `bad_args` and nothing reaches the engine or ngspice. Name nets as KiCad's circuit names them, with the sheet path: `V(/out)-V(/in)`, `V(/out)*I(R1)`.
- **The checks**, in order: the args (`bad_args`); the frame (`unsupported`, "the simulator runs in a sch frame"); the engine (`not_ready` before `ev.ready`, `unsupported` naming `kicadSimUserSignals` or `kicadCollabBusy` when absent, as on engine run 14), a schematic open (`not_ready`), a load parked, a popup menu or a dialog up, or an item hanging off the pointer (`busy`); the simulator service (`unsupported` "ngspice_service.wasm" when the release serves none); `busy` while a run is live; `no_simulation` when the service was released or died since the run.
- **The run's plot.** The island asks the service for its plots and hands the engine each in turn; the engine takes only a plot the run's finish recorded and the service still holds (and queues nothing for any other), so the island never sends a plot of its own guess. When none is taken the run is no longer held (a newer run of its kind replaced it, or it made no plot) and the answer is `no_simulation`, as `sim.vectors` answers.
- **The work.** In a fresh folder under `/tmp/cc-sim/`, the engine makes ngspice's plot current and, for each signal `i`, clears `user<i>` and sends `let user<i> = <expression>` with the circuit's net names quoted by KiCad's own rule; its record `user-signals.json` is read once the engine is idle (`not_applied` past about 30 s) and the folder is removed. No undo step; the design is not marked modified.
- **The answer**: `vectors`, one `SimVector` per expression ngspice worked out, `name` `user<i>` (`i` its index in `signals`), `signal` the expression as sent, `trace` `other`, `complex` and `length` as ngspice holds it; `errors`, `{ index, line }` for each expression ngspice refused, `line` ngspice's first error line for it, else "ngspice made no vector for this expression" or "ngspice refused the expression". `no_simulation` when the engine found the plot gone at its turn; `sim_failed` with the engine's message when ngspice could not be asked.

### Tables (`tableCsv`)

Island `v0.2.3-cc25`, in a `sch` or a `pcb` frame. Our engine from run 15 (unit m12-e) asks the host in place of KiCad's Table Properties and Table Cell Properties windows, through the prompt host (Prompts, above), and builds a table's CSV with no window.

The table asks and their records (lengths in millimeters, colors `#rrggbbaa` lowercase or `""` for none, a stroke's `style` `solid`, `dash`, `dot`, `dashdot` or `dashdotdot`):

- `table` (Edit Table, `Ctrl+E` on a table, and a cell's own Edit Table press): `{ uuid, rows, cols, cells, border: { external, header, width, color, style }, separators: { rows, cols, width, color, style }, layer, locked }`. `cells` is the grid's text row by row, `rows` arrays of `cols` entries, a cell covered by a merged one `null`; a board table on a back layer arrives mirrored, as KiCad's grid shows it. A `width` is `null` when the stroke has none. `color` is always `""` on the board (its dialog has no colors); `layer` is the board's canonical layer name, `""` in a schematic; `locked` is always false in a schematic. Answer `{ cells, border, separators, layer, locked }`, any key left out or `null` keeps its value (a stroke block may carry only the keys it changes); KiCad refuses with the key it names (`cells`, `border.width`, `separators.style`, `layer`, `locked`) a cells array not `rows` by `cols`, a negative width, a color other than `""`, `#rrggbb` or `#rrggbbaa`, a style not in the list, a board color other than `""`, a schematic `layer` other than `""` or `locked` true, a board layer its selector does not offer. One accepted answer is one undo step.
- `tableCell` (Properties of one or more selected cells): `{ uuids, text, font, bold, italic, hAlign, vAlign, margins: { left, right, top, bottom }, size, width, height, thickness, autoThickness, textColor, fill: { mode, color } }`, every key always present. A value is `null` where the selected cells differ (KiCad's mixed state) or where it does not apply in this editor: `size`, `textColor` and `fill` are the schematic's, `width`, `height`, `thickness` and `autoThickness` the board's. `text` is `null` for several schematic cells. `font` is the font's name (`KiCad Font` or a system font), `hAlign` `left`, `center` or `right`, `vAlign` `top`, `center` or `bottom`, `fill.mode` `none` or `color`. Answer the same keys but `uuids`, each optional (`null` or left out keeps the value; a non null value for a key that does not apply in this editor is refused with that key), plus `editTable`: `true` is the dialog's own Edit Table press, after which KiCad asks `table` for the cell's table as a second, separate prompt. Each accepted answer is one undo step.

`table.csv { uuid }` answers `{ name, text }`: `text` the table's text as KiCad's own Export Table to CSV writes it (each cell's shown text, a field holding a comma, quote or line break quoted with its quotes doubled, fields joined by `,`, each row ended by a line break), `name` the project's name followed by `-table` (`table` when the project has none); the host saves it as `<name>.csv`. A read: nothing changes and no undo step is made, and it is answered during a `sheet.check` or `board.check`. Failures: `bad_args` (any key but `uuid`, or a `uuid` that is not one), `unsupported` (in another frame, or on an engine without `kicadTableCsv`, named), `not_ready` (no document open), `busy` (a load parked, a popup menu, a dialog or a prompt up, or a placed item on the pointer), `not_found` (no table of the document holds the uuid; `message` the uuid).

`ev.ready` lists `tableCsv` in a `sch` or `pcb` frame exactly when the engine has `kicadTableCsv`. While it is listed, KiCad's Export Table to CSV (`common.TableEditor.exportTableCSV`, which would open a file window) is held in both frames: its window never opens, the frame sends `ev.held`, and the host asks `table.csv` for the selected table and saves the file itself. On an older engine (run 14 and before) the cap is absent, the action keeps no door in our menu, and `table.csv` answers `unsupported` naming `kicadTableCsv`.

### embedded.get, embedded.set and embedded.extract

The files a design keeps inside its own board or schematic file (KiCad's embedded files: fonts, 3D models, drawing sheets, datasheets and others), for the host's Setup section Embedded files (island `v0.2.3-cc25`, on an engine with `kicadEmbeddedFilesGet`, `kicadEmbeddedFilesSet`, `kicadEmbeddedFileExtract` and `kicadCollabBusy`, engine run 15; a host gates them on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `designEmbedded`, listed by a `sch` and a `pcb` frame). On an older engine the cap is absent and each op answers `unsupported` naming the export (`kicadEmbeddedFilesGet` first). Any other frame answers `unsupported`. Each op works on the document of its frame: the board in a `pcb` frame, the schematic in a `sch` frame.

- `embedded.get` answers the engine's read: `owner` the document's kind, `fontsEmbedded` whether the document keeps its fonts inside, `files` every embedded file (at most 1,024: `name`, `type` KiCad's word, `bytes` its size decompressed, `heldBy` how many footprints or library symbols also carry a file of that name), `fonts` the outline fonts the document draws with and whether each is kept. It is answered during a `sheet.check` or a `board.check`.
- `embedded.set` changes them, with no undo step (KiCad's Setup makes none) and the document marked modified, so the next `project.save` writes the change (after `fonts` with `embed` true, KiCad's writer carries the fonts). `add` writes `bytes` (1 byte to 25 MiB) into one fresh `/tmp/cc-embed/<n>/` and keeps the file under `name` (1 to 255 characters, no slash or backslash, never `.` or `..`, and never `embedded.json` or `.cc-embed-out.json`, the names of the engine's own records, which answer `bad_args`) as `type`, a file of that name replaced; `bad_args` "embedded.set: KiCad refused the file" when the engine refuses it. `remove` takes the named file out, with `alsoHeld` from the footprints or symbols that carry it as well (KiCad's "Delete from footprints as well?"); `not_found` names a file the document does not hold, and nothing is sent then. `fonts` sets whether the document keeps its fonts inside; with `embed` true the fonts it draws with are added now. The answer is the read (as `embedded.get`) once the engine's record is there and the read shows the change; `not_applied` when the engine refuses a remove or a fonts set, its record says so, the change does not show, or the record does not come within 30 s. The folder is removed after every answer.
- `embedded.extract` answers the named file's bytes as the document holds them, decompressed (the engine writes them into one fresh `/tmp/cc-embed-out/<n>/`, removed after the answer; the buffer is transferred). A file named `.cc-embed-out.json`, the extract's own record, is never taken out: `bad_args`. `not_found` names a file the document does not hold, `too_large` names one past 25 MiB, `not_applied` when the engine refuses the extract or its record says it failed (`message` "embedded.extract: " and the engine's word). It is answered during a check.
- The checks, in order, for each: the args (`bad_args`), the frame, the engine (`not_ready`, `unsupported`), a document open (`not_ready`), busy as for a board op in a `pcb` frame and a schematic op in a `sch` frame (and `busy` while the engine cannot read the document's files).

### Prompts (`prompts`)

Island `v0.2.3-cc25`. Our engine from run 15 (kicad `cc/main` K-PROMPT, `include/host_prompt.h`) asks the frame before a hooked dialog of KiCad's shows, in every editor frame (`sch`, `pcb`, `symedit`, `fpedit`, `ds`). The frame takes the ask while `ev.ready` listed the pseudo-cap `prompts`, a document is open, no `project.import` runs, the host is connected, and an island unit reads the ask's record (an ask no unit reads, or a record of another shape, is declined); KiCad then waits in its own modal wait (a modal ask) or keeps its dialog open unseen (a modeless one), and the frame sends `ev.prompt`. Otherwise KiCad's dialog shows as before. `ev.ready` lists `prompts` exactly when the engine has `kicadHostPrompts`, `kicadPromptAnswer`, `kicadPromptCancel` and `kicadPromptPick`; on an older engine (run 14 and before) the cap is absent and every op below answers `unsupported` naming the export.

The generic asks and their records (lengths in millimeters, angles in degrees, layers by canonical name, as the board records):

- `entry` (KiCad's unit, point, text and multi value entry dialogs: Fillet Tracks, Fillet and Chamfer Lines, Simplify and Heal Shapes, Dogbone Corners, Move Corner To and Move Midpoint To, Grid Origin, the microwave values): `{ title, fields: [{ key, label, kind, value, tip }] }`, `kind` `length`, `angle`, `text`, `check` or `point` (`value` `{ x, y }`), `key` `value` for a one value dialog, `point` for the point dialog, `f0`, `f1` and on for the multi dialog. Answer `{ values: { <key>: number | string | boolean | { x, y } } }`; a key left out keeps the dialog's value.
- `layer` (KiCad's one layer chooser: Select Layer and Place Via, Create Tracks from a selection off copper): `{ current, layers: [{ canonical, name, color }] }`, the layers the dialog allows, `color` `#rrggbbaa`. Answer `{ layer }`.
- `message` (KiCad's message boxes and confirmations: the locked item confirmations of Drag and Break Track, errors and notes): `{ title, text, extra, icon, buttons: [{ id, label }], dontAsk }`, `icon` `info`, `warning`, `error` or `question`, `id` `ok`, `cancel`, `yes` or `no`, buttons in KiCad's order with KiCad's labels, `dontAsk` true when KiCad offers its "do not show again". Answer `{ button, dontAsk }`. A message KiCad raises while an answer is applied is never asked: it is that answer's refusal `message`.
- `assignNetclass` (Assign Netclass, both editors): `{ pattern, netclass, netclasses }`. Answer `{ pattern, netclass }`, either left out keeps the dialog's value.

The placement, convert, zone and table asks (`moveExact`, `offset`, `array`, `positionRelative`, `filterSelection`, `findFootprint`, `convert`, `outset`, `zoneSettings`, `table`, `tableCell`) are declared, read and described by their own units.

`prompt.answer { token, ...answer }` gives the parked prompt `token` its answer: the ask's answer keys beside `token` (`{ token: 7, values: { value: 1 } }`). KiCad writes the values into its dialog's own controls and runs its own checks and transfer. It answers `{ accepted: true }` (a modal dialog closes as with its OK and the edit is one undo step; a modeless one may stay open, as KiCad's Apply), or `{ accepted: false, field, message }`: KiCad refused it, `field` the answer key it names (empty when none) and `message` KiCad's own sentence (empty when none); the prompt stays parked for another answer. An accepted answer is followed by a read of the selection, as a pointer release in the frame is: an answer that changes it inside the engine (Filter Selected Items drops the kinds left off) raises no canvas event, and `ev.selected` follows when it changed. Failures: `stale` (no prompt parked with that token; the host closes its card), `bad_args` (no token, or keys or types this unit's ask does not take). `prompt.cancel { token }` closes it with nothing changed, as KiCad's Cancel: `{}` or `stale`. `prompt.pick { token, kind }` runs the dialog's own picker (`item` or `point`, the placement asks only), the result arriving as `ev.promptUpdate`: `{}`, `stale` or `no_picker`; the engine's answer is awaited (the picker is a tool of KiCad's, and an engine may start it inside the call), and an engine that cannot start it there answers `island_error` naming the op and the browser's own message (engine run 15 build T16b). The three are answered as they arrive, outside the one-at-a-time order (KiCad is parked in the prompt, so a queued request would wait on it forever); no `ev.promptEnd` follows them.

While a modal prompt is parked the frame counts it as a dialog over the canvas: `key.press`, `view.fit` and `action.run` answer `busy`, `project.save` answers `busy`, the picker waits, and `ev.menu` stays quiet (nothing of KiCad's is drawn). A modeless prompt leaves the canvas to the reader. `project.forget` ends every parked prompt (`ev.promptEnd` `gone`) before it forgets the document.

KiCad's Clearance Resolution and Constraints Resolution reports (`pcbnew.InspectionTool.InspectClearance`, `pcbnew.InspectionTool.InspectConstraints`) are held in a `pcb` frame under the `rules` cap: their window never opens, the frame sends `ev.held`, and the host opens the board form's Rules block (`board.rules`) for the selection.

### Board commands: the board's own windows asked of the host, and board corners (`vertices`)

Island `v0.2.3-cc25`, in a `pcb` frame (unit m3-i). Our engine from run 15 asks the host in place of three of KiCad's board windows, through the prompt host (Prompts, above), and reads and moves a board item's corners with no window.

The board asks and their records (lengths in millimeters, layers KiCad's canonical names):

- `convert` (KiCad's conversion settings, before Create Polygon, Zone, Rule Area or Lines from a selection): `{ strategies, strategy, gap, lineWidth, deleteOriginals }`. `strategies` the choices KiCad's dialog shows, in its order, from `copyLineWidth`, `centerline`, `boundingHull` (`[]` for Create Lines from a polygon, which shows none); `strategy` the selected one, which may be one the dialog hides; `gap` and `lineWidth` (the bounding hull's) 0 or more. Answer the same keys but `strategies`, each optional; a strategy not in `strategies` is refused with field `strategy`. A zone or a rule area made by the conversion asks `zoneSettings` after it, and is made with the conversion's last settings.
- `outset` (Create Outsets): `{ outsetDistance, roundCorners, useSourceLayers, useSourceWidths, layer, layers, lineWidth, gridRounding, deleteSourceItems }`. `layer` the selected layer, `layers` the layers the dialog offers (the board's enabled layers in KiCad's order), `gridRounding` the rounding grid or `null` when rounding is off. Answer the same keys but `layers`, each optional: `layer` one of `layers`; `gridRounding` `null` turns rounding off, a length over 0 turns it on; a `lineWidth` of 0 or less is refused with field `lineWidth` and KiCad's sentence "Line width must be a positive value."
- `zoneSettings` (a NEW zone: one drawn, one made by Convert, Duplicate Zone onto Layer; an existing zone's properties stay `ev.boardProperties` for copper and `ev.boardItem` for the other kinds): `{ kind, sheet }`. `kind` `copper` (the board editor only: a new copper zone in the footprint editor shows KiCad's window), `nonCopper` or `ruleArea`. For `copper` the `sheet` is the copper area of `board.item.get` (`kind: "zone"`, `locked`, `name`, `net`, `layers`, `priority`, `clearance`, `minWidth`, `padConnection`, `thermalGap`, `spokeWidth`, `netChoices`) with `uuid` `""`; for the other two it is the `zone` item record of `fpedit.items.get` (`ruleArea` false and true) with `uuid` `""`. Answer `{ set }`: for `copper` `board.item.set`'s zone keys plus `layers` (a non empty list of copper layers the board enables, as Duplicate onto a layer needs), each refused by its own key as `field`; for the other two the zone record's set keys. A canceled prompt makes no zone.

A record of any other shape is not taken, and KiCad's own window shows as before.

- **`board.vertices.get`** (exactly `{ uuid }`, any case): the item's corners as KiCad's vertex editor lists them: `kind` `copper` (a copper zone), `nonCopper` (a zone on no copper layer), `ruleArea` or `polygon` (a polygon shape); `outlines` every closed line of the item, each outline followed by its holes, `hole` `-1` for the outline itself and else the hole's index in that outline, each at least three corners, in board millimeters (no origin moved); in an `fpedit` frame the item is one of the open footprint's, in the footprint editor's millimeters. A read: nothing changes and it is answered during a `board.check`.
- **`board.vertices.set`** (exactly `{ uuid, outline, hole, index, x, y }`): moves ONE corner to `(x, y)` (mm, within 50,000), as KiCad's vertex editor commits one ("Edit Vertex"): one undo step, a zone unfilled and marked for refill. The corner must be one the item holds (read first: `bad_args` names the missing outline, hole or corner, and nothing is sent). Answered with the item read back, as `board.vertices.get` answers it; `not_applied` when the engine refused the move or the read back does not hold the corner there. On engine build T17 the engine queues the move on the frame's tool coroutine (a move made at once trapped while the item was selected) and answers `{"queued":true}`: the frame waits for the engine's apply queue (5 s at most, else `not_applied`), then reads the item back (`not_found` when it is gone by then); the answer is the same as before, only later. A corner already where it is sent is answered at once on every engine. Moving a corner onto itself makes no undo step. An `ev.edited` follows.
- Failures of both: `bad_args` (args of another shape), `unsupported` (in another frame, "board corners are read in a pcb or an fpedit frame", or on an engine without `kicadBoardVerticesGet` or `kicadBoardVerticesSet`, named), `not_ready` (no board open; in an `fpedit` frame no footprint open), `busy` (a load parked, a popup menu, a dialog or a prompt up, or a placed item on the pointer; `board.vertices.set` also while a `board.check` runs), `not_found` (the board, or the open footprint, holds no zone, rule area or polygon of that uuid; `message` the uuid).
- **Held**: `ev.ready` lists `vertices` in a `pcb` frame and in an `fpedit` frame exactly when the engine has both exports (the footprint editor's engine reads corners from engine fix e10-3, builds T9 and later). While it is listed, KiCad's Edit Corners (`pcbnew.InteractiveEdit.editVertices`, which would open KiCad's vertex editor pane) is held in that frame: the pane never opens, the frame sends `ev.held`, and the host opens its Corners block over `board.vertices.get` (the board form's on a board, the footprint form's in the footprint editor). On an older engine (run 14 and before) the cap is absent, the action keeps no door in our menu, and both ops answer `unsupported` naming `kicadBoardVerticesGet`.

### Board item records, the tuning tool and pictures: board.item.get full, board.item.set records, board.tuning, board.image.place

Island `v0.2.3-cc25`, in a `pcb` frame, on engine run 15 (unit m4-i). A host gates each part on `ev.ready` `caps`: `boardItemKinds` (the engine has `kicadHostBoardItems`, `kicadBoardItemRecords`, `kicadBoardItemRecordsSet`, `kicadBoardItemGet` and `kicadCollabBusy`) for `full`, the record form and `ev.boardItem`; `boardTuning` (`kicadBoardTuningGet`, `kicadBoardTuningSet`) for the tuning ops; `boardImagePlace` (`kicadBoardPlaceImage`) for the picture. On an engine without them (run 14) none is listed, `board.tuning.*` and `board.image.place` answer `unsupported` naming the first missing export, `full` is ignored and the record form answers `unsupported` naming `kicadBoardItemRecordsSet`. In any other frame the new ops answer `unsupported` ("a board item op runs in a pcb frame").

- **`board.item.get` with `full: true`** (exactly `{ uuids, full: true }`): every uuid asked whose item is of a record kind answers its `BoardRecordSheet` (below) in place of today's `{ kind: "other" }` (a pad answers its own record, not its footprint); every other uuid answers as today, in the order asked. A record the island cannot read (one the closed readers of `bridge/itemRecords.ts` refuse) stays as today's answer, and a set on it answers `island_error`. `full` is honored only under `boardItemKinds`: an older host never sends it, so it never meets a kind its parser refuses.
- **`board.item.set`, the record form** (exactly `{ uuids, kind, set }`, beside today's `{ uuid, set }`): `uuids` 1 to 4,096 lower case, none twice, every one held (`not_found` names the first) and of `kind` (`bad_args`); `kind` a record kind but `tablecell` (the table's own commands); `set` a non empty partial of that kind's keys, only keys every item has: the footprint editor's item set for its kind (the `fpedit.items.set` section) plus, for a pad, `net` (a net the board holds, `""` for none; `bad_args` "the board has no net ..."); a group `name`, `locked`, `designBlock` (`"lib:name"` or `""`); a target `shape`, `size` (1 mil to 1,000 mm), `width`, `x`, `y`, `layer`; a tuning pattern every `TuningSettings` key but `mode` and `fromRules`. A kind the island reads no record of answers `unsupported`. ONE undo step "Edit" over every item, run in a fresh folder under `/tmp/cc-boarditems/` (the engine's record `items.json`, `{ run, applied, depth }`); KiCad's own rules refuse what its dialogs refuse (`bad_args`, nothing changed: the commit is reverted whole if any item refuses) and reset what they reset (a pad's shape as the pad dialog). The answer `{ items: BoardRecordSheet[] }` read back once it applied; past about 5 s `not_applied`. An `ev.edited` follows.
- **`board.tuning.get`**: the tuning tool's live settings while KiCad's track tuning tool runs, else `no_tuning`. Answered during a check (a read).
- **`board.tuning.set`**: exactly `{ set }`: amplitudes and spacing 0 to 1,000 mm, `cornerStyle`, `cornerRadiusPercent` a whole 0 to 100, the booleans, `target` replaced whole (`{ min?, opt?, max? }`, each 0 to 2,000 or null, `min` and `max` only beside an `opt`; mm, or ps while `timeDomain`; the other domain's target left unconstrained). Handed to the tool's own loop, which applies it as its dialog's OK applies it (the pattern being drawn is not committed: no undo step) and records the settings it then holds: the answer. `no_tuning` when no tuning tool runs or it ended before the set applied; `bad_args` when the engine refuses it.
- **`board.image.place`**: `name` a file name (no folder), `bytes` a PNG or a JPEG by its first bytes, at most 4 MiB. Staged under a fresh `/tmp/cc-stage/<n>/` (removed after the answer), then KiCad's own reference image tool runs with it, the file box skipped: `{}` once the picture hangs on the pointer on the active layer; the reader's press places it (KiCad's "Place Image", one undo step). `bad_args` when KiCad places nothing (another tool on its stack, a file it reads as no picture).
- **Held**: `pcbnew.LengthTuner.Settings` (Ctrl+L) in a `pcb` frame under `boardItemKinds`: `ev.held` with that action; the host opens its Tuning form on `board.tuning.get`.

The records (mm, degrees, positions the board's absolute ones, Y down; a string at most 1,024 characters unless named; any other shape is `island_error`):

- `BoardRecordSheet`: `{ kind: "record", uuid, parent, parentRef, net, netChoices, item }`. `parent` the footprint's uuid and `parentRef` its designator, both `""` for an item of the board itself; `net` a pad's net (`""` none), `null` for every other kind; `netChoices` every net of the board sorted for a pad, `[]` otherwise; `item` the item's own record, by its own `kind`.
- The footprint editor's kinds (`pad`, `shape`, `dimension`, `textbox`, `table`, `barcode`, `image`, `zone`: a rule area, a keepout or a zone on no copper layer, `point`): exactly the records of `fpedit.items.get`, but a pad's position is the board's and its `angle` relative to its footprint (as KiCad's pad dialog shows them).
- `group`: `{ kind: "group", uuid, name, locked, members, designBlock }` (`members` its item count, `designBlock` `"lib:name"` or `""`).
- `target`: `{ kind: "target", uuid, layer, x, y, shape: "plus" | "x", size, width }`.
- `tuning` (a placed pattern): `TuningSettings` plus `kind: "tuning"`, `uuid`, `layer`, as its dialog shows them (the rules' target while the pattern does not override them).
