- `TuningSettings`: `{ mode: "single" | "diffPair" | "skew" (read only), minAmplitude, maxAmplitude, spacing, cornerStyle: "chamfer" | "round", cornerRadiusPercent, singleSided, timeDomain, overrideCustomRules, target: { min, opt, max }, fromRules: { min, opt, max } | null (read only) }`, each target number or null (all three null when unconstrained; the skew target in skew mode).

### Schematic items: sch.item.get, sch.item.set, sch.image.place

Every schematic item that is not a symbol, read and changed with no KiCad window, in a `sch` frame with a schematic open (island `v0.2.3-cc25`, on an engine with `kicadSchItemGet`, `kicadSchItemSet`, `kicadHostSchItems` and `kicadCollabBusy`, engine run 15; a host gates `sch.item.get`, `sch.item.set` and `ev.schItem` on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `schItem`, and `sch.image.place` on `schImagePlace` (the engine's `kicadSchPlaceImage` and `kicadCollabGetSelection`); both listed by `sch` frames only). Positions and lengths are millimeters as the schematic stores them, Y down; colors are `""` (KiCad's default) or `#rrggbbaa`; uuids lower case 8-4-4-4-12; a sheet path is `symbol.set`'s form, or `""` in a request for the sheet shown.

- The checks, in order, every one before any effect: the args (`bad_args`); the frame's kind (`unsupported`, "a schematic edits in a sch frame"); the engine (`not_ready` before `ev.ready`; `unsupported` naming the first missing export); a schematic opened by `project.open` or `project.import` (`not_ready`); a load parked, a popup menu or a dialog up, or an item hanging off the pointer (`busy`, nothing changed).
- **Records.** `SchItemKind` is one of `label`, `wire`, `line`, `busEntry`, `junction`, `text`, `textbox`, `shape`, `ruleArea`, `image`, `sheet`, `sheetPin`, `table`, `tableCell`, `group`. Every record carries `{ kind, uuid, sheetPath }` and exactly its kind's keys:
  - `label`: `labelKind` (`local`, `global`, `hierarchical`, `directive`), `text`, `x`, `y`, `orientation` (`right`, `up`, `left`, `down`), `size`, `bold`, `italic`, `color`, `shape` (null for a local label; `input`, `output`, `bidirectional`, `tri_state`, `passive` for a global or hierarchical one; `dot`, `round`, `diamond`, `rectangle` for a directive), `pinLength` (a directive's flag length, null otherwise), `fields` (at most 32 of `{ name, value, visible, x, y }`: a global label's Intersheetrefs, a directive's Netclass and Component Class).
  - `wire`: `bus` (read only), `points` (`[[x, y], [x, y]]`), `width`, `style` (`default`, `solid`, `dash`, `dot`, `dash_dot`, `dash_dot_dot`), `color`. `line` (a graphic line): the same less `bus`. `busEntry`: `x`, `y`, `size` (`[x, y]`, read only), `width`, `style`, `color`. `junction`: `x`, `y`, `diameter` (0: the default), `color`.
  - `text`, `textbox`, `shape`: the symbol editor's records (`symedit.items.get`) with `unit` and `bodyStyle` 0, plus `sheetPath` and, on a text or text box, `color` (the text's); a text's, text box's or table cell's `text` is read at any length KiCad holds, up to 16,384 characters (KiCad sets none), a record past that refusing the read. `ruleArea`: a shape's keys with the kind `ruleArea`.
  - `image`: `x`, `y`, `scale`, `bytes` (the picture's size as KiCad keeps it, read only), `png` (its base64, null past 64 KiB).
  - `sheet`: `name`, `file` (relative to the file of the sheet that holds it), `page`, `x`, `y`, `size` (`[w, h]`), `borderWidth`, `borderColor`, `fillColor`, `inBom`, `onBoard`, `inSim`, `dnp`, `fields` (its user fields, at most 32 of `{ name, value, visible }`), `pins` (a count, read only).
  - `sheetPin`: `sheet` (its sheet's uuid), `name`, `shape` (a port shape), `side` (`left`, `right`, `top`, `bottom`), `size`, `x`, `y`.
  - `table` (read only here): `rows`, `cols`, `cells` (every cell's text, row by row), `borderExternal`, `borderHeader`, `border` (`{ width, style, color }`), `separatorsRows`, `separatorsCols`, `separators`, `colWidths`, `rowHeights`. `tableCell` (read only here): a text box's keys and `color`, plus `table` (its table's uuid), `row`, `col`, `rowSpan`, `colSpan`.
  - `group`: `name`, `members` (a count), `designBlock` (its design block's id, `""` for none).
- **`sch.item.get`** answers the records of 1 to 512 items asked, in the order asked; an item the schematic does not hold is left out. With `sheetPath` `""` the item on the sheet shown, else on the first path holding it. A read: answered while a `sheet.check` runs, no undo step.
- **`sch.item.set`** sets `set`, a non empty partial of one kind's editable keys, on 1 to 4,096 items (no uuid twice), all of `kind`. Editable: a label's every key but `kind`, `uuid` and `sheetPath`, its `fields` as at most 32 `{ name, value?, visible? }` (a named field updated, another name a user field added); a wire's and a line's `points`, `width`, `style`, `color`; a bus entry's `width`, `style`, `color`; a junction's `diameter`, `color`; a text's, text box's, shape's and rule area's keys as `symedit.items.set` takes them (`unit` and `bodyStyle` only 0) and a text's or text box's `color`; a picture's `x`, `y`, `scale` (0.001 to 1,000) and `png` (base64 of a PNG or JPEG of at most 4 MiB, which replaces it: `too_large` past it); a sheet's every key but `pins`, `fields` as a label's; a sheet pin's `name`, `shape`, `side`, `size`; a group's `name`. A table or a table cell is never set here (`bad_args`, "a table is read only here"). Text is at most 1,024 characters with no control character, a page number 1 to 64, a size above 0.
  - The items are read first: `not_found` (`message` the uuid) for the first the schematic does not hold, `bad_args` when one is of another kind or the set does not fit it (`message` why: a label shape its kind after the set has no word for, a flag length on a label that is not a directive, a point count its shape does not take, a file change on more than one sheet).
  - `labelKind` runs KiCad's own change label kind: the label keeps its net; KiCad gives the converted label a NEW uuid, listed in `replaced` as `{ from, to }` and answered under it. A label kind or a sheet file changes only on the sheet shown (`bad_args` otherwise).
  - A sheet's `file` (relative, ending `.kicad_sch`, no `..` and no backslash) is KiCad's sheet file change with the page's answer: `fileMode` `useExisting` links the sheet to that file as it is, `rename` saves this sheet's content under the new name and keeps the old file. A `file` that exists (on disk or shown by another sheet) asked without `fileMode` answers `bad_args` "fileMode is required: the file exists", nothing changed; a new name without it is a `rename`. The change is listed in `renamed` and is outside the undo list, as KiCad's own; a step KiCad refuses answers `sheet_file`.
  - Every other key is ONE commit "Edit": one undo step and an `ev.edited`. The answer is the items read back in the order asked, with `renamed` and `replaced`. The frame queues the set with a fresh `/tmp/cc-schitems/<n>/` for its record (`items.json`), waits for the apply queue to empty and the record, and removes the folder whatever the answer: `not_applied` when no record came within about 5 s or KiCad applied the set to fewer items than asked.
- **`sch.image.place`** takes a picture, `bytes` a PNG or a JPEG (at most 4 MiB: `too_large`) and `name` its file name (1 to 255 characters, no slash, backslash or control character), writes it into a fresh `/tmp/cc-schimage/<n>/`, hands it to KiCad's own image tool and answers `{}` once it hangs on the pointer. The reader's click places it as KiCad's one undo step (then an `ev.edited`); Esc drops it. While it hangs every gated op answers `busy`, as after `place`. `not_applied` when KiCad cannot read the picture, `busy` when its tool did not take it within about 10 s.
- The fixture `tests/fixtures/items-sch/` (a root with one of every kind and a sheet `sub`, and a spare sheet file) is read by `tests/e2e/sch-items.spec.ts` (its run 15 half on cc25).

### Placement windows asked of the host, and the selection filter (`selectFilter`)

Island `v0.2.3-cc25`, in a `sch` or `pcb` frame (unit m6-i). Our engine from run 15 asks the host in place of six of KiCad's placement windows, through the prompt host (Prompts, above), and reads and writes KiCad's selection filter with no window.

The placement asks and their records (lengths in millimeters, angles in degrees):

- `moveExact` (Move Exactly, modal): `{ x, y, rotation, anchor, anchors }`. `x` and `y` the move (Cartesian; a polar entry is the card's own), `rotation` the turn, `anchors` the rotation anchors the window offers in its order, from `item`, `selectionCenter`, `userOrigin`, `drillOrigin` (the last in the board editor only), `anchor` the chosen one. Answer `{ x?, y?, rotation?, anchor? }`: an anchor not offered is refused with field `anchor`; a move past KiCad's largest board is refused with field `x` (or `y`) and KiCad's sentence; `rotation` within 3,600 degrees. KiCad keeps the last answer for the next ask.
- `offset` (Interactive Offset, modal, after its two clicks): `{ x, y }`, the measured vector. Answer `{ x?, y? }`.
- `array` (Create Array, modeless): `{ shape, arrangeSelection, uniqueReferences, padNumbering, grid, circular }`. `shape` `grid` or `circular`; `padNumbering` true in the footprint editor only; `grid` `{ nx, ny, dx, dy, offsetX, offsetY, centered, stagger, staggerRows, horizontalThenVertical, reverseNumberingAlternate, numberingStartSpecified, numbering2d, primary, secondary }`; `circular` `{ centerX, centerY, angle, angleOffset, clockwise, count, rotateItems, numberingStartSpecified, axis }` (the center in board coordinates); an axis `{ type, start, step }`, `type` `numeric`, `hex`, `alphaShort` (no I, O, S, Q, X, Z) or `alphaFull`, `start` text, `step` a whole number. Answer any of the record's keys, `grid` and `circular` each with any of their own: a bad key refuses with its own name as field, a bad value inside `grid` or `circular` with field `grid` or `circular`. A given circular `angle` clears KiCad's full circle. An accepted answer makes the array in ONE undo step and ends the prompt with no `ev.promptEnd` (`{ accepted: true }` closes the card). `prompt.pick` `{ kind: "point" }` or `{ kind: "item" }` runs KiCad's own picker for the center; the new record arrives as `ev.promptUpdate`.
- `positionRelative` (Position Relative, modeless): `{ anchor: { kind, x, y, label }, x, y }`. `anchor.kind` `item`, `point`, `gridOrigin` or `userOrigin`, at `x`, `y`, with KiCad's anchor text `label`; `x`, `y` the offset. Answer `{ x?, y?, anchor? }`: `anchor` `gridOrigin` or `userOrigin` alone sets the anchor and keeps the prompt, the new record sent as `ev.promptUpdate`; an answer with `x` or `y` moves the selection (one undo step) and ENDS the prompt, as KiCad 10's OK hides its window (a second move needs the command again). `prompt.pick` `{ kind: "item" }` or `{ kind: "point" }` runs KiCad's own picker; the result arrives as `ev.promptUpdate`.
- `filterSelection` (Filter Selected Items, modal): `{ includeFootprints, includeLockedFootprints, includeTracks, includeVias, includeZones, includeItemsOnTechLayers, includeBoardOutlineLayer, includePcbTexts }`, booleans. Answer any of them, booleans only (a non boolean refused with its key as field); the locked footprints box follows the footprints box, as in KiCad.
- `findFootprint` (Get and Move Footprint, modal): `{ references }`, the references KiCad lists, in its order. Answer `{ reference }`, matched without regard to case; one the list lacks is refused with field `reference`. The footprint then hangs on the pointer (KiCad's move), placed by the next click.

A record of any other shape is not taken, and KiCad's own window shows as before.

- **`selectFilter.get`** (no args): the selection filter of the frame's editor, KiCad's Selection Filter pane's switches: in a `pcb` frame `lockedItems`, `footprints`, `text`, `tracks`, `vias`, `pads`, `graphics`, `zones`, `keepouts`, `dimensions`, `points`, `otherItems`; in a `sch` frame `lockedItems`, `symbols`, `text`, `wires`, `labels`, `pins`, `graphics`, `images`, `ruleAreas`, `otherItems`; each a boolean. A read: answered during a check.
- **`selectFilter.set`** (exactly `{ filter }`): sets the named switches and keeps the others; answered with the filter read back. A switch of the other editor, none at all or a non boolean answers `bad_args` and nothing is sent. No undo step, no modified flag; the filter lives in the engine (each engine holds its own, as KiCad does per editor) and is never saved with the design. `not_applied` when the engine refused the set or the read back does not hold it.
- Failures of both: `bad_args`, `unsupported` (in another frame, "the selection filter is read in a sch or pcb frame", or on an engine without `kicadSelectionFilterGet` or `kicadSelectionFilterSet`, named), `not_ready` (no document open), `busy` (a load parked, a popup menu, a dialog or a prompt up, or a placed item on the pointer).
- `ev.ready` lists `selectFilter` in a `sch` or `pcb` frame exactly when the engine has both exports. On an older engine (run 14 and before) the cap is absent and both ops answer `unsupported` naming `kicadSelectionFilterGet`.

### board.tuningSolve
KiCad's Tuning Profiles panel's work out for one layer row, with no KiCad window: the track width, the pair's gap or the delay KiCad's transmission line calculators give for a target impedance over the board's own stackup (island `v0.2.3-cc25`, on an engine with `kicadTuningSolve`, engine run 15, merged image only; a host gates it on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `tuningSolve`, a `pcb` frame only).

- The args are `type` (`single` or `differential`), `impedance` (ohms over 0, at most 1,000,000; a pair's differential impedance), `signalLayer` and the optional `topRef` and `bottomRef` (canonical copper layer names, `F.Cu`, `In1.Cu` to `In30.Cu`, `B.Cu`; a microstrip on `F.Cu` reads `bottomRef`, on `B.Cu` `topRef`, a stripline both), `solveFor` (`width`, `gap` or `delay`) and the optional `width` and `gap` in mm (over 0, at most 1,000). A gap, asked or given, is a pair's only.
- The answer is `{ width, gap, delay, problem }`: `width` and `gap` in mm, `delay` in ps/cm (KiCad's unit of the panel's Delay column) rounded to 4 places, each null when not worked out; `problem` is `""`, or KiCad's own message with every figure null when the row cannot be worked out ("Diff pair gap must be greater than 0 to calculate width").
- The checks, in order: the args (`bad_args`), then as for every board op (a `sch` frame answers `unsupported`; the engine with `kicadTuningSolve`, else `unsupported` naming it; a board open; `busy`). The engine answers `bad_args` for a layer the board does not enable or one that is no copper layer. A read: no record, no undo step, no `ev.edited`; answered during a check.

### settings.read
The setup of ANOTHER design, read by KiCad and applied to nothing, in a `sch` or `pcb` frame with a document open (island `v0.2.3-cc25`, on an engine with `kicadSettingsRead` and `kicadCollabBusy`, engine run 15; a host gates it on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `settingsRead`, listed by `sch` and `pcb` frames only on an engine with both). The args are exactly `{ files, sections }`: `files` 1 to 3 of exactly `{ name, bytes }`, the file's plain name (1 to 255 bytes of UTF-8, no slash, backslash or control character, never `.` or `..`, a stem before its extension) and its bytes (a `Uint8Array`), exactly one `.kicad_pro`, at most one `.kicad_pcb` and at most one `.kicad_dru`, 25 MiB together; `sections` 1 to 64 unique names of the sections that travel, KiCad's Import Settings options: the board's `layers`, `stackup`, `finish`, `zoneHatchOffsets`, `textDefaults`, `zoneDefaults`, `boardFormatting`, `constraints`, `predefinedSizes`, `teardrops`, `tuningPatterns`, `netClasses`, `componentClasses`, `tuningProfiles`, `customRules`, `checkSeverities`, and the schematic's `schFormatting`, `annotation`, `fieldTemplates`, `bomPresets`, `ercSeverities`, `pinMap`, `netClasses`, `busAliases`, `textVariables` (the page, the variants, the embedded files, the drawing sheet and the preferences never travel). The checks, in order: the args (`bad_args`: no project file, a name with a folder, two files of a kind, another extension, a section that does not travel); the frame (`unsupported` "settings.read runs in a sch or pcb frame"); the engine (`not_ready`, `unsupported` naming the first missing export); a document open (`not_ready`); a load parked, a popup or a dialog up, or an item hanging off the pointer (`busy`), and during a check or an import.

The frame writes the files under a fresh `/tmp/cc-settings-read/<n>/`, outside the project folder (the rules file beside the board under the board's stem, as KiCad reads `<board>.kicad_dru`; no rules file without a board), and queues the read: KiCad loads the other project with its own project loader and its board with its own board reader, reads each asked section with the same readers `settings.get` uses, and unloads them again. Nothing of the open design changes: no undo step, no `ev.edited`, the design's own `settings.get` answers unchanged. The folder is removed whatever the answer.

The answer is `{ sections, missing }`: each asked name either a key of `sections`, its value in the shape `settings.get` answers for that section, or in `missing`, a section the other design does not hold or this frame does not read (a board section in a `sch` frame or with no board given, a schematic section in a `pcb` frame, `customRules` with no rules file). A host applies what it takes with `settings.set`, one section at a time, asking each frame for its own. `unreadable` when KiCad could not load the project or the board (`message` KiCad's first line, the run folder taken out, at most 512 characters); `not_applied` when the engine refused the read or no record came within 60 s; `island_error` for a record of another shape.

### sheet.pins.get
Each sheet's pins against the hierarchical labels of its own file, read in a `sch` frame with a schematic open (island `v0.2.3-cc25`, on an engine with `kicadSheetPinsGet`, engine run 15; a host gates it on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `syncPins`, listed by `sch` frames only on an engine with `kicadSheetPinsGet`, `kicadSheetPinsApply`, `kicadSheetPinsPlace`, `kicadCollabBusy` and `kicadCollabGetSelection`). No args (every sheet of the design), or exactly `{ sheets }`: 1 to 4,096 of exactly `{ uuid, sheetPath }`, the sheet's uuid (lowercase) and the path the sheet sits on, as a read answers them; a sheet asked that the design does not hold is left out. The checks, in order: the args (`bad_args`); the frame (`unsupported` "a sheet pins op runs in a sch frame"); the engine (`not_ready`, `unsupported` naming `kicadSheetPinsGet`); a schematic open (`not_ready`); a load parked, a popup or a dialog up, or an item hanging off the pointer (`busy`). A read: answered during a `sheet.check`.

One row per sheet instance: `sheet` its uuid, `sheetPath` the path it sits on (`/` for the root, then `/<uuid>/` per level), `name` its sheet name, `file` its file, and `pairs` (at most 4,096; `truncated` true when a sheet had more), paired by name as KiCad's Sync Sheet Pins window pairs them: a pin and a label of the same name and shape first, then a pin and a label of the same name whose shapes differ (one row, the two shapes apart), then each pin or label alone, its other side `null`. `pin` and `label` are uuids (lowercase); `PortShape` is one of `input`, `output`, `bidirectional`, `tri_state`, `passive`, present exactly where its side is. Two names that differ are two rows: a host that pairs them sends the rename. An engine answer of another shape is `island_error`.

### sheet.pins.apply
The host's changes to sheet pins and hierarchical labels as ONE commit "Synchronize sheet pins" (one undo step, where KiCad's own window commits each change apart), in a `sch` frame with a schematic open (island `v0.2.3-cc25`, on an engine with `kicadSheetPinsApply` and `kicadCollabBusy`, engine run 15; cap `syncPins`). The args are exactly `{ actions }`, 1 to 4,096 of:

```ts
type SyncAction =
  | { op: 'renamePin'; pin: string; to: string } | { op: 'renameLabel'; label: string; sheetPath: string; to: string }
  | { op: 'shapePin'; pin: string; to: PortShape } | { op: 'shapeLabel'; label: string; sheetPath: string; to: PortShape }
  | { op: 'removePin'; pin: string } | { op: 'removeLabel'; label: string; sheetPath: string };
```

each with exactly its keys: uuids lowercase as `sheet.pins.get` answered them, a label's `sheetPath` the path of the sheet that HOLDS it (the row's `sheetPath` followed by the row's `sheet` and a slash), a name 1 to 1,024 characters with no control character. The checks, in order: the args (`bad_args`); the frame; the engine (`not_ready`, `unsupported` naming the first missing export); a schematic open (`not_ready`); `busy` as for `sheet.pins.get`, and during a check or an import.

The frame queues the run with a fresh `/tmp/cc-sheet-pins/<n>/` for its record, waits for the apply queue to empty and the record, and removes the folder whatever the answer. Every pin and label named must be in the design (a label on its sheet path): else the engine refuses the whole run and the answer is `not_applied` with nothing changed. A pin is removed as KiCad's own delete removes one. The answer counts the actions applied (an action on an item an earlier action of the same request removed is skipped); an `ev.edited` follows once the depth moved. `not_applied` also when no record came within about 30 s; `island_error` for a record of another shape.

### sheet.pins.place
The missing side placed by KiCad's own placement tools, in a `sch` frame with a schematic open (island `v0.2.3-cc25`, on an engine with `kicadSheetPinsPlace`, `kicadCollabBusy` and `kicadCollabGetSelection`, engine run 15; cap `syncPins`). The args are exactly `{ sheet, sheetPath, place, names }`: the sheet's uuid and the path it sits on as a read answered them, `place` `pin` (a sheet pin on the sheet for each name, named and shaped after the sub sheet's hierarchical label of that name) or `label` (a hierarchical label inside the sheet for each name, after the sheet's pin of that name), and 1 to 64 names. The checks, in order: the args (`bad_args`); the frame; an item of ours still hanging off the pointer is dropped first (`busy` when it would not go); the engine (`not_ready`, `unsupported` naming the first missing export); a schematic open (`not_ready`); `busy` as for `sheet.pins.get`. `not_applied` when the sheet is not there or a name has no counterpart (nothing armed), or when the editor did not run the tool.

KiCad's window never opens. For `label` the editor enters the sheet and the first label hangs off the pointer: the answer `{ hanging: true }` comes once it does, the placement joined to the place watch (`busy` everywhere while it hangs, Esc drops it and leaves the tool, a press commits one undo step and the next name hangs, the tool ending after the last); `busy` when it never hangs within about 10 s. For `pin` the editor shows the sheet's own level with the sheet chosen, and KiCad's sheet pin tool makes its item at the reader's first press on the drawing, at the sheet's edge: the answer `{ hanging: false }` comes once the tool runs with nothing hanging yet, `{ hanging: true }` (joined to the watch) when an item already hangs. Each press after that places the next name; Esc ends the placement and nothing stays armed.

- **Held**: while `ev.ready` lists `syncPins` in a `sch` frame, KiCad's Sync Sheet Pins (`eeschema.InteractiveDrawing.syncSheetPins`) and Sync All Sheet Pins (`eeschema.InteractiveDrawing.syncAllSheetsPins`, seeded Ctrl+Alt+Y) are held: KiCad's window never opens, the frame sends `ev.held { action }` and the host opens its Sheet pins page over the three ops. On an older engine (run 14 and before) the cap is absent, the two actions are KiCad's own, and the three ops answer `unsupported` naming the first missing export.

### sim.command

The lines a KiCad simulator workbook (`.wbk`) carries for one setup, so the host can write a workbook KiCad reads (island `v0.2.3-cc25`, in a `sch` frame whose engine has the simulator; a host gates it on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `simCommand`). `analysis` and `options` are read closed exactly as `sim.run` reads them; a number outside the SPICE grammar, a key not listed or a value of another type answers `bad_args`.

- `commands[0]` is the analysis line the island writes with its own writer, the line `sim.run` sends for the same analysis (`.tran 1u 5m`, `.ac dec 10 1 1meg`, `.noise v(/out) V1 dec 5 10 100k`). An FFT gives its own lines (`set specwindow=...` when a window is asked, `linearize ...`, `fft ...`), its signals written as given: pass KiCad's signal names (`V(/out)`). `{ kind: "sheet" }` writes no analysis line (the schematic's own directive stands).
- Then the option lines KiCad's workbook writes, in its order: `.save all` (voltages), `.probe alli` (currents), `.probe allp` (dissipations), `.kicad esavenone` (events not kept). Never `.kicad adjustpaths`: the model paths are the design's own. KiCad's defaults for a new tab (voltages, currents and events kept) give `.save all` and `.probe alli`.
- A read: no engine call, no document needed, answered during a check. The names of a DC sweep or a noise analysis are not checked against the netlist (`sim.run` checks them when the workbook runs); a DC sweep source that is not a V, I or R element, or a noise source that is not a V or I element, answers `bad_args` (`sim.command: C1 is not a source`), as `sim.run` refuses it.
- Outside a `sch` frame: `unsupported` (`the simulator runs in a sch frame`); an engine without the simulator's run exports: `unsupported` naming the first missing one.

### Show Bus Syntax Help, held

Island `v0.2.3-cc25`. In a `sch` frame KiCad's `eeschema.InspectionTool.showBusSyntaxHelp` (Inspect > Show Bus Syntax Help) is held under `heldKeys`: with a document open the press is dropped, no window of KiCad's opens, and the frame sends one `ev.held { action: "eeschema.InspectionTool.showBusSyntaxHelp" }`. The host answers it with its own card on how bus names are written. A key bound to the action reaches the row on every engine that asks the page (run 12 on); the menu item reaches it where `ev.ready` also lists `heldMenus` (engine run 15). In every other frame the action is KiCad's.

### Calculator Tools (`openCalculator`)

Island `v0.2.3-cc25`. KiCad's PCB Calculator is a program of its own that the browser build cannot start: KiCad's Tools > Calculator Tools hands its name, `pcb_calculator`, to the frame, which sends `ev.openTool { frame: "calculator" }` and opens nothing (no window, no error). A key bound to the action `common.Control.showCalculatorTools` (it has no default key) is held as every held row is and sends `ev.held { action: "common.Control.showCalculatorTools" }`; on an engine whose menus ask the frame (`heldMenus`) the menu item is held the same way and sends `ev.held` in place of `ev.openTool`. A host answers both by opening its calculator. `ev.ready` lists `openCalculator` in a `sch` or `pcb` frame exactly when the engine has `kicadHostActions` (engine run 12 and later).

### item.compare
A placed symbol or a footprint compared with its source, the item of the same name in the library its nickname names, loaded through the frame's own libraries (the hosted mirror, the design's tables): KiCad's own Compare Symbol with Library in a `sch` frame and Compare Footprint with Library in a `pcb` frame, with no KiCad window (island `v0.2.3-cc25`, on an engine with `kicadCompareWithSource`, engine run 15; a host gates it on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `compare`, listed by `sch` and `pcb` frames). The args are exactly `{ uuid }` or, in a `sch` frame, `{ uuid, sheetPath }` in `symbol.get`'s grammar (a lowercase uuid; a sheet path in `project.open`'s form, absent for the engine's own choice of path); in a `pcb` frame the uuid is a footprint's and no `sheetPath` is taken. The checks, in order, before any effect: the args (`bad_args`); the frame (`unsupported` "item.compare runs in a sch or pcb frame"); the engine (`not_ready`, `unsupported` naming `kicadCompareWithSource`); a document open (`not_ready`); a load parked, a popup or a dialog up, or an item hanging off the pointer (`busy`). A read: answered during a `sheet.check` or a `board.check`. The source may not be in the frame yet (a set of the hosted mirror is fetched on its first use): the engine loads it inside the compare, and the op waits for the engine's answer, at most 25 s (`island_error` "the engine did not answer the compare within the time limit" past that). An engine whose `kicadCompareWithSource` cannot wait inside the call (engine run 15 build T16b, where the export is not registered to park) answers `island_error` with the browser's own message for such a source; a source already in the frame (the design's own set) is compared at once on every engine.

`status` is KiCad's own verdict, `same` or `differs`, or `noSource` when the library is absent from the tables or disabled, or holds no item of that name: a success answer, never an error (the host offers Keep a copy). `source` is the source's library id text (`Device:R`, `Resistor_SMD:R_0603_1608Metric`). `findings` lists at most 512 differences, one per line KiCad's comparison reports, each with its `code`, the item it concerns (`uuid`, lowercase, or null), a pin number (`pin`, else `""`) and a field or setting name (`field`, else `""`); they are kept whatever the status (a `same` footprint can carry `boardStackup` lines, which KiCad calls informational). `DiffCode` is one of `power`, `unitCount`, `graphicCount`, `graphic`, `pinExtra`, `pin`, `pinMissing`, `fieldExtra`, `field`, `fieldMissing`, `fpFilters`, `keywords`, `pinNameOffset`, `showPinNames`, `showPinNumbers`, `excludeSim`, `excludeBom`, `excludeBoard`, `excludePos` (a symbol's), and `pad` (the uuid the board's pad, `pin` its number), `padCount`, `shape` (the uuid the board copy's item), `attributes` (`field` `type`, `allowSolderMaskBridges` or `boardOnly`), `boardStackup`, `dnp`, `stackup`, `override` (`field` `clearance`, `solderMask`, `solderPaste`, `solderPasteRatio` or `zoneConnection`), `netTie`, `barcodeCount`, `barcode` (the uuid the board copy's), `ruleAreaCount`, `ruleArea` (a footprint's), with `text`, `model` and `other` reserved (KiCad compares no footprint text and no model). `not_found` (naming the uuid, and the sheet path when one was asked) when the document holds no such item or the placed symbol carries no copy of its own to compare; `island_error` for an engine answer of another shape.

KiCad's own Compare Footprint with Library (`pcbnew.InspectionTool.DiffFootprint`) is held in a `pcb` frame and Compare Symbol with Library (`eeschema.InspectionTool.diffSymbol`) in a `sch` frame, both under the cap `compare`: each sends `ev.held { action }` and opens no window (`tests/e2e/st/st-compare-i.spec.ts`).

### Copy the drawing: KiCad's Export Drawing to Clipboard held

Island `v0.2.3-cc25`. In a `sch` frame, while `ev.ready` lists `heldKeys` and a document is open, `eeschema.EditorControl.drawSheetOnClipboard` (KiCad's File > Export > Drawing to Clipboard) is held: the press is dropped, nothing of KiCad's opens and the frame sends one `ev.held { action }`. KiCad would copy through its own clipboard, which never reaches the browser's; the host answers with its own Copy the drawing (`project.export` `sch_export_plot_svg` for the sheet on screen). A key reaches the row on every engine with `kicadHostActions`; the menu item reaches it where `ev.ready` also lists `heldMenus` (engine run 15). KiCad's table gives the action no default chord. In a `pcb` or `symedit` frame the action is not held.

### Editing preferences: the sections `schEditing`, `pcbEditing` and `mouse`

Island `v0.2.3-cc25`, on an engine of run 15 or later. `settings.get` and `settings.set` take three more sections, KiCad's own Preferences pages with no KiCad window: `schEditing` in a `sch` frame (the schematic editor's editing options), `pcbEditing` in a `pcb` frame (the board editor's editing options) and `mouse` in both (the mouse and touchpad page; one object in the engine, so a set in either frame changes both). The keys are KiCad's own dotted JSON paths, each value KiCad's kind: `schEditing` the switches `appearance.footprint_preview`, `system.never_show_rescue_dialog`, `autoplace_fields.enable`, `autoplace_fields.allow_rejustify`, `autoplace_fields.align_to_grid`, `input.drag_is_move`, `drawing.auto_start_wires`, `input.esc_clears_net_highlight`, `annotation.automatic`, `input.allow_unconstrained_pin_swaps` and the numbers `drawing.new_power_symbols`, `drawing.line_mode`, `editing.arc_edit_mode`, `drawing.default_repeat_offset_x`, `drawing.default_repeat_offset_y` (mils), `drawing.repeat_label_increment`; `pcbEditing` the switches `editing.flip_left_right`, `editing.esc_clears_net_highlight`, `editing.show_courtyard_collisions`, `editing.magnetic_graphics`, `editing.ctrl_click_highlight`, `editing.auto_fill_zones`, `editing.allow_free_pads`, `pcb_display.ratsnest_footprint`, `pcb_display.ratsnest_curved`, `pcb_display.show_page_borders` and the numbers `editing.magnetic_pads`, `editing.magnetic_tracks`, `editing.track_drag_action`, `editing.arc_edit_mode`, `editing.pcb_angle_snap_mode`, `editing.rotation_angle` (tenths of a degree), `pcb_display.ratsnest_thickness`; `mouse` the switches `input.center_on_zoom`, `input.auto_pan`, `input.zoom_acceleration`, `input.zoom_speed_auto`, `input.horizontal_pan`, `input.reverse_scroll_zoom`, `input.reverse_scroll_pan_h` and the numbers `input.mouse_left`, `input.mouse_middle`, `input.mouse_right`, `input.motion_pan_modifier`, `input.scroll_modifier_zoom`, `input.scroll_modifier_pan_h`, `input.scroll_modifier_pan_v`, `input.auto_pan_acceleration`, `input.zoom_speed`. A set's `value` names any of its section's keys; another key, or a value of the other kind, is `bad_args` before the engine is asked, and the engine answers `bad_args` for a number outside what KiCad's page offers. The sheet colors of KiCad's schematic page are left out (colors stay the host's).

These are app settings, not the design's: a set makes no undo step, marks nothing modified and writes no file, and runs the rest of KiCad's Preferences OK (the tools reset and the canvas redrawn). An engine boots with KiCad's defaults, so a host keeps its reader's choices itself and sends them again after each `ev.ready`. A host gates each section on `ev.ready` `caps`: `settings:schEditing` in a `sch` frame, `settings:pcbEditing` in a `pcb` frame and `settings:mouse` in both, each listed exactly when the engine's `kicadSettingsSections()` names the section in that frame's half (never on engine run 14).

KiCad's Preferences window (`common.SuiteControl.openPreferences`: its key `Ctrl+,`, and its menu item where `heldMenus` is listed) is held in `sch` and `pcb` frames under `heldKeys` while a document is open: the frame sends `ev.held { action }` and opens nothing, and the host opens its own editing preferences instead.

### find
KiCad's own Find over the LIVE document (unsaved edits included), in a `sch` or a `pcb` frame with a document open (island `v0.2.3-cc25`, on an engine with `kicadFind`, engine run 15; a host gates it on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `find`, listed by `sch` and `pcb` frames). The args are exactly `{ text, matchCase, wholeWord, wildcards, regex }` and ONE face object, the frame's: `sch` with its nine booleans (`refs` designators, `values`, `fields` other fields, `hidden` hidden fields too, `pins` pin names and numbers, `labels` labels and text, `nets` net names, `thisSheet` this sheet only, `selected` the selection only) or `pcb` with its five (`refs`, `values`, `texts` other text and zone names, `nets`, `markers`). `text` is 1 to 256 code points with no NUL. The options follow KiCad's find dialogs in order: a regular expression, then whole words, then wildcards, else plain text; `matchCase` applies to each. The checks, in order, before any effect: the args (`bad_args`, a face of the other frame included); the frame (`unsupported` "find runs in a sch or pcb frame"); the engine (`not_ready`, `unsupported` naming `kicadFind`); a document open (`not_ready`); a load parked, a popup or a dialog up, or an item hanging off the pointer (`busy`). A read: answered during a `sheet.check` or a `board.check`.

The answer lists at most 4,096 hits, `truncated` true past that. Each hit names its item (`uuid`, lowercase), the sheet path it was matched on (`""` on a board), its `kind`, the designator `ref` of its part when it has one, the `field` name (a pin's number for kind `pin`), the item's `text` (cut at 1,024 code points) and where the match lies in it (`start`, `length` in code points; a wildcard match or a net name lights the whole text). In a schematic a part itself is never a hit: its designator or value is kind `symbol` (`field` "Reference" or "Value"), other fields `field`, pins `pin` (or `net` when only the net name matched), labels and sheet pins `label` (or `net`), text and text boxes `text`; a designator is listed on every sheet path that holds it, any other item on its first path only. On a board: one hit per footprint (`footprint` for its designator or value, else `text` or `field`), the board's own text `text`, zone names `other`, markers `marker`, nets `net`. A regular expression KiCad cannot compile answers `bad_args` "find: the regular expression does not compile"; an engine answer of another shape is `island_error`.

KiCad's own Find (`common.Interactive.find`, Ctrl+F), Search panel (`common.Interactive.search`, Ctrl+G), Find Next (`common.Interactive.findNext`, F3) and Find Previous (`common.Interactive.findPrevious`, Shift+F3) are held in a `sch` and a `pcb` frame under the cap `find`; Find and Replace (`common.Interactive.findAndReplace`, Ctrl+Alt+F) and Replace and Find Next (`common.Interactive.replaceAndFindNext`) in a `sch` frame under the cap `replace`. Each sends `ev.held { action }` and opens no window (`tests/e2e/st/st-find-e-i.spec.ts`).

### find.replace
KiCad's own Find and Replace in a `sch` frame with a schematic open (island `v0.2.3-cc25`, on an engine with `kicadSchReplace` and `kicadCollabBusy`, engine run 15; a host gates it on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `replace`, listed by `sch` frames only). The args are exactly `{ find, with, targets }`: `find`'s own args with the `sch` face, the replacement (0 to 256 code points, no NUL), and `"all"` or 1 to 4,096 hits sent back as `find` answered them. The checks, in order: the args (`bad_args`); the frame (`unsupported` "a replace runs in a sch frame"); the engine (`not_ready`, `unsupported` naming the first missing export); a schematic open (`not_ready`); `busy` as for `find`, and during a check or an import.

The frame queues the run with a fresh `/tmp/cc-replace/<n>/` for its records, waits for the apply queue to empty and the run's record, and removes the folder whatever the answer. Every target is found again first with KiCad's own matching (a target matched by its `uuid`, `sheetPath`, `kind` and `field`; one that no longer matches is counted in `skipped`), each replaced through its item's own Replace (designators only when `find.sch.refs` is true), as ONE commit "Find and Replace": one undo step, and an `ev.edited` once the depth moved. `no_match` when nothing was replaced (nothing changed); `not_applied` when the engine refused the run or no record came within about 30 s; `island_error` for a record of another shape.

### KiCad never shows: held menus and toolbars (`heldMenus`)

Island `v0.2.3-cc25`. Our engine from run 15 (kicad `cc/main` K-HOLD, in `ACTION_MENU::OnMenuEvent` and `ACTION_TOOLBAR`'s press handling) asks the frame for every menu item and toolbar button a press resolves to, through the same slot keys use since run 12 (`ev.held` above): a held action is taken from a key, a menu bar item, a toolbar button and an item of KiCad's own context menus alike, the press is dropped, and the frame sends one `ev.held { action }`. KiCad's context menus still open (they are reported as `ev.menu` as before); only a held item inside one is taken. The 45 items of KiCad's Preferences > Set Language are a wx menu range, not tool actions: the engine asks for any of them as one name of ours, `cc.Control.setLanguage`. The engine says it asks with the marker export `kicadHeldMenus`; `ev.ready` lists the pseudo-cap `heldMenus` in a `sch`, `pcb` or `symedit` frame exactly when the engine has `kicadHeldMenus` and `kicadHostActions`. A host shows KiCad's menus (`chrome.show`) with a held action's door gated on this word: where it is absent (engine run 14 and older) a menu or toolbar press still reaches KiCad's window, and only keys are held.

Held with nothing of the host's to open (the host answers each `ev.held` with nothing, or a short line of its own): `cc.Control.setLanguage` (every editor frame, under `heldMenus`: the menus stay in English), `eeschema.RemoteSymbols.showPanel` (`sch`, the Remote Symbols panel), `common.API.pluginsReload` (`sch` and `pcb`, Refresh Plugins), `pcbnew.ScriptingTool.pluginsShowFolder` (`pcb`, Open Plugin Directory) and `pcbnew.Control.showPythonConsole` (`pcb`, the scripting console), the last four under `heldKeys`. The island runs no Python and no plugin API, so these windows would show nothing that works. Every other action a wave 2 door replaces is held by that door's own rows; the island reads every row as one table, so a host that lists its doors reads the same union.

### sheet.increment

KiCad's Increment Annotations From with no window (island `v0.2.3-cc25`, on an engine with `kicadSchIncrementAnnotations`, engine run 15; a host gates it on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `incrementAnnotations`, which a `sch` frame lists on such an engine). It is the body of KiCad's own `SCH_EDITOR_CONTROL::IncrementAnnotations` after its dialog: every part whose designator has the prefix of `first` and a number at or above the number of `first` moves up by `by`, on the shown sheet or, with `allSheets`, on every sheet; the other designators are untouched. It is ONE commit, "Increment Annotations": one undo step for the whole shift, and an `ev.edited` follows when the depth moved. A `first` that matches no part changes nothing and still answers, `shifted` false.

- The args are exactly `{ first, by, allSheets }`: `first` a designator of 2 to 64 characters that splits into a prefix ending in a character other than a digit and a number (`R10`, `U3`; `R` or `R?` refuse), no control character and no space at either end; `by` a whole number from 1 to 100,000 (KiCad's dialog spins 1 to 64); `allSheets` a boolean.
- The answer is `{ shifted, depth }`: `depth` the undo depth after the shift, `shifted` true when it moved. The host reads the new designators back (`symbols.get`).
- The checks, in order, every one before any effect: the args (`bad_args`); in any frame but `sch` it answers `unsupported`; then as a schematic op: `not_ready` before `ev.ready`, `unsupported` naming `kicadSchIncrementAnnotations`, `kicadCollabBusy` or `kicadCollabTestUndoDepth` on an engine without it, `not_ready` with no schematic open, `busy` while a load is parked, a popup menu or a dialog is up, or a placed item hangs off the pointer, or during a `sheet.check`. Then the engine's shift: `not_applied` when it refuses (an open in flight), or when its apply queue does not empty within 30 s.
- Held under the cap: `eeschema.EditorControl.incrementAnnotations` (KiCad's Increment Annotations From) in a `sch` frame sends `ev.held { action }` and opens no window; the host's page asks for the three values and sends `sheet.increment`. On an engine without the export the action is KiCad's.

### keys.editor.get and keys.editor.set
The editor's own hotkeys, read and set in a `sch` or `pcb` frame (island `v0.2.3-cc25`, on an engine with `kicadHotkeysGet` and `kicadHotkeysSet`, engine run 15; a host gates both on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `hotkeys`, listed by `sch` and `pcb` frames only). No KiCad window opens and no document is needed: the keys are the editor's, not the design's, and they last for the engine's life only, so a host sends its keys again after each `ev.ready`.

`keys.editor.get` takes no args and answers the frame's `editor` and every action of that editor's action manager with a friendly name (KiCad's own hotkey list rule): `eeschema.` actions in a `sch` frame, `pcbnew.` actions in a `pcb` frame, and `common.` actions in both, sorted by name. `key` is the key it holds now, `default` KiCad's default, `alt` its second key, each in KiCad's key grammar (`Ctrl+Shift+M`, `Num Pad 1`), `""` for none; `label` is KiCad's friendly name, left out when KiCad gives none. A read: answered during a `sheet.check` or `board.check`.

`keys.editor.set` takes exactly `{ keys }`: 1 to 2,048 action names, each with a key in KiCad's grammar of at most 32 characters, or `""` for no key. It writes each action's PRIMARY key and keeps its second key, all or nothing, then KiCad rebuilds its key map. It is no edit: no undo step, no modified flag, no `ev.edited`. A key that is a chord the island seeds for another tool answers `taken` and nothing is sent; an action's own seeded chord given back to it is taken.

The checks, in order: the args (`bad_args`); the frame (`unsupported`); the seeded chords (`taken`, a set); the engine (`not_ready` before `ev.ready`, `unsupported` naming `kicadHotkeysGet` or `kicadHotkeysSet`); a load parked, a popup menu or a dialog up (`busy`). The get answers `busy` when the engine answered nothing and `island_error` for an answer of another shape; the set answers `bad_args` "the engine refused the keys" when the engine refused them (an action this editor lacks, a key KiCad cannot read), nothing changed. A set answers `busy` during a check.

KiCad's hotkey list (`common.SuiteControl.listHotKeys`, Ctrl+F1 and its Help menu item) is held in `sch` and `pcb` frames under `heldKeys`: `ev.held { action: "common.SuiteControl.listHotKeys" }` goes to the host, which opens its own keys window, and no KiCad window opens.

### board.nets
The board's nets with their lengths, read in a `pcb` frame with a board open (island `v0.2.3-cc25`, on an engine with `kicadNetReport`, engine run 15; a host gates it on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `netReport`, listed by `pcb` frames only). No KiCad window opens. The args are none or `{}` (every net but the unconnected one, in KiCad's net code order), or exactly `{ nets }`: 1 to 8,192 names of 1 to 1,024 UTF-8 bytes, each matching a net's stored name or its unescaped form; a name the board lacks is left out of the answer. The checks, in order: the args (`bad_args`); the frame (`unsupported` "a board op runs in a pcb frame"); the engine (`not_ready`, `unsupported` naming `kicadNetReport`); a board open (`not_ready`); a load parked, a popup or a dialog up, or an item hanging off the pointer (`busy`). A read: answered during a `board.check`.

Each row holds the figures of KiCad's own Net Inspector: `name` as the board stores it, `netClass` its human readable name, `length` the routed track length in mm with the inspector's optimizations (vias, merged tracks, the track inside a pad trimmed), so it can be shorter than the plain sum of the segments (on Glasgow GND reads 254.4734 mm where the segments sum to 255.8152 mm), `byLayer` the copper layers that carry length, `vias` and `viaLength`, `pads`, `dieLength` (mm), and `delay` the total in picoseconds or null when the board gives no delay. `busy` when the engine answered nothing (an open began); `island_error` for an answer of another shape.

### Show Footprint Associations held

Island `v0.2.3-cc25`. KiCad's Inspect > Show Footprint Associations (`pcbnew.InspectionTool.ShowFootprintAssociations`) opens a window of KiCad's own that the host's Linked to block of the board form replaces (the footprint's source and the schematic part it belongs to, read through `board.footprints`), so the island holds it in a `pcb` frame and the frame sends `ev.held { action }` in its place (from a key, and from KiCad's menus under `heldMenus`); `action.run` of it answers `held`. It is held only while `ev.ready` listed `boardFootprints`, so a host that cannot read the links never has the window taken from it. A `sch` or `symedit` frame never holds it.

### board.multichannel
KiCad's multichannel tool (Generate Placement Rule Areas and Repeat Layout) with no dialog, progress window or info bar, in a `pcb` frame with a board open (island `v0.2.3-cc25`, on an engine with `kicadMultichannel`, `kicadMultichannelApply` and `kicadCollabBusy`, engine run 15; a host gates it on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `multichannel`, listed by `pcb` frames only). The checks, in order, before any effect: the args, read closed (`bad_args`); the frame (`unsupported` "a multichannel op runs in a pcb frame"); the engine (`not_ready`, `unsupported` naming the first missing export); a board open (`not_ready`); a load parked, a popup or a dialog up, or an item hanging off the pointer (`busy`). The two reads are answered during a `board.check` or a `sheet.check`; `generate` and `repeat` then answer `busy`. During an import every op answers `busy`.

- `{ op: "areas" }` reads, with nothing changed: `areas`, every candidate KiCad finds for a rule area (`key` `sheet:<path>`, `class:<name>` or `group:<name>`, what `generate` takes; `name` the area's name KiCad gives it, `auto-placement-area-<source>`; `source` `sheet`, `componentClass` or `group`; `sheetPath` for a sheet, else ""; `parts` its part count; `exists` and `zone`, the uuid of a placement rule area holding exactly the same parts, else `false` and ""); `ruleAreas`, the board's placement rule areas (`zone` uuid, `name`, `source` as above or `designBlock`, `sourceName` KiCad's source text, `parts`); `canGenerate`, false where KiCad says it cannot make any (one or no candidate).
- `{ op: "check", ref }` (a rule area's zone uuid) answers KiCad's topology verdict for every OTHER placement rule area against it, with nothing changed: `zone`, `name`, `parts`, `matched` (the parts KiCad matched), `ok`, `message` ("OK" or KiCad's first reason) and `reasons` (KiCad's own mismatch lines). `not_found` (`message` the ref) when the ref is no rule area. The match runs to its end before the answer.
- `{ op: "generate", areas, replaceExisting, groupItems? }` (1 to 4,096 distinct keys from `areas`) makes a placement rule area for each, as KiCad's Generate Placement Rule Areas does past its dialog, the dialog's choices from the args: an area that exists is skipped unless `replaceExisting` (then removed first), `groupItems` groups each new area with its parts. ONE commit "Auto-generate placement rule areas" (one undo step), none when nothing was made. The answer `{ preview: false, areas, applied, depth }`: a row per key `{ key, name, parts, ok, reasons, skipped, zone? }` (`skipped` "", "exists" or "empty", a group with no board items; `zone` the new area's uuid when `ok`), `applied` the areas made, `depth` the undo depth after. `too_few` when KiCad cannot make any, `not_found` (`message` the key) for a key it does not find; nothing changed.
- `{ op: "repeat", ref, targets, options, preview }` (1 to 4,096 distinct zone uuids, not the ref) repeats the reference area's layout onto each target through KiCad's Repeat Layout, with KiCad's options (each absent one at KiCad's default; `anchor` a footprint uuid of the reference area, or ""). Group the items is not an option of a repeat (`bad_args`). A target that does not match is reported (`ok: false`, KiCad's reasons) and skipped, as KiCad skips it; the matching ones go in ONE commit "Repeat layout" (one undo step, and an `ev.edited` once the depth moved). `preview: true`, or a run where nothing matched, is reverted. The answer `{ preview, areas, applied, depth }`: a row per target `{ zone, name, parts, ok, reasons }`, `applied` the targets copied. `not_found` (`message` the uuid) for a ref, target or anchor the board does not hold, `copy_failed` when a copy failed (the whole commit reverted); nothing changed.

The apply's records are written under a fresh `/tmp/cc-multichannel/<n>/`, removed whatever the answer; `not_applied` when the engine refused the run or no record came within about 60 s; `island_error` for a record of another shape. KiCad's own Generate Placement Rule Areas (`pcbnew.Multichannel.generatePlacementRuleAreas`) and Repeat Layout (`pcbnew.Multichannel.repeatLayout`) are held in a `pcb` frame under the cap `multichannel`: each sends `ev.held { action }` and opens no window. The fixture is KiCad's own qa board `qa/data/pcbnew/issue21739/topology_mismatch` (read from the KiCad tree by path; `tests/e2e/st/st-mc-i.spec.ts`, engine run 15).

### microwave.arm
The values of KiCad's microwave tools given before the press, in a `pcb` frame with a board open (island `v0.2.3-cc25`, on an engine with `kicadMicrowaveArm`, engine run 15; a host gates it on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `microwave`, listed by `pcb` frames only). The host arms, then presses the row's chord with `key.press` (`pcbnew.MicrowaveTool.createGap`, `createStub`, `createStubArc`, `createLine`, `createFunctionShape`): the shape hangs on the pointer with no KiCad window, one click places it as one undo step, and the tool ends after that one placement. The engine holds ONE pending arm: the start of any microwave action consumes it and only a matching shape uses it (arming `stub` then pressing Gap loses the arm and Gap asks KiCad's value). Unarmed, Gap, Stub and Arc stub ask their value through the generic `entry` ask, but Shape opens KiCad's own dialog (it has no ask), so a host always arms before pressing Shape.

The args, read closed with the engine's own bounds: `value` in mm, over 0 and at most 500 (the track length for `line`, the gap or stub size, the arc stub's radius); `angle` 0 to 180 degrees, absent 0; `polygon.points` 2 to 2,048 pairs as a shape file's `$COORD` lines write them (fractions, not mm), `polygon.size` the scale in mm (the file's `XScale` and `YScale`, or what the reader typed), each component nonzero and at most 500, every point times the size within 1,000 mm; `kind` `plain` (KiCad's Normal), `symmetrical` or `mirrored`. `line` places an `mw_inductor` valued `L`. The checks, in order: the args (`bad_args`); the frame (`unsupported` "a board op runs in a pcb frame"); the engine (`not_ready`, `unsupported` naming `kicadMicrowaveArm`); a board open (`not_ready`); a load parked, a popup or a dialog up, or an item hanging off the pointer (`busy`). `busy` too when the engine cannot arm now (an open in flight, a dialog showing); the earlier arm then stands. Errors KiCad raises after the values (a line shorter than its minimum, a shape of null size) stay KiCad's info bar messages and the armed run still ends.

### Origins: the section `origins`

Island `v0.2.3-cc25`, on an engine of run 15 or later. `settings.get` and `settings.set` take one more board section, `origins`, in a `pcb` frame: the board's grid origin and its drill and place origin, with no KiCad window. A get answers `{ grid_origin: { x, y }, aux_origin: { x, y } }` in mm (KiCad's own names; `aux_origin` is the drill and place origin). A set's `value` names `grid_origin`, `aux_origin` or both, each exactly `{ x, y }`, finite numbers within 1000 mm of the page origin either way; `{ x: 0, y: 0 }` is KiCad's Reset. Another key, a part of an origin or a number past the bound is `bad_args` before the engine is asked; in a `sch` frame the section answers `unsupported` (`origins is a board setting`). Like every setting, a set makes no undo step; the two origin markers on the canvas move with it.

A host gates the section on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `settings:origins`, listed in a `pcb` frame exactly when the engine's `kicadSettingsSections()` names `origins` in its board half (never on engine run 14). Under that cap, while a document is open, KiCad's three origin actions are held in the `pcb` frame: `common.Control.editGridOrigin` (the grid origin window), `common.Control.gridResetOrigin` and `pcbnew.EditorControl.drillResetOrigin` send `ev.held { action }` and open nothing, and the host opens its own Origins fold instead. The schematic's grid origin reset is not held.

### paste.special
KiCad's own Paste Special with the host's choice and no window of KiCad's (island `v0.2.3-cc25`, on an engine with `kicadPasteSpecial` and `kicadCollabGetSelection`, engine run 15; a host gates it on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `pasteSpecial`, listed by a `sch` and a `pcb` frame on such an engine). What KiCad's clipboard holds (a Ctrl+C or Ctrl+X pressed through `key.press`, or text the frame received) is pasted as KiCad's Paste Special pastes it.

- `mode`: the designators of the pasted parts. `"unique"` gives each a new designator (KiCad's next free number), `"keep"` keeps the copied ones (the checker then flags the duplicates), `"remove"` clears them (`R?`).
- `clearNets` (optional, false when absent): a board only. True pastes a board clip with no net names; on copper KiCad still gives a track the net it lands on. True in a `sch` frame answers `bad_args` "paste.special: clearNets is a board choice".

Any other key or type answers `bad_args` "paste.special". The checks, in order, before any effect: the args (`bad_args`); the frame (any frame but `sch` and `pcb` answers `unsupported` "a paste runs in a sch or pcb frame"); the engine (`not_ready`, `unsupported` naming the first missing export, so on engine run 14 `unsupported` "kicadPasteSpecial"); a document open (`not_ready`); a load parked, a popup or a dialog up, or an item hanging off the pointer (`busy`).

The answer `{}` comes once the items hang off the pointer, read as `place` reads its item (the selection holds a uuid it did not hold before) within the same bound. A click lands them as ONE commit, one undo step and an `ev.edited`; Esc leaves nothing and no undo step. The hanging paste is joined to `place`'s watch: every gate answers `busy` until it ends, and a later `place` cancels it first. `busy` "paste.special: the editor cannot paste now" when the engine refuses the call; `not_applied` when KiCad put nothing on the pointer within about 10 s (the clipboard holds nothing it pastes).

KiCad's own Paste Special (Ctrl+Shift+V and its menu item) opens nothing: under `pasteSpecial` a `sch` and a `pcb` frame hold `common.Interactive.pasteSpecial`, which sends `ev.held { action }`; the host opens its own choices.

### place.file
Puts a file the design holds on the pointer by KiCad's own Import Sheet (a `sch` frame, `kind: "sheet"`) or Append Board (a `pcb` frame, `kind: "board"`), with no file chooser and no window of KiCad's (island `v0.2.3-cc25`, on an engine with `kicadPlaceFromFile` and `kicadCollabGetSelection`, engine run 15; a host gates it on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `placeFile`, listed by a `sch` and a `pcb` frame on such an engine).

The host first writes the file into the project folder, under `imports/`, through `project.write`; `name` is that project path (relative, in its normal form: no empty, `.` or `..` segment, no backslash or control character, at most 1,024 bytes of UTF-8), ending `.kicad_sch` for a sheet and `.kicad_pcb` for a board. No bytes travel with the request. Any other key or shape answers `bad_args` "place.file", a kind of the other frame `bad_args` "place.file: a sheet places in a sch frame, a board in a pcb frame".

The checks, in order, before any effect: the args (`bad_args`); the frame (any frame but `sch` and `pcb` answers `unsupported` "a file places in a sch or pcb frame"); the engine (`not_ready`, `unsupported` naming the first missing export); a document open (`not_ready`); a load parked, a popup or a dialog up, or an item hanging off the pointer (`busy`); then the file: `not_found` (`message` the name) when the project folder holds none, `too_large` "place.file: <name> is over 25 MB". The frame copies the file to a fresh `/tmp/cc-stage/<n>/` (the engine reads only there, never the project folder), calls the engine with that path, and removes the folder after the answer, whatever it is.

The answer `{}` comes once the items hang off the pointer: a sheet's contents (always the contents: the island never turns KiCad's place as sheet option on, and a page wanting the file placed as a sheet uses `block.place` with `asSheet`), or the other board's items on KiCad's move tool (the destination keeps its stackup, page and title block). A click lands them as ONE commit, one undo step and an `ev.edited`; Esc leaves nothing and no undo step. The hanging placement is joined to `place`'s watch: every gate answers `busy` until it ends, and a later `place` cancels it first. `busy` "place.file: the editor cannot take a file now" when the engine refuses the call (an open in flight, a dialog up); `not_applied` when KiCad put nothing on the pointer within about 30 s (the file holds no items, or KiCad did not take it); `island_error` when the engine logged a refusal. A KiCad message about the file (a load error) reaches the host as KiCad's message ask, as every KiCad message box does on engine run 15.

KiCad's own Import Sheet and Append Board open nothing: under `placeFile` a `sch` frame holds `eeschema.InteractiveDrawing.importSheet` and a `pcb` frame holds `pcbnew.EditorControl.appendBoard`, each sending `ev.held { action }`; the host opens its own Import card. KiCad's drag and drop append (`pcbnew.Control.DdAppendBoard`) is left alone: no drop reaches KiCad's canvas.

### sch.rescue
Keep a copy of missing symbols: KiCad's Rescue Symbols with no window, in a `sch` frame with a schematic open (island `v0.2.3-cc25`, on an engine with `kicadSchRescue`, `kicadSchRescueApply` and `kicadCollabBusy`, engine run 15; a host gates it on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `rescue`, listed by `sch` frames only). The args are exactly `{ preview: true }` or exactly `{ names }`: 1 to 4,096 names, each 1 to 1,024 bytes of UTF-8 with no control character. The checks, in order, before any effect: the args (`bad_args`); the frame (`unsupported` "a rescue runs in a sch frame"); the engine (`not_ready`, `unsupported` naming the first missing export); a schematic open (`not_ready`); a load parked, a popup or a dialog up, or an item hanging off the pointer (`busy`). During a `sheet.check`, a `board.check` or an import it answers `busy`.

- `preview: true` lists the candidates and changes nothing: `RescueCandidate` is `{ name, libId, uses, rescuedAs }`, at most 4,096: `name` the symbol's name, `libId` the source the design names (`MyParts:R`), `uses` the placed symbols naming it, `rescuedAs` the source KiCad gives it once kept (`<stem>-rescue:R-MyParts`). A part is a candidate when the library its nickname names cannot be read and the design holds its own copy of the symbol (on a KiCad 6 or later design that copy stands in for KiCad 5's cache library), when that library's symbol has pins that disagree with the design's copy, or when its name holds characters KiCad no longer takes.
- `names` keeps a copy of the chosen candidates (a name matches a candidate's `libId`, or its `name` when no other candidate shares it): the design's copies are written into `<stem>-rescue.kicad_sym` in the project folder with its row in the project's `sym-lib-table` (type KiCad, `${KIPRJMOD}/<stem>-rescue.kicad_sym`), and every placed symbol naming them is relinked to the copy as ONE commit "Rescue Symbols": one undo step, and an `ev.edited` once the depth moved. The undo list is kept; an undo relinks the parts to their old source and the written files stay. Both files arrive as `ev.saved` before the answer; the answer is `rescued` (the candidates kept), `symbols` (the placed symbols relinked), `depth` (the undo depth after) and `saved` (the project paths sent as `ev.saved`).

`not_found` "a name matches no rescue candidate" when a name matches none (nothing changed, nothing written); `island_error` with KiCad's first line when the copies could not be written (the commit taken back); `not_applied` when the engine refused the run or no answer came within about 60 s. KiCad's own Rescue Symbols (`eeschema.EditorControl.rescueSymbols`) and Remap Legacy Library Symbols (`eeschema.EditorControl.remapSymbols`) are held in a `sch` frame under the cap `rescue`: each sends `ev.held { action }` and opens no window. The fixture `tests/fixtures/st-rescue-myparts/` (Glasgow with R4 and 11 more resistors drawn from `MyParts:R`, a table row the design does not carry) answers one candidate used 12 times (`tests/e2e/st/st-rescue-i.spec.ts`, engine run 15).

### The File card: KiCad's New, Open, Save As, Save a Copy and Revert held

Island `v0.2.3-cc25`. In a `sch` and a `pcb` frame, while `ev.ready` lists `heldKeys` and a document is open, `common.Control.new` (Ctrl+N), `common.Control.open` (Ctrl+O), `common.Control.saveAs` (Ctrl+Shift+S), `common.Control.saveCopy` and `common.Control.revert` are held: the press is dropped, nothing of KiCad's opens and the frame sends one `ev.held { action }`. The host owns the project's files and answers with its own File card: New and Open open Open another (with Exit's unsaved rule), Save As and Save a Copy open Save a copy, Revert opens Revert to saved (the saved files reopened through `project.open`). A key reaches the rows on every engine with `kicadHostActions`; the menu items reach them where `ev.ready` also lists `heldMenus` (engine run 15). KiCad's table gives Save a Copy and Revert no default chord. Open Recent has no action name in KiCad (a menu range) and is not held here. In a `symedit`, `fpedit` or `ds` frame these rows do not apply (those frames hold their own file actions).

### Routing: the sections `router` and `layerPairs`

Island `v0.2.3-cc25`, on an engine of run 15 or later. `settings.get` and `settings.set` take two more board sections in a `pcb` frame, KiCad's interactive router settings and its layer pairs with no KiCad window.

`router` reads KiCad's own router keys: `mode` (0 highlight collisions, 1 shove, 2 walk around; 2 on a fresh engine), `effort` (0 low, 1 medium, 2 full) and the switches `remove_loops`, `smart_pads`, `shove_vias`, `suggest_finish`, `follow_mouse`, `free_angle_mode`, `snap_to_tracks`, `snap_to_pads`, `optimize_dragged_track`, `auto_posture`, `fix_all_segments`; and the current sizes, in mm: `currentTrack { index, custom, width, customWidth }`, `currentVia { index, custom, diameter, drill, customDiameter, customDrill }`, `currentDiffPair { index, custom, width, gap, viaGap, customWidth, customGap, customViaGap }`. `index` points into the board's own list of sizes (0 is the net class value); `width`, `diameter`, `drill`, `gap` and `viaGap` are the sizes in use now and are read only; `custom` of the track and the via is one switch in KiCad, so a set of either reads back in both. A set's `value` names any of `mode`, `effort` (an integer 0 to 2), the switches (booleans) and the current objects, each with at least one of `index` (an integer from 0), `custom` (a boolean) and its own `custom*` sizes (0.001 to 100 mm); anything else, a read only size included, is `bad_args` before the engine is asked, and the engine answers `bad_args` for an `index` past the board's list. The engine applies an object's `index` first, then its sizes, then `custom`. The router's settings are the editor's, not the design's: a set makes no undo step and marks nothing modified, the live router takes it at once (a route in progress takes a new current size), and an engine boots with KiCad's defaults, so a host that keeps its reader's choices sends them again after each `ev.ready`.

`layerPairs` reads `{ pairs: [{ top, bottom, enabled, name }], current: { top, bottom } }`, layers by their file names (`F.Cu`, `In1.Cu`, `B.Cu`), `name` `""` for none. A set's `value` names `pairs`, `current` or both: `pairs` replaces the saved list whole (at most 256 rows, each `top` and `bottom`, an optional `enabled` defaulting to true and an optional `name` of at most 128 characters; KiCad drops a repeated pair), `current` sets the pair the router's layer switch uses. Each pair is two different copper layers by file name, else `bad_args` before the engine is asked; the engine answers `bad_args` for a layer the board does not enable. A set makes no undo step; KiCad keeps the saved pairs with the project.

A host gates each section on `ev.ready` `caps`: `settings:router` and `settings:layerPairs`, each listed in a `pcb` frame exactly when the engine's `kicadSettingsSections()` names the section in its board half (never on engine run 14). Both are board settings: a `sch` frame answers them `unsupported`.

Three of KiCad's router windows are held in a `pcb` frame while a document is open, each only while `ev.ready` listed the cap beside it: Interactive Router Settings (`pcbnew.InteractiveRouter.SettingsDialog`, its key `Ctrl+<`) and Differential Pair Dimensions (`pcbnew.InteractiveRouter.DiffPairDialog`) under `settings:router`, Set Layer Pair (`pcbnew.InteractiveRouter.SelectLayerPair`) under `settings:layerPairs`. The frame sends `ev.held { action }` and opens nothing (from a key, and on an engine whose menus ask the page, `heldMenus`, from KiCad's menus and toolbars); `action.run` of one answers `held`. The host opens its own routing settings instead.

### board.session
Reads a router's Specctra session (`.ses`) into the open board with KiCad's own session reader, in a `pcb` frame with a board open (island `v0.2.3-cc25`, on an engine with `kicadImportSession` and `kicadCollabBusy`, engine run 15; a host gates it on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `session`, listed by `pcb` frames only). The args are exactly `{ name, bytes, preview }`: the file's name (ending `.ses` in any case, 1 to 255 bytes of UTF-8, no slash, backslash or control character), its bytes (a `Uint8Array` of 1 byte to 25 MiB) and a boolean. The checks, in order, before any effect: the args (`bad_args`); the frame (`unsupported` "a router session is read in a pcb frame"); the engine (`not_ready`, `unsupported` naming the first missing export); a board open (`not_ready`); a load parked, a popup or a dialog up, or an item hanging off the pointer (`busy`). During a `board.check`, a `sheet.check` or an import it answers `busy`.

The frame writes the bytes under a fresh `/tmp/cc-stage/<n>/` (never the project folder: the session does not join the design through the frame; the host keeps the reader's file under `imports/` itself), queues the run with a fresh `/tmp/cc-session/<n>/` for its records, waits for the apply queue to empty and the run's record, and removes both folders whatever the answer.

- `preview: true` loads the session and counts it against the board with nothing changed: `tracks` (each track segment and arc the session routes), `vias` (each via it places), `nets` (the nets it names) and `missingNets` (the names the board lacks, at most 256), `undoCleared` false, `depth` the undo depth as it stands.
- `preview: false` reads it as KiCad's Import Specctra Session does (the unlocked tracks and vias and every marker removed, each placed footprint moved as the session places it, the routes added, the ratsnest rebuilt) as ONE commit "Import Specctra Session": one undo step, and an `ev.edited` once the depth moved. The answer adds `removed` and `added` (`{ tracks, vias }`, locked copper counted in neither) and `depth` the undo depth after; `undoCleared` is true only on an engine that clears the undo history as KiCad's own menu path does (engine run 15 never does).

`session_invalid` when KiCad cannot read the session (the board as it was); `not_applied` when the engine refused the run or no record came within about 60 s; `island_error` for a record of another shape. KiCad's own File > Import > Specctra Session (`pcbnew.EditorControl.importSpecctraSession`) is held in a `pcb` frame under the cap `session`: it sends `ev.held { action }` and opens no window. The fixtures `tests/fixtures/st-session-card/business-card.ses` (5 tracks, 1 via, 3 nets, missing `/NFC_ANT`) and `corrupt.ses` (truncated) on the business card board (`tests/e2e/st/st-ses-i.spec.ts`, engine run 15).

### Engine build T17's reads: library tables, action states, the tool KiCad holds, exact clarify (unit t17-i)

The island of engine build T17 (engine run 15, train 17), in every frame. Each is feature detected on the engine export it needs, so an island on an older engine (T16b and before) lists none of these caps and answers both ops `unsupported` naming the export.

- **`libs.problems`** (no args; the cap `libTableProblems`, the engine's `kicadLibraryTableProblems`): the library tables KiCad could not read at its last pass over them, each `{ path, kind, message }`: `path` the table file's path in the frame's file system (a project table under the project folder, or a global table), `kind` `symbol`, `footprint` or `designBlock`, `message` KiCad's own sentence (the parser's, for example "Syntax error at line 5, column 131"). `[]` when none. A table that loads at a later pass leaves the list. KiCad shows no warning window for such a table on this engine (it did at engine start before T17, and the frame was busy until it was answered). A read: answered during a check, with or without a document. `bad_args` for any args, `not_ready` before the engine is up, `island_error` for an answer of another shape.
- **`actions.state { actions }`** (the cap `actionStates`, the engine's `kicadActionStates`): whether each named KiCad action reads checked now, as KiCad's own menus and toolbars mark it (its registered check condition on the frame's selection), in the top frame. `states` holds one key per distinct name asked: `true` or `false`, or `null` for a name that is no action of this frame, an action that is no toggle (no check condition registered, or KiCad's default never-checked one: reading false there would be a mark that lies), or a check that threw. The frame first waits for the engine's apply queue (1 s at most), so an `action.run` sent just before has run: `busy` when the queue stays busy, or a load is parked. `bad_args` for args of another shape (exactly `{ actions }`, 1 to 256 names, each KiCad's dotted action name), `not_ready` before the engine is up or when the engine has no frame up or an open in flight, `island_error` for an answer of another shape. A read: answered during a check.
- **`ev.tool`** (the cap `activeTool`, the engine's `kicadHostToolChanges` and `kicadActiveTool`): above. The frame takes the engine's slot (`window.kicadHostTool.onChange`) before the engine boots and reads `kicadActiveTool()` right after `ev.ready`, so the first `ev.tool` names the tool held at `ev.ready`.
- **`menuClarify`** (a cap only, listed when the engine has `kicadHostToolChanges`, a name build T17 brought with the clarify fix): this engine marks `ev.contextMenu` `clarify` true ONLY for KiCad's clarify-selection list; every other menu (a right click on one item, the bus unfold menu, a drawing tool's menu) reads `clarify` false. On an older engine every parked menu read `clarify` true, so a host tells the list apart by its items there and drops that guard only under `menuClarify`.

### theme.set

The running frame repaints in the canvas colors the host sends, live: no reload of the document, the view kept, KiCad's chrome still hidden (island after `v0.2.3-cc26`, on any engine with `kicadSetColorTheme`; a host gates it on `ev.ready` `caps` listing `theme`, which a `sch` and a `pcb` frame list on such an engine). `colors` is a whole KiCad color theme, the shape of the island's `theme/colors/circuitcenter.json` (`meta`, `schematic`, `board`, `3d_viewer`, `gerbview`): a key it leaves out takes KiCad's default for that key. `null` brings back the island's own palette (`circuitcenter`); `null` while the palette shows changes nothing and answers `{ ok: true }`.

The island writes the theme as a file of its own in the engine's colors folder, under a fresh name for every set, and switches to it through `kicadSetColorTheme`, the path of KiCad's own Preferences dialog (the frame's color theme setting, then its settings changed, which reloads the colors into the painter and recaches the view). The answer, `{ ok: true }`, comes once the engine's apply queue is empty and a frame has painted.

Every set that applied is kept on the island's own origin (localStorage, key `cc.island.theme`, partitioned by the page's site, so every island frame of one page reads it), and `null` forgets it, also when nothing was applied. Every frame of every kind boots in the kept colors in place of the palette (the drawing sheet editor with its sheet color laid over them, as over the palette), with no flash; a kept value that does not read as a theme is removed and the palette drawn. A host that sends its current theme at every `ev.ready`, `null` included, so corrects stale kept colors.

Only the schematic and board editors take the switch on engine build T18b: in a `symedit`, `fpedit` or `ds` frame the op answers `theme_frame` and no cap is listed (the engine's switch reaches only the top window as KiCad's schematic or board editor). A theme change while one of those editors is open reaches it the next time it opens.

A burst is coalesced: the op is answered off the request queue, a set that arrives while another waits replaces it, and the island runs one switch at a time, always the newest set waiting; every set is answered with the outcome of the switch that carried it. A set waits for the requests on the queue that came before it.

The checks, in order: args other than `{ colors }` with `colors` null or a plain object of at least one section, every section a plain object of strings, finite numbers, booleans and nested objects (at most 4 deep, no arrays or null, at most 256 KiB of JSON) answer `bad_args`; `not_ready` before `ev.ready`; `unsupported` naming `kicadSetColorTheme` or `kicadCollabBusy` on an engine without it; `theme_frame` in a frame the switch does not reach. Then, at its turn: `busy` when a request on the queue stays in flight past 15 s, while a load is parked, an import or a check runs, or a popup menu or a dialog is up; `not_applied` when the engine's apply queue does not empty within 15 s; `island_error` when the theme file cannot be written or the switch throws.

The engine's switch sets KiCad's chrome to its dark appearance for any theme (pcbjam `pcbjam_theme.h`: dark unless the theme is `_builtin_default`), so by day, after a set, KiCad's own panes draw dark once `chrome.show { on: true }` brings them back. The canvas is not affected, and a boot in the kept colors calls no switch.

<!-- ext sections end -->

### Error codes
`message` is a short explanation for logs, never for display. A host should accept any `code` string; these are the ones the frame answers today.

| code | when |
|---|---|
| `unknown_op` | `op` is not one of the request ops above; `message` is the op. During wave 2's build only, an op the wave's manifest names whose unit has not landed answers `unsupported` with the message `seam placeholder: <op> waits for unit <unit>` instead (`bridge/ext/pending.json`); a released island has none (the join test fails while any is left), so a host never meets it. |
